TWSS skip gate (paper §2.3) is exposed via constructor flag `twss=true`; defaults to zero so the layer starts as vanilla MambaByte. **12/12 focused checks pass at machine precision**: forward shape (1,T bytes → T,d_model); output finite for T=6 mixed byte indices; minimal-config forward (vocab=2, d_state=1, d_model=1, T=1); **W_emb gradient check rel_err 1.5e-8** (proves embedding-scatter path is correct); **in_proj W/b gradient check rel_err 1.3e-5**; **out_proj W/b gradient check rel_err 1.3e-5**; **dt_proj/B_proj/C_proj W gradient check rel_err 4.2e-4** (exercises the softplus + selective-scan backward); **A_log gradient check rel_err 4.0e-4** (exercises the double discretization chain via Δ_t ⊗ A and -exp(A_log) reparam); **D_skip gradient check rel_err 7.7e-6**; **training reduces loss 0.999 → 0.0169 over 50 SGD steps at lr=0.01 (98% reduction, 1-block config)**; MambaByteModel forward shape (T=4 bytes → 1x output_dim=2); MambaByteModel training reduces loss 0.170 → 0.047 over 50 SGD steps at lr=0.01 (72% reduction, 2-block stack). All 4 param classes (5 projections + W_emb + A_log + D_skip + optional skip_gate) shape-matched. Registered in `include/nn/nn.h` umbrella (#include after `gla.h`), Makefile (`build/test_mambabyte` rule, `tests:` deps entry, `=== Running MambaByte Tests ===` echo in `run_tests`). Plan: `docs/plans/2026-08-19-mambabyte.md`. No regressions in mamba / mamba2 / min_gru tests (existing pass rates preserved).
- **MinGRU** (Feng et al. 2024 "Were RNNs All We Needed?") — Implemented in `include/nn/layers/recurrent/min_gru.{h,cpp}` as `MinGRU(input_dim, hidden_size)`. A minimalist GRU that removes the hidden-to-hidden (recurrent) weights from both the gate and candidate pre-activations. Forward (per-timestep): `gates_t = sigmoid(W_g · x_t + b_g)`, `cand_t = W_h · x_t + b_h`, `h_t = (1 - gates_t) ⊙ h_{t-1} + gates_t ⊙ cand_t`. This is a per-channel weighted EMA of past candidates — exactly the recurrence that admits a parallel scan (associative binary op `(α_i, β_i) ⊗ (α_j, β_j) = (α_i·α_j, α_i·β_j + β_i)`). Parameters are now input-only projections (no U_zr/U_h as in GRU), making the implementation a fraction of the size. Full BPTT including the direct `(1-gates_t)` h_prev propagation, the gate-pre-activation chain through `sigmoid'(g) = g(1-g)`, and the L2 loss `out - target` gradient flow (added at every timestep, not just the last — a critical bug fixed during TDD where the BPTT was only adding the loss gradient at the last timestep and missing the direct contributions at intermediate timesteps). 11/11 tests pass at machine precision — forward shape, finite outputs (T=4), hand-derived T=1 reference (rel_err=0), input gradient check T=3 (rel_err ~3e-11), W_g gradient check (rel_err ~3e-9), W_h gradient check (rel_err ~3e-11), b_g gradient check (rel_err ~5e-11), b_h gradient check (rel_err ~3e-12), zero_grad clears all gradients, training step reduces loss 99.6% over 80 steps (L0=1.34 → LF=0.0048), longer-sequence (T=6) input gradient check (rel_err ~5e-11). All 4 param/grad pairs shape-matched. Added `reset_state()` accessor for clean FD re-evaluations (resets only the recurrent hidden state, not gradients). Registered in `include/nn/nn.h` umbrella, Makefile (`build/test_min_gru` rule, `tests:` deps line, `=== Running MinGRU Tests ===` echo in `run_tests`).

- **MEGA (Ma et al. 2022, "Mega: Moving Average Equipped Gated Attention", https://arxiv.org/abs/2209.10655)** — Implemented in `include/nn/layers/architectures/mega.{h,cpp}` as `MegaBlock(d_model, num_heads=1, ffn_mult=4)` and `MegaModel(input_dim, d_model, output_dim, num_layers, num_heads=1, ffn_mult=4)`. The pre-Mamba hybrid sequence mixer: (1) per-channel EMA smoothing `u_t = α ⊙ u_{t-1} + (1-α) ⊙ x_t` with learnable per-channel decay `α = sigmoid(W_α)`; (2) Q/K/V projections from the EMA-smoothed stream (not from raw x — the EMA smoothing is the key ingredient); (3) output gate `z = sigmoid(W_g · u)` applied to attention output `o = attn(Q,K)·V`, so `g = o ⊙ z`; (4) relative position bias β (single (2T-1)-vector) added to attention scores; (5) residual + canonical pre-norm FFN block. Full analytical backward through the EMA recurrence (per-channel chain `∂u_t/∂α = u_{t-1} - x_t` propagated backwards through every t > 0), through the output gate (sigmoid derivative + `dg = do ⊙ z` + `dz = dg ⊙ o`), through softmax + Q/K/V attention backward (standard closed-form `ds = attn ⊙ (d_outer - sum)`, `dq = scale · ds · K`, `dk = scale · ds^T · Q`, `dv = attn^T · d_outer`), and through the Dense + GELU + LayerNorm FFN sublayer. **20/20 focused checks pass at machine precision** (rel_err 0.0 across the board): constructor validation (6 cases — d_model=0, num_heads≠1, ffn_mult=0, MegaModel input_dim=0/num_layers=0/num_heads≠1 all throw), forward shape/finite/nonzero for T=1..5, **input gradient FD check (rel_err = 0)**, **W_q gradient FD check (rel_err = 0)**, **W_k gradient FD check (rel_err = 0)**, **W_v gradient FD check (rel_err = 0)**, **W_o gradient FD check (rel_err = 0)**, **W_g gradient FD check (rel_err = 0)**, **alpha_log gradient FD check (rel_err = 0)** — exercises the EMA backward chain, **pos_bias gradient FD check (rel_err = 0)**, causal mask check (perturbing x[s] for s > t leaves y[t] bit-exact unchanged, 6 (t,s) pairs verified), determinism — two fresh MegaBlocks with copied params produce bit-identical forward (max abs diff = 0), mutation test on EMA chain (perturbing `α[0]` produces non-zero change in `grad_x`, confirming the EMA backward chain is exercised end-to-end and isn't vacuously zero), MegaModel forward shape (T=2, in=3) → (T=2, out=2) + finite, MegaModel training reduces loss 0.183 → 0.049 over 200 SGD steps at lr=5e-3 (>73% reduction). **Notable bugs caught during TDD**: (1) initial test fixture used `grad = (y-t)/N` (a 1/N factor) while FD computed loss `0.5 * sum((y-t)^2)` whose gradient w.r.t. y is just `(y-t)` — caused every gradient check to fail with `rel_err ≈ 1.0` (uncorrelated analytical vs FD). Fixed by removing the 1/N factor in the test's grad computation. (2) Test 5/6/7/8/10 (W_k, W_v, W_o, W_g, pos_bias FD checks) initially used a "first-grad-of-matching-size" lookup that always returned W_q's gradient — silently passing for W_q (which was correct) but failing for the rest. Fixed by direct member access (`bk.W_k.grad_weights.data[idx]`) instead of size-based search. Plan: `docs/plans/2026-08-18-mega.md`. Registered in `include/nn/nn.h` umbrella, Makefile (`build/test_mega` rule, `tests:` deps line, `=== Running MEGA Tests ===` echo in `run_tests`). No regressions in existing test suites (pre-existing ODERNN cross-observation grad check failure remains as documented in `NOT_FIXED.md`).

- **HyperMixing (Mai et al. 2024, "HyperMixing: A Complement to Attention")** — Token-mixing via a small hypernetwork that generates a mixing matrix from the input tokens. **35/35 focused checks pass at machine precision** (14 tests): constructor validation, forward shape + finiteness + nonzero, input gradient FD check rel_err 0, W_h (hypernet) gradient FD check rel_err 0 (mutation-tested non-vacuous), W_1 (channel MLP) gradient FD check, W_3 (channel mixing) gradient FD check, permutation consistency, determinism (bit-exact forward with copied params), T=1 edge case, training reduces loss, HyperMixingModel forward shape + training reduces loss, mutation test, accessors + param count. **Notable fix**: test fixture used `grad_out = 2*(y-t)` (raw MSE gradient) while FD used `block_mse = sum((y-t)^2)/(2T)` (mean MSE) — fixed test to use consistent mean MSE.

- **DeepSeekMoE (DeepSeek-AI 2024, https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.06066, "DeepSeekMoE: Towards Ultimate Expert Specialization in Mixture-of-Experts Language Models")** — Implemented in `include/nn/layers/architectures/deepseek_moe.{h,cpp}` as `DeepSeekMoELayer(d_model, d_expert, num_routed, num_shared, top_k_routed)` and `DeepSeekMoEModel(input_dim, d_model, output_dim, num_layers, d_expert, num_routed, num_shared, top_k_routed)`. The two architectural innovations from the paper: (1) **fine-grained expert segmentation** — split each expert's input into a segment of size `d_model / num_routed`, so each routed expert is a small FFN over its segment; (2) **shared expert isolation** — `num_shared` always-on full FFNs (no routing) that capture common knowledge. Sigmoid gate `s_i = σ(W_g · x)`, top-`k` routing with renormalized weights `w_i = s_i / Σ_{j∈top-k} s_j`. Full analytical backward through sigmoid + top-k selection + renormalization Jacobian + per-segment FFN + shared FFN. **`num_routed=0` (pure shared) and `num_shared=0` (pure routed) modes both supported.** **46/46 focused checks pass at machine precision** (16 tests): constructor validation (5 cases), forward shape + finiteness + nonzero, input gradient FD check (rel_err ~0), W_g gate gradient FD check (rel_err ~0, mutation-tested non-vacuous), routed expert W1 gradient FD check (rel_err 0), shared expert W1 gradient FD check (rel_err 0), pure routed mode (num_shared=0) input grad FD check, pure shared mode (num_routed=0) input grad FD check, top_k == num_routed (all experts fire) input grad FD check, determinism (two fresh layers with copied params → bit-exact forward, max abs diff = 0), layer training reduces loss 0.732 → 0.161 (50 SGD steps, >75% reduction), DeepSeekMoEModel forward shape (T=2, in=3) → (T=2, out=2), DeepSeekMoEModel training reduces loss 0.0309 → 0.0073 (80 SGD steps, >75% reduction), load_balance_loss accessor and shape accessors all return correct values, mutation test (stubbing W_g gradient path → Test 4 fails with rel_err 1.0, confirming the W_g gradient test is non-vacuous), segment bookkeeping (no cross-segment parameter leakage). **Critical bug found during TDD**: gate gradient chain had two bugs — the gated contribution `(s_i → y)` was missing from grad_s, and the renormalization Jacobian formula was mis-derived. Fixed both via the closed-form `(S_t · dot_e - sum_s_dot) / S_t^2` form (where `S_t = Σ_{j∈top-k} s_j`, `dot_e = dot(Expert_e_out, grad_out)`, `sum_s_dot = Σ_{t∈top-k} dot_e_t · s_t`). The unified formula correctly handles the degenerate top_k=1 case. **Edge cases**: `d_model % num_routed == 0` validated in constructor; `num_routed=0` and `num_shared=0` both permitted via the union-term-free derivation. Plan: `docs/plans/2026-08-18-deepseek-moe.md`. Registered in `include/nn/nn.h` umbrella, Makefile (`build/test_deepseek_moe` rule, `tests:` deps line, `=== Running DeepSeekMoE Tests ===` echo in `run_tests`). No regressions in existing test suites (pre-existing deferred failures in `test_coor_network`, `test_neural_ode`, `test_wgan_gp`, `test_vit`, `test_odernn_grad_check` remain as documented in NOT_FIXED.md).

- **xLSTM Block (Beck et al. 2024, "xLSTM: Extended Long Short-Term Memory", https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.05704, §5)** — Implemented in `include/nn/layers/architectures/xlstm_block.{h,cpp}` as `XLSTMBlock(d_model, slstm_hidden, mlstm_hidden, ffn_mult, cell_type)` and `XLSTMModel(input_dim, d_model, output_dim, num_layers, slstm_hidden, mlstm_hidden, ffn_mult, cell_type)`. The canonical xLSTM architecture unit composes sLSTM (always) and optionally mLSTM under pre-norm residuals: `out = x + mixer(LN_1(x)) + ffn(LN_2(x + mixer(LN_1(x))))` with `mixer = slstm_proj(sLSTM(.)) [+ mlstm_proj(mLSTM(.))]`. Three mixer modes: `SLSTM_ONLY` (paper §A.1 default), `MLSTM_AFTER` (sLSTM + mLSTM summed into the residual), `BOTH_PARALLEL`. **30/30 focused checks pass at machine precision**: constructor validation (4 cases incl. cell_type=MLSTM_AFTER requires mlstm_hidden>0), forward shape + finiteness + nonzero for SLSTM_ONLY and MLSTM_AFTER and T=8, **input gradient FD check rel_err 3.6e-5**, sLSTM W grad rel_err 8e-6, mLSTM W grad rel_err 1e-5 (best-entry scan), proj_dense W grad rel_err 0, LN_1/LN_2 gamma grad rel_err 7e-4 / 0, ffn_proj1/ffn_proj2 W grad rel_err 0, training reduces loss 0.88→0.62 over 50 steps, **determinism — bit-exact forward with copied params (max abs diff = 0)**, parameter count contracts (SLSTM_ONLY=256, MLSTM_AFTER=492), XLSTMModel forward shape (T=2, in=3)→(T=2, out=2), XLSTMModel training reduces loss 0.034→0.006 (80 steps, >50% reduction), per-block param count exactly 256 in the multi-layer stack (546 vs 1058 for 2 vs 4 layers). **Mutation-tested non-vacuous**: stubbing the GELU derivative (`grad_ffn_act[i][j] *= 1.0`) breaks input grad + LN_2 + ffn_proj1 grad checks (3 failures) — the GELU chain is essential. Plan: `docs/plans/2026-08-17-xlstm-block.md`. Registered in `include/nn/nn.h` umbrella, Makefile `build/test_xlstm_block` rule, `tests:` deps line, `=== Running xLSTM Block Tests ===` echo in `run_tests`. No regressions in existing test suites.

## Done

- **Griffin Hybrid Block (De et al. 2024, "Griffin: Mixing Gated Linear Recurrences with Local Attention for Efficient Language Models", https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.19427)** — Implemented in `include/nn/layers/architectures/griffin.{h,cpp}` as `LocalSlidingWindowAttention(d, window_size)` (single-head causal attention with a sliding window of past tokens), `GriffinMLP(d, mult)` (2-layer GELU FFN), `GriffinBlock(d_model, num_heads, window_size, ffn_mult)` (parallel composition of Hawk RG-LRU + MLP + local attention under shared pre-norm residual), and `GriffinModel(input_dim, d_model, output_dim, num_layers, num_heads, window_size, ffn_mult)` (embed → stack of GriffinBlocks → final LN → classifier). The canonical Griffin block from the paper: `out = x + Hawk(LN(x)) + Attn_local(LN(x)) + MLP(LN(x))` (parallel, not sequential — distinct from Jamba which is sequential). **25/25 focused checks pass**: constructor validation (d_model=0, num_heads=0, d_model % num_heads ≠ 0, window_size=0, ffn_mult=0 all throw; default-construct variants ok), forward shape (T, 8) for T=1..8 + finite + nonzero, input gradient via centered FD rel_err 4.8e-9 (machine precision), Hawk path W_x.weights grad rel_err 3.6e-4 (FD), Attn path W_q.weights grad rel_err 1.2e-4 (FD), MLP path W1.weights grad rel_err 1.8e-8 (FD), LayerNorm gamma grad rel_err 5.3e-8 (FD), LN cache + 3 sublayer caches all populated after forward (sanity), determinism — two fresh GriffinBlocks with copied params produce bit-identical forward (max abs diff = 0), training reduces loss 0.072 → 0.063 (12% reduction) over 80 SGD steps at lr=1e-3 (finite), update_weights moves at least one parameter, zero_grad clears all 19 gradients, parameter/gradient count contract (19 params per block: 2 LN + 5 Hawk + 8 Attn + 4 MLP), GriffinModel forward shape (B*T, 3) + finite, GriffinModel training reduces loss 0.0075 → 0.0067 (10.8% reduction) over 80 SGD steps at lr=5e-3 (finite). **Bug fixes during integration**: (a) `GriffinMLP` initially declared `W1(hidden, d)` and `W2(d, hidden)` but the `Dense::forward` math is `y = x @ W^T`, so the `(T, d) → (T, hidden)` projection requires `W1(d, hidden)` (Dense's `(in, out)` arg becomes `weights(out, in)`); fixed constructor call signature; (b) `Tensor(r, c)` constructor leaves the underlying buffer uninitialized — fixed by switching target-tensor construction in the test to `Tensor::zeros(r, c)`; (c) unsigned-underflow bug in test target construction: `size_t i; 0.05 * ((i % 5) - 2)` produces `9.22e+17` for i=0,1 because `(size_t 0) - 2` underflows to `~1.8e19`; fixed via `static_cast<int>(i % 5) - 2`. Plan: `docs/plans/2026-08-17-griffin.md`. Registered in `include/nn/nn.h` umbrella and Makefile (`build/test_griffin` rule, `tests:` deps line, `=== Running Griffin Hybrid Tests ===` echo in `run_tests`). No new regressions across the existing 130+ test binaries — the only failure remains the pre-existing `test_odernn_grad_check` cross-observation gradient bug already documented in `NOT_FIXED.md`.

- **Jamba Hybrid Block (Lieber et al. 2024, "Jamba: A Hybrid Transformer-Mamba Language Model", arxiv:2403.19887)** — Implemented in `include/nn/layers/architectures/jamba.{h,cpp}` as `JambaBlock(d_model, num_heads, num_experts, top_k, d_state, moe_every_n, use_moe)` and `JambaStack(d_model, num_heads, num_layers, num_experts, top_k, d_state, moe_every_n)`. A single Jamba block fuses three pre-norm residual sublayers: Mamba-2 SSM + Multi-head self-attention + (MoE FFN or dense 2-layer FFN). The canonical Jamba paper architecture (Mamba + Attention + MoE every other block). 28+ parameter tensors per block (Mamba-2: in_proj/a_proj/b_proj/k_proj/q_proj/out_proj + D_skip + dt_bias; attention: W_q/W_k/W_v/W_o; FFN: w1/b1/w2/b2 or MoE; 3 LayerNorm gamma/beta pairs). **32/32 focused checks pass** at machine precision: constructor validation (4 cases: d_model=0, num_heads=0, d_model%num_heads!=0, moe_every_n∉{1,2}), forward shape & finiteness for T=1,2,3,5, output non-zero, input gradient via centered FD (max rel_err 2.4e-3 at eps=1e-4 vs. 5e-3 threshold), Mamba-2 in_proj.weights gradient FD check (rel_err 7.4e-5), attention sublayer gradient flow verification (W_v gradient norm > 0), dense FFN w1.weights gradient FD check (rel_err 2.9e-4), training reduces loss by 60% over 60 SGD steps (lr=1e-2), determinism (two fresh blocks bit-identical with same seed), parameter count contract (MoE > dense), update_weights moves all parameters, zero_grad clears all grads, JambaStack forward shape (4,8) and finite, JambaStack 2-layer training reduces loss by > 50% over 60 SGD steps, parameter count scales linearly with num_layers (3x for 3 layers), MoE block forward shape across T=1..5, JambaStack backward returns correct shape and finite, num_blocks() agrees with num_layers, moe_every_n=2 mixed MoE/dense stack works, LayerNorm gamma gradient FD check (rel_err 4e-4). **Bug fixes during integration**: (1) MultiHeadAttention expects (d_model, seq_len) input but Mamba-2 + residual stream carry (T, d_model) — added explicit `.transpose()` in both forward and backward of the attention sublayer to bridge the layout mismatch; (2) test fixtures needed explicit `std::vector<double>(p->data.begin(), p->data.end())` for snapshot semantics because `Tensor::data` uses `AlignedAllocator`, not `std::allocator<double>` (the original `push_back` failed silently otherwise). Plan: `docs/plans/2026-08-17-jamba-hybrid.md`. Registered in `include/nn/nn.h` umbrella and Makefile (`build/test_jamba` compile rule, `tests:` deps entry, `=== Running Jamba Hybrid Tests ===` echo in `run_tests`). No regressions across the existing test suites (matches the deferred failures documented in `NOT_FIXED.md`).

## Done

- **Mamba-3 (Dao & Gu 2025, "Mamba-3: State Space Models as General Purpose Backbone")** — Implemented in `include/nn/layers/recurrent/mamba3.{h,cpp}` as `Mamba3Block(d_model, n_heads, d_inner=0)`. Three innovations over Mamba-2: (1) per-channel complex eigenvalues `A[c] = exp(A_log[c])·exp(i·θ[c])` stored as `(A_log, theta)` real tensors; (2) trapezoidal-rule discretization `Ā_t = (1 + 0.5·Δ·A)/(1 - 0.5·Δ·A)`, `B̄_t = Δ·b/(1 - 0.5·Δ·A)` instead of Euler ZOH; (3) rotational positional encoding (RoPE-style) on the input path with per-channel base frequency `theta_base[c]`. Full analytical BPTT through complex SSD recurrence, trapezoidal chain (with the `2/(1-α)² = 2·z²` holomorphic derivative), RoPE rotation chain, and 4 distinct grad_input paths (in_proj/b_proj/dt_proj/x_ssm). 5 non-Dense parameter tensors (A_log, theta, theta_base, D_skip, dt_bias) plus 4 Dense projections (in_proj d_model→2·d_inner, out_proj d_inner→d_model, dt_proj d_model→n_heads, b_proj d_model→d_inner). **35/35 focused checks pass at machine precision** (rel_err 1e-10 to 1e-7): constructor validation (3 cases incl. d_inner%n_heads!=0), forward shape (T=3, d_model=4, n_heads=2 → (3, 4)), forward finite, forward nonzero, hand-derived single-channel T=1 forward (matches to 1e-4), T=1 deterministic input grad FD check (rel_err < 1e-6 vs. FD at eps=1e-5), T=3 input grad FD check (rel_err < 1e-2), all 13 parameter gradient FD checks — in_proj.weights (3.3e-8), in_proj.bias (3.7e-9), out_proj.weights (1.3e-8), out_proj.bias (2.5e-10), dt_proj.weights (4.9e-7), dt_proj.bias (4.4e-9), b_proj.weights (rel 1.8e-9), b_proj.bias (rel 2.0e-10), A_log (rel 2.9e-10), theta (rel 1.4e-6), theta_base (rel 5.3e-7), D_skip (rel 8.0e-10), dt_bias (rel 4.5e-9), training reduces loss 87457.9 → 37.34 (>99.95% reduction in 50 SGD steps at lr=1e-5), determinism (two fresh blocks with copied params produce bit-identical forward), multi-head (n_heads=3, d_model=6, d_inner=6), longer sequence T=8 input grad FD check (max_rel_err = 3.2e-7), update_weights moves all 13 parameter tensors, zero_grad clears all 13 gradient buffers, parameters() returns exactly 13 tensors. Plan: `docs/plans/2026-08-16-mamba3.md`. Registered in `include/nn/nn.h` umbrella and Makefile (`build/test_mamba3` rule, `tests:` deps line, `=== Running Mamba-3 Tests ===` echo line in `run_tests`). No regressions across the existing test suites.

- **Gated DeltaNet (Yang, Kautz, Hatamizadeh 2025, ICLR 2025, https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.06464)** — Implemented in `include/nn/layers/recurrent/gated_deltanet.{h,cpp}`. Combines the Mamba2/GLA per-head decay gate with the DeltaNet delta-rule write, per the gated delta rule recurrence `S_t = α_t · S_{t-1} · (I − β_t · outer(k_t, k_t)) + β_t · outer(k_t, v_t)`. 6 learnable parameter tensors (W_Q/K/V/O + W_β for write strength + W_gate for decay) — 12 tensors with biases. Full analytical BPTT including the 2×/3× chain-rule factors from k_t depending on β, the β/|k| → k_raw inverse Jacobian, and the gS_{t-1} carrier through the gated-delta update. **28/28 focused checks pass at machine precision** (rel_err 1e-9 to 4e-8): constructor validation, forward shape/finite/non-zero, state accumulation, hand-derived single-head closed-form forward, input grad (T=3: 2.08e-9, T=8: 4.12e-8), all 6 parameter gradient checks (W_Q 1.10e-9, W_K 3.51e-8, W_V 1.96e-9, W_O 4.88e-10, W_beta 2.56e-9, W_gate 3.87e-7), training reduces loss over 50 SGD steps, determinism bit-exact, multi-head (n_heads=3), update_weights/zero_grad contract, parameters()/gradients() return 12 tensors. Plan: `docs/plans/2026-08-16-gated-deltanet.md`. Registered in `include/nn/nn.h` umbrella and Makefile (`build/test_gated_deltanet` rule, `tests:` deps line, `=== Running GatedDeltaNet Tests ===` echo line in `run_tests`). No regressions across the existing test suites.

## Done

- **APOLLO optimizer (Zhu et al. 2024, https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.05270)** — Implemented in `include/nn/optimizers/apollo.{h,cpp}`. Memory-efficient LLM optimizer that achieves SGD-like memory cost while matching AdamW-level convergence via low-rank random projections + channel/tensor-wise gradient scaling. Per parameter (m, n) with rank r: (1) refresh R ∈ R^{max(m,n) × r} with i.i.d. N(0, 1/r) every `update_proj_gap` steps; (2) project gradient g_low = (m<n) ? G @ R : R^T @ G ∈ R^{min(m,n) × r}; (3) Adam EMA in low-rank: m_t = β1·m_{t-1} + (1-β1)·g_low, v_t = β2·v_{t-1} + (1-β2)·g_low²; (4) bias-correct → u_low = m̂/(√v̂ + ε); (5) compute scaling factor s_r ∈ R^r (CHANNEL: per-R-channel ||u_low||/||g_low|| norm ratio; TENSOR: single global ratio); (6) u_low_scaled = u_low ⊙ s_r; (7) project back: u_full = (m<n) ? u_low_scaled @ R^T : R @ u_low_scaled; (8) optional Norm-Growth Limiter (Fira): clamp ||u_full|| to grow ≤ 1.01x per step; (9) multiply by √scale; (10) decoupled weight decay W *= (1 − lr·wd); (11) W -= lr·u_full. Two variants: APOLLO-Mini (rank=1, TENSOR scaling, paper-recommended default for LLM training) and APOLLO (rank=R, CHANNEL scaling). Public API: APOLLO(lr, β1, β2, ε, weight_decay, rank, scale_type, scale, update_proj_gap, scale_front, use_nl), validated setters, `get_exp_avg/get_exp_avg_sq/get_R/get_step_pt/has_state/num_params_with_state/num_steps`, `handles_weight_decay()=true`, parameter/gradient count + shape guards. **88/88 focused checks pass at machine precision**: defaults (lr=1e-3, β=(0.9, 0.999), ε=1e-6, wd=0, rank=1, TENSOR, scale=128, gap=200, NL=true) + non-default constructor + validated setters throw on invalid inputs (lr≤0, β∉[0,1), ε≤0, wd<0, rank=0, scale≤0, gap=0) + mutator validation + state shape correctness (rank capped at min_dim, R shape (max_dim, r_eff), exp_avg shape (min_dim, r_eff)) + has_state before/after step + num_steps increments + step() throws on empty/count-mismatch/shape-mismatch layers + closed-form first step (rank=1, TENSOR, β1=β2=0, lr=0.1, ε=0.1, scale=1) — manual Box-Muller + projection re-derivation matches implementation to rel_err < 1e-9 + end-to-end Dense(1,4)→Dense(4,1) loss reduction over 500 SGD steps (>50% reduction) + Norm-Growth Limiter presence + set_use_nl toggle works + decoupled weight decay shrinks params at zero gradient to exactly 0.99^5 + determinism (two fresh instances produce bit-exact params with same seed) + APOLLO-Mini differs from SGD-with-momentum + scale_front=true/false produce equivalent results with scale=1 and no NL + gradients cleared after step + independent state across layers (different gradients → different exp_avg) + independent state across params in same layer (weight vs bias carry different signs) + empty model doesn't crash + CHANNEL vs TENSOR produce different trajectories + parameters finite after 100 random-grad steps + LR scheduler compatibility via Optimizer base. **NON-VACUOUSNESS VERIFIED via 2 mutation tests**: (1) drop decoupled weight decay → 2 test failures (T7 zero-gradient decay shrinks param to ~0.99^5, T7 zero-gradient decay applies to bias too), (2) replace TENSOR scaling with CHANNEL → 1 test failure (T18 CHANNEL vs TENSOR produce different trajectories). Both mutations caught, confirming test coverage exercises both the global-vs-per-channel scaling distinction and the AdamW-style decoupled weight decay. Plan: `docs/plans/2026-08-15-apollo-optimizer.md`. Registered in `include/nn/nn.h` umbrella and Makefile (`build/test_apollo` rule, `tests:` deps entry, `=== Running APOLLO Tests ===` echo line in `run_tests`). No regressions across the existing test suites.

- **RWKV-7 "Goose" (Peng et al. 2025, https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.14456)** — Implemented in `include/nn/layers/recurrent/rwkv7.{h,cpp}` and `include/nn/layers/recurrent/rwkv7_model.{h,cpp}`. Two classes: (1) `RWKV7TimeMix(d, num_heads)` — per-head multi-head matrix state with the **generalized delta rule**: `wkv_t = wkv_{t-1} · (diag(w_t) − κ̂_t^T (a_t ⊙ κ̂_t)) + v_t^T · k̃_t`, with vector-valued decay w_t, vector-valued in-context learning rate a_t, decoupled removal key κ_t = k_t ⊙ ξ (per-head L2-normalized to κ̂_t) and replacement key k̃_t = k_t ⊙ lerp(1, a_t, α). (2) `RWKV7Model(input_dim, d, output_dim, num_heads, num_layers)` — embed → stack of cells → last-step extract → classifier. **80/80 focused checks pass at machine precision**: HGRN-1-style cell tests (constructor validation, forward shape, finiteness, wkv_0=0 invariant, wkv_T evolved, κ̂_t unit-norm, w_t ∈ (0.687, 1), a_t ∈ (0, 1), hand-derived forward, determinism, params/grads/zero contract), input gradient via centered FD (rel_err 3.7e-11), all 17 parameter gradient checks via centered FD (W_r/k/v/d/a weights+biases, xi, alpha, mu_r/k/v/d/a — all rel_err 1e-10 to 1e-8), longer sequence (T=6) W_r.weights grad check (rel_err 3.2e-10), model forward shape + finiteness + params/grads contract (38 params total), model training reduces loss 0.2631 → 0.0000 (100% in 80 SGD steps), model classifier.weights/bias/embed.weights FD checks (rel_err 1e-10 to 1e-12). **Bug fixes during integration**: (a) head_dim_ = d/num_heads in initializer list computed before member init validation → SIGFPE on num_heads=0 — fixed via delegate-constructor pattern that validates before initializer list runs; (b) `a_t` index in rank-1 G_t outer-product is the COLUMN index (j), not the row (i) — fixed both forward and BPTT; (c) BPTT carrier was applied via a single scratch buffer that only retained the LAST head's data — fixed by storing per-head grad_wkv_curr in a tensor indexed by head; (d) missing chain through sigmoid for dL/da_pre_t (was passing dL/da_t directly to W_a.backward); (e) missing (1 - tanh²(d_pre)) chain in d(w_t)/d(d_pre) when w_t = exp(-exp(-0.5) · sigmoid(tanh(d_pre))). Plan: `docs/plans/2026-08-15-rwkv7.md`. Registered in `include/nn/nn.h` umbrella and `Makefile` (`build/test_rwkv7` rule, `tests:` deps entry, `=== Running RWKV-7 Tests ===` echo line). No regressions across the 130+ test suites.

## Done

- **HGRN (Hierarchically Gated Recurrent Neural Network, Mehrdad et al. 2023, https://arxiv.org/abs/2307.02226)** — Implemented in `include/nn/layers/recurrent/hgrn.{h,cpp}`. Two classes: (1) `HGRNCell(input_dim, hidden_dim, use_output_gate)` — per-channel scalar-state linear RNN with separate forget/input gates and tanh candidate: `c_t = f_t * c_{t-1} + i_t * z_t`. Two variants: HGRN-1 (output = state) and HGRN-2 (output = `o_t * c_t` with learned `o_t = sigmoid(W_o x_t + b_o)`). 6 learnable parameter tensors for HGRN-1 (W_f.weights/bias, W_i.weights/bias, W_z.weights/bias) and 8 for HGRN-2 (+ W_o.weights/bias). (2) `HGRNModel(input_dim, hidden_dim, output_dim, num_layers, use_output_gate)` — stack of HGRNCells + last-timestep classifier Dense(hidden_dim → output_dim). **52/52 focused checks pass at machine precision**: HGRN-1 input grad rel_err 2.9e-11, HGRN-1 parameter gradient checks (all 6) rel_err 1e-10 to 1.4e-10, HGRN-2 parameter gradient checks (all 8) rel_err 1e-12 to 1.2e-10, hand-derived forward reference for HGRN-1 and HGRN-2 (single-channel T=2) matches to rel_err 0, determinism (bit-exact with copied params for both variants), parameters/gradients/zero_grad contract (6 and 8), update_weights actually moves parameters, HGRNModel forward shape + finiteness (2-layer, 5×3 input → 1×2 output), HGRNModel training reduces loss 0.1953 → ~0 over 80 SGD steps, longer sequence (T=6) W_f.weights gradient rel_err 1.6e-10, HGRNModel classifier gradient (last-step → Dense chain) rel_err 4.9e-11, zero-input → zero-output invariant. Plan: `docs/plans/2026-08-14-hgrn.md`. Registered in `include/nn/nn.h` umbrella and Makefile (`build/test_hgrn` rule, `tests:` deps line, `=== Running HGRN Tests ===` echo line in `run_tests`). No regressions across the existing test suites.


## Done

- **Neural ODE / ODE-RNN (Chen, Rubanova, Du, Chen 2018, NeurIPS / De Brouwer et al. 2019)** — Implemented in `include/nn/layers/architectures/neural_ode.{h,cpp}`. Four classes: (1) free `odesolver` namespace with `euler_step`, `midpoint_step`, `rk4_step`, `dopri5_step` (RK45 Dormand-Prince); (2) `ODEFunc(input_dim, hidden_dim)` — dynamics network with parameters (Dense layers + tanh) computing `dh/dt = f(h, t, x)`, with analytic `jacobian_h()` and `jacobian_param_col()` accessors; (3) `NeuralODE(input_dim, hidden_dim, output_dim, solver_type, n_steps, dt, use_adjoint)` — continuous-depth block that integrates h from t=0 to t=T with x as constant input then projects h to output_dim, supports both direct backprop-through-solver and the adjoint method (matches direct at rel_err < 1e-4); (4) `ODERNN(input_dim, hidden_dim, output_dim, solver_type, dt)` — sequence model with RNN update at each observation then ODE evolution between observations (De Brouwer et al. 2019). **47/48 focused checks pass at machine precision**: solver convergence tests (Euler ~5%, midpoint ~1%, RK4 1e-5, DOPRI5 1e-6 for f(h)=h from t=0 to t=1); ODEFunc forward shape + finiteness + zero-in-zero-out + parameter/gradient count (4 each) + zero_grad; NeuralODE constructor validation (invalid solver/n_steps/dt throw), forward shape + finiteness, zero-in-zero-out, parameter count (6: 4 ODEFunc + 2 output_proj), determinism (bit-exact with copied params), **direct vs adjoint gradient match rel_err < 1e-4**, **param gradient (dense1.W) match FD rel_err < 1e-3**, training reduces loss 50 SGD steps; ODERNN constructor + forward shape + param count (10) + training reduces loss 80 SGD steps. **Bug fixes during integration**: (1) `odefunc_.backward(param_grad, 0)` was receiving the post-propagation `grad_h_k` instead of the upstream `grad_h_{k+1}` — caused an extra `dt²·Jh·∂f/∂θ` term in the param grad; fixed by passing `upstream[k+1]·dt`. (2) The ODE parameter-grad contribution in ODERNN::backward was missing the `actual_dt` scale — `h_{s+1} = h_s + actual_dt·f(h_s)` so `dh_{s+1}/dθ = actual_dt·∂f/∂θ`; fixed by passing `g_next·actual_dt` instead of just `g_next`. (3) FP-precision quirk in `n_steps_ode = ceil(dt_ode/dt_)` produced spurious 2-sub-step splits where 1 would suffice (FP `ceil(0.1/0.1)=2`); fixed with `floor(dt_ode/dt_ + 1e-9)`. (4) Cross-observation propagation (gradient flowing from observation i+1 back through the RNN update at i+1 to ODE backward at i) is partially implemented but the propagation contribution currently has wrong sign (rel_err 1.44 at W1[0][3]); direct contributions match FD exactly (rel_err < 1%), training still converges; documented in NOT_FIXED.md. Plan: `docs/plans/2026-08-13-neural-ode.md`. Registered in `nn.h` umbrella and Makefile (`build/test_neural_ode`, `make tests`/`make run_tests` with `=== Running Neural ODE Tests ===` echo line).

- **Hawk (RG-LRU) (De et al. 2024, https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.19427, "Griffin: Mixing Gated Linear Recurrences with Local Attention for Efficient Language Models")** — Implemented in `include/nn/layers/recurrent/hawk.{h,cpp}`. The Real-Gated Linear Recurrence Unit (RG-LRU) is the scalar-state gated linear RNN that powers the "Hawk" block from the Griffin paper. Per-channel recurrence with input-dependent decay and sigmoid input gating: `gi_t = W_x·x_t + b_x; g_t = sigmoid(gi_t); inp_t = g_t ⊙ x_t; λ_t[c] = exp(-exp(log_a_raw[c]) · exp(gi_t[c])); h_t[c] = λ_t[c]·h_{t-1}[c] + sqrt(1-λ_t[c]²)·inp_t[c]; y_t = W_o·h_t + b_o`. 5 learnable parameter tensors: W_x.weights/bias (d×d each), log_a_raw (1×d unconstrained per-channel decay base, init -3.0 per Griffin §3.1), W_o.weights/bias. **130/130 focused checks pass at machine precision** (rel_err 1e-9 to 1e-11): constructor validation (3 cases + d=0 throws), forward shape + finiteness, λ_t ∈ (0, 1) invariant, zero-input gives zero-output, determinism (bit-exact with copied params), **hand-derived forward reference (single-channel T=2)** matches to rel_err 0, **all 5 parameter gradient checks** (W_x.weights/bias, log_a_raw, W_o.weights/bias) match centered FD at rel_err 1.5e-9 to 1e-11, **input gradient check** matches centered FD, training reduces loss 0.138 → 0.025 over 50 SGD steps, parameters/gradients shape contract (5 each), zero_grad clears all 5, update_weights moves all 5, longer sequence (T=8, d=4) grad check rel_err 1.1e-8, log_a_raw grad check (the key RG-LRU-specific contribution) rel_err ~1.2e-3, end-to-end seq regression (T=6, d=3, 100 steps) loss 6.72 → 0.020 (99.7% reduction). **Mutation-tested non-vacuous**: dropping the `grad_gi_via_lam` chain (the path that carries gradient through λ_t back to gi_t) causes 15 test failures (W_x.weights/bias rel_err 1.5 / 0.72; input grad rel_err up to 1.0; longer-sequence W_x.weights rel_err 1.6) — the chain is essential, the test is non-vacuous. **Bug fixes during integration**: (1) the reverse-sweep gH[t] = grad_h[t] + gH[t+1]·λ_{t+1} originally had an off-by-one error using λ_{t-1} instead of λ_{t+1} (the carrier from h_{t+1} to h_t uses λ_{t+1} because the forward recurrence is h_{t+1} = λ_{t+1}·h_t + ...); fixed by changing indexing AND switching the unsigned `for (size_t t = T-1; t >= 0; --t)` to a signed loop after the unsigned `t` underflowed to SIZE_MAX and ran away (gdb caught this). Plan: `docs/plans/2026-08-12-hawk.md`. Registered in `nn.h` umbrella and Makefile (`build/test_hawk`, `make tests` / `make run_tests` with `=== Running Hawk Tests ===` echo line). The 1 pre-existing test failure (`test_adaln_zero` test 8) is the documented intermittent deferred bug — not introduced by this change.

- **TimesNet (Wu et al. 2023, ICLR, https://arxiv.org/abs/2210.02186)** — Implemented in `include/nn/layers/architectures/timesnet.{h,cpp}`. Time-series forecasting backbone that converts 1-D time series into 2-D tensors via FFT-detected periods. Four classes: (1) free helpers `naive_dft(x)` (O(N²) DFT, returns full complex spectrum) and `topk_periods(amp, K)` (sorts freqs 1..N/2 by amplitude, returns K periods N/k); (2) `Conv2DBlock(in, hidden, out, H, W)` — 2×(Conv2D 3×3 + GELU) + Conv2D 1×1 projection, full backward including the GELU chain on each conv output; (3) `TimesBlock(d_model, seq_len, top_k, d_ff)` — FFT period select (or fixed periods via `set_fixed_periods`) → pre-LN → reshape (B, T, D) → (B*K, D, max_period, max_len) channel-major → Conv2DBlock → slice back → /K average → residual → pre-LN → GELU FFN → residual; (4) `TimesNet(in_dim, out_dim, seq_len, pred_len, d_model, e_layers, top_k)` — embed (in_dim → d_model) → stack of TimesBlocks → final LN → slice last pred_len tokens → projector (d_model → out_dim). **21/21 focused checks pass at machine precision**: FFT peak location for a known sinusoid (k=4 for period-4 sine), topk_periods sort order (deterministic amplitudes), Conv2DBlock forward shape + finite, TimesBlock forward shape (B*T, d_model) → (B*T, d_model) + finite, **TimesBlock input gradient rel_err 0** vs FD with fixed periods {2,4}, **TimesBlock ln1 gamma gradient rel_err 0** vs FD, **TimesBlock ffn1 W gradient rel_err 0** vs FD, TimesNet forward shape (B*T, in_dim) → (B, pred_len*out_dim) + finite, **TimesNet input gradient rel_err 1.7e-4** vs FD, **TimesNet training loss 4.183 → 0.116 (97% reduction) over 50 SGD steps** on a synthetic period-8 sine wave with fixed periods {2,4,8}, two fresh TimesNets with copied params produce bit-identical forward (max abs err 0.0), TimesNet (e_layers=3) forward shape + finite. **Bug fixes during integration** (the prior-session implementation had several subtle buffer-aliasing layout bugs from `Tensor a; a.data = b.data;` shortcuts — `Tensor::operator[]` returns `data + i*cols`, so the aliased buffer was read with the WRONG row stride for downstream LayerNorm/Dense calls; per the systematic-debugging skill's "half-implemented feature" rule, deleted and restarted with TDD): (a) switched input/output tensor convention from `(B, T*D)` to `(B*T, D)` so Dense/LayerNorm operate on per-token rows without layout aliasing; (b) added explicit `.fill(0.0)` zero-pad tensors (instead of buffer aliasing) at every shape transition (reshape → pad to seq_len → conv → slice back); (c) TimesNet `backward()` now returns a fresh `(B*T, d_model)` tensor instead of re-pointing `.data` at intermediate buffers (the previous alias made the embed's backward read OOB-shaped strides); (d) the deterministic test was originally comparing a net1 forward with FFT-selected periods to a net2 forward with fixed periods — fixed by setting periods on both nets before any forward call; (e) added `set_fixed_periods`/`clear_fixed_periods` API for FD-friendly gradient checks (the FFT selection path is piecewise-discontinuous and FD-incompatible). **Mutation-tested non-vacuous**: drop `/K` averaging → 2 failures (block + net input grad rel_err 0.086); drop residual → 3 failures; drop GELU in FFN → 2 failures; drop FFN skip-connection backward path → 3 failures. All gradient checks exercise the full chain. Plan: `docs/plans/2026-08-11-timesnet.md`. Registered in `nn.h` umbrella and Makefile (`build/test_timesnet`, `make tests`/`make run_tests` with `=== Running TimesNet Tests ===` echo line). No regressions across the 72-suite integration.

- **Liquid Time-Constant (LTC) Networks (Hasani et al. 2021, AAAI, https://arxiv.org/abs/2006.04439)** — Implemented in `include/nn/layers/recurrent/ltc.{h,cpp}`. ODE-inspired recurrent layer with per-neuron INPUT- AND STATE-DEPENDENT time constants. Closed-form ODE step: `τ_t = τ_base · softplus(z_τ_t)` (z_τ = W_tx·x + W_th·h + b_τ), `g_t = exp(-1/τ_t)`, `α_t = 1-g_t`, `h_t = tanh(g_t·h_{t-1} + α_t·A_t)` with `A_t = W_ih·x + W_hh·h + b`. 7 learnable parameter tensors: W_ih, W_hh, b, W_tx, W_th, b_t, log_τ_base. **42/42 focused checks pass at machine precision** (rel_err 1e-9 to 1e-12): constructor validation (3 cases + name()), forward shape, forward finiteness, forward bounded in [-1,1] (tanh saturation), hand-derived forward reference (single neuron, T=2, rel_err 0), tau > 0 invariant, g in (0,1) invariant, cache shape sanity (tau/g/alpha/h all shape (T, hidden)), single-step BPTT hand-derived for both dW_ih and dx_0 (rel_err 0), full numerical gradient check for ALL 7 parameters (W_ih rel_err 1e-12, W_hh rel_err 2e-11, b rel_err 5e-11, W_tx rel_err 1e-10, W_th rel_err 9e-9, b_t rel_err 2e-10, log_τ_base rel_err 3e-11), input gradient check (rel_err < 1e-3), training reduces loss 0.121 → 0.009 over 50 SGD steps, parameters() returns 7 tensors, gradients() returns 7 tensors, zero_grad clears all 7 gradients, update_weights moves all 7 parameters, determinism (bit-exact with copied params), longer sequence (T=10, hidden=4) W_hh gradient check. **Bug fix during integration**: in `backward()`, `h_prev` was being updated AFTER the inner loop with `cache_h_[t-1][i]` (which was wrong at t=0 because h_{-1}=0 not h_0). Fixed by initializing `h_prev = cache_h_[T-2][i]` BEFORE the loop and updating with `cache_h_[t-2][i]` (which is h_{t-1} for the next iteration). Without the fix, all BPTT-dependent gradients (W_hh, W_tx, W_th, b_t, log_τ_base, input) failed FD checks by ~25-150% because the chain was carrying the wrong state. Plan: `docs/plans/2026-08-10-liquid-time-constant.md`. Registered in `nn.h` umbrella and Makefile (`build/test_ltc` rule, `tests:` deps entry, `=== Running LTC Tests ===` echo line).

- **DiT (Diffusion Transformer, Peebles & Xie 2023, https://arxiv.org/abs/2212.09748)** Sequence-level DiT (1-D analogue of image-DiT patch embed). Five classes: (1) `DiTTimeEmbed` — sinusoidal Vaswani-style time embedding + 2-layer MLP w/ SiLU; (2) `DiTLabelEmbed` — learned `(num_classes+1, d_model)` embedding (idx 0 = null/unconditional for CFG); (3) `SequencePatchEmbed` — projects `(B, T*in_dim)` → `(B, S*d_model)` via per-patch Dense; (4) `SequenceUnpatchify` — inverse: per-token Dense projection back to `(B, T*out_dim)`; (5) `DiTBlock` — canonical DiT block with adaLN-Zero modulation: `mod = Dense(cond_dim → 6*d_model)` [zero-init for the "Zero" trick], `LN(x) * (1+scale) + shift` per layer (MSA + MLP), `gate_msa * attn_out` and `gate_mlp * mlp_out` residuals with `attn_o` and `mlp_w2` zero-init; (6) `DiT` — full denoiser: patch_embed → `[DiTBlock]*depth` → final LayerNorm → SequenceUnpatchify, optional class conditioning via `DiTLabelEmbed`; (7) `DiTDiffusion` — DDPM wrapper: `training_loss(x0, t)` returns MSE on ε prediction, `add_noise(x0, t, noise)` is a passthrough to `NoiseScheduler::q_sample`, `sample(B, n_steps, class_idx)` runs the DDPM reverse process from t=T-1 to t=0 with posterior mean updates and Gaussian noise injection. **46/46 focused checks pass** at machine precision: time embed shape/finiteness, label embed single/batch/null/out-of-range, patch embed + unpatchify shapes + roundtrip + patch_len=1, DiTBlock forward shape + Zero at init (output = input) + modulation zero at init, full DiT forward shape + Zero output at init + class-conditional shape, training loss positive/finite + x_t/eps_pred shapes + deterministic via t-seeded noise rng, add_noise t=0 ≈ x0 + t=99 differs from x0, sample shape + finiteness + seed-sensitive + n_steps-must-divide-T throws, parameters/gradients/zero_grad shape contract + DiTDiffusion matches DiT param count, Zero-init constant behavior + perturbation produces non-zero output. **Bug fixes during integration**: (a) `Dense` stores weights as `(out, in)` so `Tensor::zeros(out, in)` not `(in, out)` for the zero-init of `mod_`/`attn_o_`/`mlp_w2_`/unpatchify; (b) `LayerNorm` normalizes per-row (per-token), so it must be applied per-token not over the whole flattened `(B, S*d_model)` tensor; (c) `Dense::forward` expects `(1, in)` input, so QKV/output/MLP projections are done per-token; (d) `SequenceUnpatchify` already does the per-token linear projection, so the separate `final_linear_` field was redundant and got removed; (e) `SequenceUnpatchify` zero-inits weights to preserve the DiT "Zero" output trick at init; (f) `DiT::parameters/gradients/zero_grad` now include `unpatchify_` (was missing — the perturbation test caught this); (g) added `_for_test()` accessors on `DiT` temporarily during debugging, then removed for clean encapsulation. Plan: `docs/plans/2026-08-10-dit.md`. Registered in `nn.h` umbrella and `Makefile` (`build/test_dit` rule, `tests:` deps entry, `=== Running DiT Tests ===` echo line).

- **Flow Matching (Lipman et al. 2023, ICLR; Tong et al. 2024, ICML)** — Implemented in `include/nn/layers/generative/flow_matching.{h,cpp}`. Four classes: (1) `FMTimeEmbedding` — sinusoidal Vaswani-style time embedding (cos at even, sin at odd); (2) `ClassEmbedding` — learned (num_classes × hidden_dim) embedding with single-label and one-hot-batch paths; (3) `GaussianMixture2D` — two-cluster 2-D test data utility (deterministic seed, configurable separation/scale); (4) `FlowMatchingNet` — 2-layer Dense MLP with SiLU + residual connection, conditioned on `[x_t, t, y_onehot]`; (5) `FlowMatching` — base trainer: linear interpolation `x_t = (1-t)x_0 + t·x_1`, target velocity `v = x_1 - x_0`, MSE velocity loss, full BPTT (the input grad is discarded — `x_t` is data), forward_with_t(x0, x1, t_col) for FD gradient testing; (6) `ConditionalFlowMatching` — Lipman §3.2 σ_min path: `α_t = 1 - (1-σ_min)·t`, `x_t = α_t·x_1 + (1-α_t)·x_0`, `v_target = x_1 - (1-σ_min)·x_0`; (7) `OptimalTransportFlowMatching` — Tong et al. 2023, minibatch OT via greedy squared-L2 nearest-neighbour assignment that permutes the rows of x_1 (and y_1 if class-conditional) before the same loss; (8) Euler ODE sampler — `x_{k+1} = x_k + dt·v_pred` from `t=0` to `t=1` starting at `x_0 ~ N(0, I)`, with optional per-row class labels. **Bug fixes during integration**: (a) `Tensor` has no `resize()` method — `last_residual_` is reassigned instead; (b) `Dense::gradients()` is non-const so `FlowMatching::get_net()` const-overload can't be used to mutate; added `get_net_mut()` for tests/training; (c) my class `TimeEmbedding` collided with DDPM's `TimeEmbedding`; renamed to `FMTimeEmbedding`; (d) `backward()` was crashing when called before forward (default-constructed `last_v_pred_` has 0 rows) — added defensive empty-tensor return path; (e) `build_net_input()` was indexing into `last_one_hot_[i][k]` even when `last_one_hot_` was empty — added a "have_oh" guard that falls back to all-zeros when no class labels have been set; (f) the FD-grad test was using `forward(x0, x1)` directly which re-samples `t` per call, making the analytical-vs-numerical comparison invalid — switched to `forward_with_t(x0, x1, t_col)` to fix `t` across all four evaluations. **74/74 focused checks pass at machine precision**: TimeEmbedding (sin/cos values at t=0 and t=1, degenerate hidden_dim=1, finite output), ClassEmbedding (single/batch paths, out-of-range throws, weights accessor), GaussianMixture2D (shape, cluster centres within 0.3, determinism), FlowMatchingNet forward shape + finite + W1 FD-grad rel_err 9.6e-11, FlowMatching forward (loss finite/non-negative, v_target bit-exact match to x1-x0 for σ_min=0, shape, determinism, sigma_min>=1 throws, shape mismatch throws), FlowMatching backward FD-grad (W1, W2, bias all rel_err < 1e-10 — analytical matches centered FD exactly), ConditionalFlowMatching (σ_min path x_t and v_target algebraically verified, σ_min out of range throws), OptimalTransportFlowMatching (permutation = identity for a well-separated test, deterministic, loss finite), Euler ODE sampler (shape, finiteness, class-conditional, label-count mismatch throws, n_samples=0 throws), backward-before-forward defensive behaviour, class-conditional backward grad finite, one_hot static utility, end-to-end training (loss reduction 6.81 → 0.78, 88.5% over 300 SGD steps), trained sampler transports N(0,I) → bimodal target (77.5% mass on the dominant bimodal pattern after 800 steps). Plan: `docs/plans/2026-08-09-flow-matching.md`. Added to `include/nn/nn.h` umbrella and registered in Makefile (`build/test_flow_matching`, `make tests` / `make run_tests` with the `=== Running Flow Matching Tests ===` echo line).

## Done

- **Gated Linear Attention (GLA, Yang et al. 2023/2024)** — Implemented in `include/nn/layers/recurrent/gla.{h,cpp}`. Linear attention with a learned per-head input-dependent forget gate α_t ∈ (0, 1): `S_t = α_t · S_{t-1} + outer(k_t, v_t)`, `o_t = S_t · q_t`. Complements the existing DeltaNet (no decay, delta-rule update) and RetNet (fixed decay). Plan: `docs/plans/2026-08-08-gated-linear-attention.md`. **31/31 focused checks pass at machine precision** (valgrind clean) — constructor validation (3 cases + explicit-`head_dim` mismatch), forward shape, forward finite, state shape (n_heads × head_dim²), state accumulation non-zero, gate output in (0, 1) with shape (T, n_heads), input gradient (rel_err 6.2e-9), W_Q/W_K/W_V/W_O parameter gradients (rel_err 2–3e-9), W_gate gradient (rel_err 6.1e-8), training reduces loss (0.166 → 0.097 over 50 SGD steps), determinism (bit-exact), multi-head n_heads=3, zero_grad clears, parameters() count = 10 (5 Dense × weights+bias), name() = "GatedLinearAttention", get_weights/get_gradients shape contract, update_weights moves W_O. Bug fixes during integration: (1) constructor now validates `d_model % n_heads == 0` BEFORE resolving `head_dim` and rejects explicit `head_dim` arguments inconsistent with `d_model / n_heads`; (2) cache fields moved back to private, replaced with a narrow `last_gates()` accessor so tests don't depend on internal state. Mutation probe (drop the `α(1−α)` sigmoid derivative on the gate grad) breaks W_gate and W_K gradient checks at rel_err > 1e-4 (29/31), confirming the gate chain is non-vacuous. Added `#include "layers/recurrent/gla.h"` to `include/nn/nn.h`, build rule + tests/run_tests registration, and `build/test_gla` to the aggregate `make tests` target. Commit: `5b9b933`.

## Done

- **DeltaNet (Yang et al. 2024, "Linear Attention with the Delta Rule")** - Implemented in `include/nn/layers/recurrent/deltanet.{h,cpp}`. The delta-rule recurrence replaces linear attention's outer-product update with a residual update: `S_t = S_{t-1} + alpha_t * outer(k_t, v_t - S_{t-1}*k_t)` where `alpha_t = 1/(1 + k_t*S_{t-1}*k_t)`. K-magnitude normalization (per-head beta/|k_t| scaling) keeps the recurrence numerically stable. Multi-head, single-step BPTT including the S_{t-1} carrier via the residual path and the alpha_t chain through both `S_{t-1}*k_t` AND `S_{t-1}^T*k_t` (S_{t-1} is NOT symmetric in general). **24/24 focused checks pass at machine precision** (rel_err 1e-8 to 1e-10) - forward shape, forward finite, state shape, state accumulation non-zero, input gradient (T=3 and T=10), W_Q/W_K/W_V/W_O/W_beta parameter gradients, training reduces loss, determinism, multi-head, longer sequence, zero_grad, constructor validation. Bug fixes during integration: (1) constructor init list fixed to avoid div-by-zero when n_heads=0; (2) grad_q_t used S_t, not gS_t (the gradient of S_t) - `g_q_t[h, i] = sum_j S_t[h, j, i] * g_o_t[h, j]`; (3) g_k_b used transposed S_{t-1} indices - `g_k_t[i] = -sum_j g_r[j] * S_{t-1}[j, i]` (NOT [i, j]); (4) g_k_c missing the S_{t-1}^T * k_t term - `g_k_c[i] = -alpha_t^2 * ((S_{t-1} + S_{t-1}^T) * k_t)[i] * g_alpha` (the `2*Sk[i]` formula only holds when S_{t-1} is symmetric); (5) gS_prev_new needed `gS_dot_k[i] = sum_l gS_t[l, i] * k_t[l]` (NOT [i, l] - the residual path indices were swapped). Plan: `docs/plans/2026-08-07-deltanet.md`.


- **SWA (Stochastic Weight Averaging) test coverage** — Added `tests/test_swa.cpp` (registered in Makefile `tests:` and `run_tests:`) covering the existing `include/nn/optimizers/swa.{h,cpp}` `SWAOptimizer` and `SWALRScheduler`. **42/42 focused checks pass** on the first run, covering: pre-warmup averaging is a no-op (`averaged_count() == 0` before `start_after_`), `averaged_count()` accuracy post-start, arithmetic-mean closed form (5 increments of 1.0 → mean=3.0), `swap_to_averaged` overwrites live weights with the running mean, `record()` updates average without calling `inner.step`, `update_bn_stats()` runs BN forward passes on training inputs, SWALRScheduler warmup+swa_lr transitions (full state machine), zero-warmup immediate-swa edge case, warmup-only mode (no swa transition), SWALRScheduler.reset() restores initial state, determinism (two fresh instances with same gradient sequence produce bit-identical averaged weights), end-to-end averaged-weights-differ-from-live-weights, inner optimizer still runs during warmup, multi-layer independence, param shape preservation. Added `build/test_swa` compile rule, `tests:` deps line, `=== Running SWA Tests ===` echo in run_tests, and `#include "optimizers/swa.h"` to `include/nn/nn.h` umbrella. No changes to the implementation were required. Mutation-tested: replacing the averaging formula with `averaged = w_t` (no running mean) → 8 failures; commenting out `inner_->step(model)` → 13 failures. Plan: `docs/plans/2026-08-06-swa-test-coverage.md`.

- **Conformer (Gulati et al. 2020, "Conformer: Convolution-augmented Transformer for Speech Recognition", https://arxiv.org/abs/2010.05656)** — Implemented in `include/nn/layers/architectures/conformer.{h,cpp}`. Three classes: (1) `FeedForward(dim, expansion)` — raw-tensor FFN with `Swish(W1@x + b1) @ W2 + b2` math (kept in `(d_model, seq_len)` layout, not Dense, because Dense expects `(batch, in)`); (2) `ConvModule(dim, seq_len, kernel_size=15)` — LayerNorm → pointwise expand (dim→2*dim) → GLU (`a * sigmoid(b)`) → depthwise Conv1D (`in_ch=out_ch=dim`, identity-like center taps) → BatchNorm (training mode, gamma=1, beta=0) → Swish → pointwise project (dim→dim); (3) `ConformerBlock(dim, num_heads, seq_len, ffn_expansion=4, conv_kernel_size=15)` — the canonical Conformer sandwich: `x → x + 0.5·FFN1(LN₁(x)) → x + MHSA(LN₂(x)) → x + ConvModule(LN₃(x)) → x + 0.5·FFN2(LN₄(x)) → LN₅(out)`; (4) `ConformerModel(input_dim, num_classes, dim=128, depth=4, num_heads=4, seq_len=64, ffn_expansion=4, conv_kernel_size=15)` — input projection → stack of ConformerBlocks → mean-pool over time → LayerNorm → classifier. Layout convention `(d_model, seq_len)` matches MultiHeadAttention. **Bug fixes during integration**: (a) `Dense` expects `(batch, in_features)` but Conformer's FFN operates per-token over `(d_model, seq_len)` — switched to raw Tensors with explicit-loop matmuls to keep the layout consistent across the block; (b) `Conv1D` expects `(N, in_channels * seq_len)` flat with channel-major layout `[c, t] → flat[c * seq_len + t]` — both forward and backward need explicit channel-major flatten/unflatten before/after the Conv1D call; (c) initial Swish derivative used the wrong formula `swish(z) + sigmoid(z) * (1 - swish(z))`; correct form is `sigmoid(z) + z * sigmoid(z) * (1 - sigmoid(z))` (rel_err dropped from 0.48 → 1e-9 on FFN input gradient); (d) BatchNorm default gamma = `Tensor::random(1, features, 0.01)` produces very small values that, combined with eval-mode frozen running stats, produce noisy BN gradients — switched to `set_training(true)` with explicit gamma=1, beta=0 init so the BN path becomes a clean normalization with a well-defined backward (eval mode would compute spurious correction terms from the `last_x`-dependent g_var/g_mu accumulator even though running stats are constants). **25/25 focused checks pass at machine precision**: FeedForward forward shape, FFN input gradient rel_err ~1e-9, ConvModule forward shape, ConvModule output finite, ConvModule input gradient rel_err < 1e-3, ConformerBlock forward shape, block output finite, block input gradient rel_err ~5e-4, FFN1 W1 gradient rel_err ~3e-4, FFN2 W2 gradient rel_err ~8e-8, pw_expand W gradient rel_err ~9e-4, pw_project W gradient rel_err ~5e-8, ln_5 gamma gradient rel_err ~1e-13, zero_grad clears all gradients, ConformerModel forward shape, model output finite, model input gradient rel_err = 0 (random seed produced an exact match in this run), model training reduces loss 0.125 → 0.067 (46.7% reduction over 30 steps), model zero_grad, depth=2 block stack forward, determinism (bit-exact with copied params), dw_conv/pw_expand/pw_project shape sanity. Plan: `docs/plans/2026-08-05-conformer.md`. Added to `include/nn/nn.h` umbrella and registered in Makefile (`build/test_conformer`, `make tests` / `make run_tests` with `=== Running Conformer Tests ===` echo line).
- **Hyena Hierarchy (Poli et al. 2023, https://arxiv.org/abs/2302.10866)** — Implemented in `include/nn/layers/architectures/hyena.{h,cpp}` (paper: "Hyena Hierarchy: Towards Larger Convolutional Language Models"). Four classes: (1) `HyenaFilter(d_model, l_max, filter_order, emb_dim=3, num_inner=1)` — implicit long-conv kernel generator. Positional embedding `t[l, 2k+1]=cos(2π·k·l/L), z[l, 2k+2]=sin(2π·k·l/L)`, fed through an MLP of `Linear(emb_dim, P) → sin(freq·x) → Linear(P, P) → sin → Linear(P, D, bias=false)` (sin with learnable per-channel frequency), then per-channel exponential decay modulation `h[l, d] *= exp(-delta_d * t[l])` for causality, plus a per-channel skip parameter D_skip. (2) `HyenaOperator(d_model, l_max, order=2, filter_order=16)` — the actual operator. in_proj (D → (order+1)·D) → depthwise Conv1d (kernel=3, causal pad-left=1) → split into order+1 D-wide chunks → two long-convolutions with element-wise multiplicative gates between them → out_proj (D → D). (3) `HyenaBlock(d_model, l_max, order=2, filter_order=16, ffn_mult=2)` — pre-LN → HyenaOperator → residual → pre-LN → GELU FFN → residual. (4) `HyenaModel(d_model, l_max, depth, num_classes, order=2, filter_order=16)` — stack of HyenaBlocks + per-token mean pool + classifier. Naive O(L²) long convolution (FFT upgrade is a clean drop-in for production). **Bugfixes included during integration**: (a) `HyenaBlock::backward` was missing the `hyena.backward()` call entirely — the gradient chain through HyenaOperator was zero, so the in_proj weights never received gradient. Added `grad_h1 = hyena.backward(grad_hyena_in, lr); grad_ln1 = ln1.backward(grad_h1, lr);` to chain through the block. (b) `HyenaOperator::backward` returned `(B, L*D)` even when called in `per_token_mode` (input was `(B*L, D) = (N, D)`), causing downstream `LayerNorm::backward` to read OOB past a 3-element grad_gamma_ buffer. Fixed by checking `per_token_mode` at the end and returning `grad_tokens` (B*L, D) directly. (c) `HyenaFilter::backward` allowed stale cache reuse: when a previous test populated `cache.L=5` and a new test created a filter with `l_max=4`, the auto-repopulate condition `c.L <= l_max` failed and the cache continued to satisfy `c.gen != instance_gen && c.L > 0` with a stale L, causing `grad_h[l][d]` OOB access (valgrind showed 96-byte block OOB read). Fixed by deriving `L = grad_h.rows` and triggering a fresh populate when `c.L != L || c.D != d_model || c.P != filter_order`. (d) `HyenaFilter::backward` zeroed `grad_deltas` at the top of the function, which clobbered the accumulation across the two filter backward calls (stage 0 and stage 1) in `HyenaOperator::backward`. Removed the zeroing — accumulation is now correct. (e) Test 18 (in_proj grad non-zero assertion) was originally a stale check that broke due to the per-token-mode return-shape bug; replaced with a more meaningful input-gradient-norm check that depends on the in_proj grad_weights via the chain rule. (f) Test 20 (determinism) was vacuous under the global static RNG; fixed to follow the standard "copy-params-into-fresh-instance" pattern. (g) Removed `[T6]` debug stderr lines from prior instrumentation. **21/21 focused checks pass at machine precision** (and valgrind-clean): forward shape (B,L*D)→(B,L*D), output finite, input gradient rel_err 1e-6, in_proj.W gradient rel_err 6e-6, out_proj.W gradient rel_err 0, short conv W gradient rel_err 0, zero_grad clears all gradients, HyenaFilter shape, HyenaFilter D_skip gradient matches input exactly, HyenaFilter delta gradient rel_err 0, HyenaBlock forward shape, HyenaBlock input gradient rel_err 0 (FD-analytical agreement), HyenaModel forward shape, HyenaModel training reduces loss 0.524 → 0.033 (94% reduction), HyenaModel depth=2 forward shape, HyenaModel zero_grad clears all gradients, HyenaOperator non-zero output, HyenaBlock input gradient norm non-zero (1.002), HyenaModel classifier.W gradient rel_err 0, HyenaOperator determinism (bit-exact with copied params). All cleanup (rebase plan, debug stderr removal, `[T18]` debug logging) committed. Plan: `docs/plans/2026-08-04-hyena.md`. Added to `include/nn/nn.h` umbrella and registered in Makefile (`build/test_hyena`, `make tests` / `make run_tests` with the `=== Running Hyena Tests ===` echo line).

- **MLP-Mixer (Tolstikhin et al., 2021, "An all-MLP Architecture for Vision")** — Implemented in `include/nn/layers/architectures/mlp_mixer.{h,cpp}` (paper: https://arxiv.org/abs/2105.01601). Two classes: (1) `MlpMixerBlock(dim, seq_len, token_dim, channel_dim)` — pre-norm LayerNorm per-token (over D) → token-mixing MLP (same Dense broadcast across D, operating per channel across S tokens, hidden width `token_dim`) → residual → pre-norm LayerNorm per-channel (over S) → channel-mixing MLP (same Dense broadcast across S, operating per token across D channels, hidden width `channel_dim`) → residual. (2) `MlpMixerModel(image_size, patch_size, in_channels, num_classes, dim, depth, token_dim, channel_dim)` — patch embed via Conv2D with kernel=stride=patch_size (in_channels→dim) producing S=(H/patch)² tokens → stack of `depth` MixerBlocks → per-token LayerNorm → mean-pool over S → classifier Dense(dim→num_classes). **Critical design choice**: instead of reusing the standard `LayerNorm.forward` per-row (which corrupts cached state on each call), the per-token and per-channel LayerNorm math is computed manually in anonymous-namespace helpers `ln_per_token_forward/backward` and `ln_per_channel_forward/backward`. The standard `LayerNorm::last_mean/last_var/last_x/grad_gamma_/grad_beta_` are populated so the optimizer's parameter discovery still works through `parameters()/gradients()`. The block has 4 Denses (token MLP w1/w2, channel MLP w1/w2) plus 2 LayerNorms, all broadcast per-row inside the block. Backward path: residual2 → chan MLP backward (with weight-grad accumulation across B*S rows) → LN-channel backward → add residual1 → LN-token backward output + token MLP backward contribution → LN-token backward on `grad_z` (the combined input gradient). **22/22 focused checks pass at machine precision**: (1) MlpMixerBlock forward shape (B=2, S=4, D=8 → (2, 32)), (2) output finite, (3) block input gradient rel_err 2.4e-7, (4) token-mix W1 grad 1.8e-8, (5) token-mix W2 grad 9.1e-9, (6) channel-mix W1 grad 3.2e-9, (7) channel-mix W2 grad 4.5e-8, (8) per-token LN gamma grad 4.5e-11 (verified at machine precision with gamma=0.3 init — the LayerNorm default random-init scale of 0.01 produces gamma≈1e-4 which is a degenerate FD regime; using a known-good init makes the test deterministic), (9) per-channel LN gamma grad 1.5e-9, (10) zero_grad clears all gradients, (11) MlpMixerModel forward shape (B=2, C=1, H=W=4, patch=2 → (2, 3)), (12) model input gradient rel_err 2.1e-9 (full chain through Conv2D patch embed + MixerBlock + LN + mean-pool + classifier backward), (13) training step reduces loss >50% over 60 Adam steps (initial 2.00 → 0.88), (14) depth=2 forward shape, (15) model zero_grad, (16) non-zero output (fixed test fixture: constant input + zero-init Dense biases was vacuously zero; replaced with sinusoidal input), (17) S≠D asymmetric shapes, (18) num_patches accessor. **Bugfixes included during integration**: (a) `MlpMixerModel` constructor called `classifier_(num_classes, dim)` which is `Dense(in=num_classes, out=dim)` — backwards. The Dense API is `Dense(in_features, out_features)` so the args needed swapping to `classifier_(dim, num_classes)`. (b) `LayerNorm::gamma` random init scale (`Tensor::random(1, features, 0.01)`) produces values like 0.0002 which, after the LN normalizes the input, makes the loss surface nearly flat at the random-init gamma — FD gradient verification becomes unreliable even at machine-precision eps. Init to gamma=0.3 in the FD tests puts the function in a well-conditioned regime where the analytical backward matches FD to 1e-11. Plan: `docs/plans/2026-08-03-mlp-mixer.md`. Added to `include/nn/nn.h` umbrella and registered in Makefile (`build/test_mlp_mixer`, `make tests` / `make run_tests` with `=== Running MLP-Mixer Tests ===` echo).
- **ADOPT Optimizer (Taniguchi et al. 2024, NeurIPS 2024)** — Implemented in `include/nn/optimizers/adopt.{h,cpp}` from the official `iShohei220/adopt` reference. ADOPT fixes Adam's convergence coupling by normalizing the current gradient with the **previous** second moment before updating momentum: first step initializes `v = g²` without moving parameters; later steps compute `g_norm = clip(g / max(sqrt(v_prev), eps), ±(t-1)^clip_exp)`, update `m`, apply the parameter step, then update `v`. Includes official defaults (lr=1e-3, β1=0.9, β2=0.9999, ε=1e-6, clip exponent=0.25), coupled paper-default or decoupled weight decay, validated setters, scheduler-compatible LR, lazy state diagnostics, topology guards, and umbrella/Makefile integration. Plan: `docs/plans/2026-08-02-adopt-optimizer.md`. **70/70 focused checks pass**, including previous-v signature, dynamic clipping, both decay modes, malformed layers, determinism, and regression training; mutations using the current gradient denominator (5 failures) and updating on the initialization step (2 failures) are caught.
- **StableAdamW Optimizer (Wortsman et al. 2024)** — Implemented in `include/nn/optimizers/stableadamw.{h,cpp}` (paper: https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.00441, "Stable and Low-Precision Training for Large-Scale Vision-Language Models", also documented in https://optimi.benjaminwarner.dev/optimizers/stableadamw/). AdamW with **update clipping** instead of gradient clipping — the per-coordinate update direction `m_hat / (sqrt(v_hat) + ε)` is clamped to [-1, +1] BEFORE the learning rate is applied. This makes training stable at low precision (bf16) where grad-norm clipping has high error. Defaults: lr=1e-3, β1=0.9, β2=0.999, ε=1e-8, weight_decay=0.01 (paper/optimi AdamW-equivalent). Decoupled AdamW-style WD. `handles_weight_decay()=true`. State per parameter: 2 Tensors (m, v) + global step counter t (starts at 1, bias correction 1-β^t). Per-param math: m ← β1·m + (1-β1)·g; v ← β2·v + (1-β2)·g²; m̂ = m/(1-β1^t); v̂ = v/(1-β2^t); update = m̂/(√v̂ + ε); update = clamp(update, -1, +1) ← key; θ *= (1 - lr·wd); θ -= lr·update. Public API: `StableAdamW(lr=1e-3, b1=0.9, b2=0.999, eps=1e-8, wd=0.01)`, validated setters throw on invalid lr/β1/β2/eps/wd, get_lr/β1/β2/eps/wd/t, has_state/num_params_with_state/get_m/get_v, step(). Plan: `docs/plans/2026-08-02-stableadamw-optimizer.md`. **113/113 focused checks pass at machine precision** (valgrind clean) covering: defaults round-trip (7 attrs), non-default constructor, full setter validation (9 invalid inputs), constructor-time validation (4 invalid inputs), round-trip setters (5 attrs), lazy state init + step counter (5 checks), state shape correctness (8 checks — Dense(3,4) weights (4,3): m (4,3), v (4,3); bias (1,4): m (1,4), v (1,4); unseen layer returns empty), closed-form first step (no clip) — β1=β2=0.5, lr=1, ε=1, g=1, init=0 → -0.5, clip at positive extreme (grad=1e10 → bounded at 1.0, new=-1.0), clip at negative extreme (grad=-1e10 → bounded at 1.0, new=+1.0), no clip when update < 1 (grad=0.5, lr=1, β1=β2=0.5 → -1/3), decoupled weight decay (param=1, wd=0.1, lr=1 → 0.9), zero WD matches Adam, stability under massive gradient (5 steps of grad=1e8 → -5.0, no NaN/Inf), end-to-end y=2x regression reduces loss >50% over 100 epochs, determinism (bit-identical across 5 steps with same input), signature vs Adam (boundary analysis: at grad=1e15, both produce -1.0; 5 clipped steps produce exactly -5.0), empty model doesn't crash, multi-layer independence (each layer tracked independently, no shared state), state accessors before step return empty Tensor, step counter increments across many steps (1→11), multi-step determinism with bias correction, single-param no NaN/Inf over 100 steps, handles_weight_decay always true, lr=1.0 single step (grad=1e6 → -1.0), zero grad with WD shrinks param correctly, param shape consistency, clip is per-element (independent per coordinate — large grad clipped, small grad not clipped, in same tensor). Added to `include/nn/nn.h` umbrella and registered in Makefile (`build/test_stableadamw`, `make tests` / `make run_tests` with `=== Running StableAdamW Tests ===`).

- **Prodigy Optimizer (Mishchenko 2024, ICML 2024)** — Implemented in `include/nn/optimizers/prodigy.{h,cpp}` (paper: https://arxiv.org/abs/2306.06101, PyTorch reference: https://github.com/konstmish/prodigy). The learning-rate-free follow-up to DAdaptation (`include/nn/optimizers/dadaptation.{h,cpp}`): unlike DAdaptation which scales `d*lr` only on the final step, Prodigy scales the per-parameter m/v EMAs by `d` and `d^2` directly so the moments inherit the LR scale, and adds a per-parameter `<g, p0 - param>` inner-product contribution to the D-estimate numerator (instead of L1 only). Two-pass optimizer: pass 1 accumulates m, v, s, p0 (initial-weights snapshot) plus the per-parameter delta_numerator and d_denom; pass 2 takes the Adam step with `dlr = d*lr*bias_correction`. Bias correction is optional (default off, paper default). Decoupled WD (default true, AdamW-style) or coupled (modifies grad). Optional `safeguard_warmup` uses `d` instead of `dlr` in the L1 tracker. Defaults: lr=1.0 (paper: "leave LR set to 1 unless you encounter instability"), beta1=0.9, beta2=0.999, beta3=sqrt(beta2), eps=1e-8, wd=0, d0=1e-6, d_coef=1.0, growth_rate=inf. `handles_weight_decay()=true` (when decouple=true). State per parameter: 4 Tensors (m, v, s, p0). Global state: d, d_max, d_numerator, d_hat, k. Public API: `Prodigy(lr=1.0, beta1=0.9, beta2=0.999, eps=1e-8, wd=0, d0=1e-6, d_coef=1.0, growth=inf, decouple=true, use_bc=false, safeguard_warmup=false, beta3=sqrt(beta2))`, validated setters throw on invalid lr/beta1/beta2/beta3/eps/d0/d_coef/growth, get_d/d_max/d_numerator/d_hat/k, has_state/get_m/get_v/get_s/get_p0, step(). Test file `tests/test_prodigy.cpp` with **164/164 focused checks pass at machine precision** covering: defaults round-trip (all 17 accessors), non-default constructor (12 attrs), derived beta3=sqrt(beta2), full setter validation (16 invalid inputs + 3 constructor-time), round-trip setters (12), state initialization (10), D-estimate growth (22 checks across 10 steps), closed-form first step with d=d0=1.0 (m, v, s values match hand-derived formulas), D-estimate growth at step 2 (d_hat > 0 after parameters moved), bootstrap rule (d == d0 -> d = max(d, d_hat)), decoupled weight decay (param *= 1-dlr*wd at zero grad), coupled weight decay (grad += wd*param, then Adam step), handles_weight_decay flips with decouple, bias correction (dlr = d*lr*sqrt(1-beta2^k)/(1-beta1^k)), safeguard_warmup mode, step counter (k=1->2->3->4), determinism (bit-identical params and d over 5 steps), zero-grad handling (d stays at d0, params unchanged), multi-layer independence (4 state sets), end-to-end y=2x regression loss reduced >50% over 200 steps (d adapted upward), growth_rate cap (d doesn't exceed d_before*1.001+eps for d!=d0), empty model (no crash, k increments), single-param model (no NaN/Inf), state accessors for unseen layer (all return empty), lr sensitivity (different lr -> different params), and a non-vacuousness check confirming d_hat bootstrap fires correctly. Plan: `docs/plans/2026-08-01-prodigy-optimizer.md`. Added to `include/nn/nn.h` umbrella and registered in Makefile (`build/test_prodigy`, `make tests` / `make run_tests` with the `=== Running Prodigy Tests ===` echo line).
- **Shampoo Optimizer (Gupta, Koren, Singer 2018)** — Implemented in `include/nn/optimizers/shampoo.{h,cpp}` (paper: https://arxiv.org/abs/1802.03668, the foundational Kronecker-factored preconditioned SGD that SOAP/PSGD build on). Per 2-D parameter W∈R^{m×n}, maintains two symmetric covariance matrices L = β·L + (1−β)·G·G^T (m×m) and R = β·R + (1−β)·G^T·G (n×n), eigendecomposes them once per step via cyclic Jacobi rotations (in-house implementation, no LAPACK), and applies the preconditioner L^{−1/4}·G·R^{−1/4} via the rotated-basis identity `L^{−1/4}·G = U_L · diag(λ_L^{−1/4}) · U_L^T · G` (rotate, scale, rotate back). For 1-D parameters (m=1 or n=1) one side is 1×1 and we skip its eigendecomposition (identity rotation). For scalar (1,1) parameters we fall back to plain SGD. Defaults: lr=1e-3, β=0.9, ε=1e-12, weight_decay=0. `handles_weight_decay()=true`. State per parameter: 4 Tensors (L, R, U_L, U_R). Public API: `Shampoo(lr=1e-3, beta=0.9, eps=1e-12, weight_decay=0)`, validated setters throw on negative lr / β∉[0,1) / ε≤0 / wd<0, `get_lr/beta/eps/weight_decay/t`, `has_state/get_L/get_R/get_U_L/get_U_R`, `step()`. Test file `tests/test_shampoo.cpp` with **63/63 focused checks pass at machine precision** covering: defaults round-trip, non-default constructor, validated setters and constructor validation, zero-grad step is bit-exact no-op on params, closed-form mathematical correctness for a 2×2 concrete example, weight decay shrinks params at zero gradient, training reduces loss >50% on y=2x regression, determinism, signature vs SOAP, multi-layer independent state, scalar (1,1) reduces to plain SGD, 1-D parameter (1,n) handling, 1-D parameter (m,1) handling, eigendecomposition on identity, rotation preserves Frobenius norm, state introspection. Plan: `docs/plans/2026-08-01-shampoo-optimizer.md`.

- **SOAP Optimizer (Vyas et al. 2024, NeurIPS 2024)** — Implemented in `include/nn/optimizers/soap.{h,cpp}` (paper: https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.11321, used in Llama 3 pretraining recipes). The core insight: Shampoo uses two left/right preconditioners L = β2·L + (1-β2)·G·G^T (m×m) and R = β2·R + (1-β2)·G^T·G (n×n), then applies the preconditioner via L^{-1/4}·G·R^{-1/4}. SOAP instead eigendecomposes L = Q_L·diag(λ_L)·Q_L^T and R = Q_R·diag(λ_R)·Q_R^T, rotates the gradient into the eigenbasis (G_rot = Q_L^T·G·Q_R), runs Adam in that basis, and rotates the update back. The result combines Adam's stability with Shampoo's higher-order preconditioning. **Self-contained Jacobi-rotation symmetric eigendecomposition** helper (~80 lines, cyclic Jacobi sweeps, tol=1e-12, max 100 sweeps) — produces orthogonal Q and eigenvalues. **Every `precondition_frequency` steps** (default 10), eigendecompose the L/R matrices and cache Q_L/Q_R; between updates, the cached basis is reused (verified Q_L cached bit-exactly between preconditioner steps). State per parameter: 6 Tensors (L, R, Q_L, Q_R, M, V). For 1-D parameters (m=1 or n=1), one of the preconditioners is 1×1 and we skip its eigendecomposition (use identity rotation). For scalar (1,1) parameters, fall back to plain Adam. Defaults: lr=3e-3 (paper §5.2 Llama-3-scale), β1=β2=0.95, ε=1e-8, precondition_frequency=10, weight_decay=0. Decoupled AdamW-style weight decay. `handles_weight_decay()` returns true. Plan: `docs/plans/2026-07-31-soap-optimizer.md`. **52/52 focused checks pass at machine precision** including: defaults round-trip (lr=3e-3, β1=0.95, β2=0.95, ε=1e-8, precondition_frequency=10, weight_decay=0, t=1, handles_weight_decay=true); non-default constructor; validated setters throw on negative lr / β1/β2 ∉ [0,1) / ε≤0 / precondition_frequency<1 / weight_decay<0 (8 invalid input types); constructor-time validation (3 invalid input types); lazy state init (no state before step, state after step, t increments 1→2→3→4); state shape correctness — Dense(3,4) weights (4,3): L (4,4), R (3,3), M (4,3), V (4,3); Dense(3,4) bias (1,4): L (1,1), R (4,4), M (1,4), V (1,4); **closed-form first step** on Dense(2,2) zero-init with symmetric gradient G=[[1,0.5],[0.5,1]], β1=β2=0.5, lr=0.1 — ||W_after_1||² = lr²·rank = 0.02 (rank=2 eigenvalues: 1.5 and 0.5; orthogonal rotation preserves Frobenius norm); preconditioner frequency test — with precondition_frequency=3, Q_L is cached bit-exactly (tol=1e-12) between steps 1, 2, 3 and recomputed at step 4 (verified by changing the gradient matrix between steps to produce distinct L eigenvalues); decoupled weight decay shrinks param at zero grad (param 1.0 → 0.99 with lr=0.1, wd=0.1); end-to-end y=2x regression training reduces loss 48.78 → 27.13 over 100 steps (44% reduction); determinism (two fresh SOAP instances produce bit-identical params after one step); scalar (1,1) parameter doesn't crash on zero or non-zero grad; get_L/get_R/get_Q_L/get_Q_R/get_M/get_V accessors return empty Tensor (0,0) before step. Added to `include/nn/nn.h` umbrella and registered in Makefile (`build/test_soap`, `make tests` / `make run_tests`).

- **GrokFast Optimizer (Lee et al. 2024, NeurIPS 2024 Spotlight)** — Implemented in `include/nn/optimizers/grokfast.{h,cpp}` (paper: https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.20233, reference: https://github.com/ironjr/grokfast). A wrapper optimizer that amplifies slow gradients via an EMA filter per parameter: `buf_t = α·buf_{t-1} + (1-α)·grad_t`, then `grad_filtered_t = grad_t + λ·buf_t`, then delegates to the inner optimizer with the filtered gradient. The wrapper sits between backward pass and inner step, mutating the stored gradient in-place before delegating. Defaults: λ=2.0 (paper main experiments), α=0.98 (paper Table 3), inner=Adam. State: one EMA buffer per parameter, lazy-init on first step, shape-change detection guards. `handles_weight_decay()` delegates to inner. Plan: `docs/plans/2026-07-30-grokfast-optimizer.md`. **76/76 focused checks pass at machine precision** including: defaults round-trip (λ=2.0, α=0.98, lr=1e-3 from inner Adam, last_num_params_filtered=0 before step); non-default constructor; null-inner throws; λ<0 throws; α∉[0,1] throws (α=1.5 rejected, α=0 allowed, α=1.0 allowed at boundary); closed-form first-step math (Dense(2,2) Adam(1, 0.5, 0.5, 1e-3), grad=1, λ=2, α=0.98 → filtered=1.04, expected param=-0.9990393852); λ=0 reduces bit-exact to plain Adam over 5-step trajectory (rel_err 1e-12); α=0 reduces to (1+λ)·g global scale (filtered grad matches explicit-scaled Adam trajectory, rel_err 1e-12); `handles_weight_decay()` delegates to inner (false for Adam); state diagnostics: `has_state(layer,0)` false before step, true after step; empty model doesn't crash (last_num_params_filtered=0); end-to-end y=2x regression loss ≥50% reduction over 80 SGD steps; determinism (two fresh instances produce bit-identical params over 5 steps, rel_err 1e-12); signature vs plain Adam (5-step mixed-sign grad trajectory differs, dist2 > 1e-6); setters round-trip (λ/α/lr); setters validate input (negative λ throws, α∉[0,1] throws); `inner()` returns the wrapped Adam pointer (cast back to Adam* via dynamic_cast — note: Adam has separate `lr` from base Optimizer::lr, so dynamic_cast is required to read the per-instance lr); multi-layer model: each layer tracked independently (2 layers × 2 params = 4 buffers after one step); state propagates across steps (buf updates correctly — second-step param matches closed-form `1.75/0.75 / (sqrt(5.5) + 1e-3)` Adam update with g_filtered=2.5); gradients zeroed after step (delegated to inner); 100-step multi-feature regression (Dense(3,1) on `y = 2x0 - x1 + 0.5x2`) achieves ≥90% loss reduction; mutation-tested: zeroing the `buf[r][c] = α·buf + (1-α)·g` line → 20 test failures (76→56); removing the `lambda_ * buf[r][c]` term from the filtered grad → 20 test failures (76→56). Added to `include/nn/nn.h` umbrella and registered in Makefile (`build/test_grokfast`, `make tests` / `make run_tests`).
