- **Test coverage for 4 shipped-but-untested utilities** — Added `tests/test_label_smoothing.cpp` (63 checks), `tests/test_clip_grad_norm.cpp` (53 checks), `tests/test_mixup_cutmix.cpp` (4302 checks), `tests/test_elastic_net.cpp` (46 checks). All four utilities were in `include/nn/nn.h` umbrella but had no test file; total **4464 focused checks pass at machine precision**. **BUGFIX INCLUDED**: `LabelSmoothingCrossEntropy::backward()` was reading `last_smooth_targets_` from a prior `forward()` call (default-constructed Tensor when called without prior forward → segfault on first call), AND the smoothing convention was wrong — the formula `y_smooth[label] = 1-eps` (instead of `1-eps+eps/K`) made the smoothed distribution sum to `1 - eps/K` instead of 1, breaking the gradient formula `(softmax - y_smooth) / B` (which is only correct when Σy_smooth = 1). Fixed by factoring `build_smooth_targets()` static helper, using PyTorch convention (true class = `1-eps+eps/K`, off classes = `eps/K` so the distribution sums to 1), and recomputing smooth targets inside `backward()` from `targets` alone (matching `softmax_cross_entropy_grad`'s self-contained API). Numerical vs analytical gradient check rel_err ~1e-10. All four registered in Makefile (`build/test_label_smoothing`, `build/test_clip_grad_norm`, `build/test_mixup_cutmix`, `build/test_elastic_net`, plus `=== Running X Tests ===` lines in `run_tests:`). Added two `.gitignore` exceptions for `tests/test_clip_grad_norm.cpp` which was being filtered by the `test_*check*.cpp` and `test_*grad_*.cpp` debug-test patterns. Plan: `docs/plans/2026-07-30-shipped-utility-coverage.md`. Commit: `724bc40`.

- **LAMB optimizer correctness hardening (You et al. 2019, ICLR 2020)** — Corrected `include/nn/optimizers/lamb.{h,cpp}` to follow Algorithm 2 of "Large Batch Optimization for Deep Learning: Training BERT in 76 minutes" (https://arxiv.org/abs/1904.00962). The previous kernel computed its trust ratio as `||w||/||raw_gradient||`, omitted weight decay, and consumed a shadowing `LAMB::lr`; canonical LAMB instead forms bias-corrected Adam direction `r = m_hat/(sqrt(v_hat)+eps)`, complete update `u = r + wd*w`, then applies tensor-local `q = ||w||/||u||` and `w -= lr*q*u`. Preserved the historical `trust_ratio_gamma` extension as a validated `[1/gamma, gamma]` clamp and the existing five-argument constructor prefix; added coupled weight decay, scheduler-compatible inherited `Optimizer::lr`, validated setters/accessors, lazy `(m,v)` state diagnostics, cached trust ratios, and parameter/gradient count/shape guards. Plan: `docs/plans/2026-07-29-lamb-correctness.md`. Added `tests/test_lamb.cpp` and Makefile registration. **78/78 focused checks pass** including a hand-derived signature that distinguishes the corrected denominator from the old raw-gradient formula, decay-inside-adaptation, upper/lower clamp boundaries, zero-norm fallbacks, two-step moments/bias correction, scheduler compatibility, state isolation, malformed layers, deterministic trajectory, gradient clearing, and regression loss `8.11790 -> 3.97e-11`. Mutation tests caught raw-gradient denominator (9 failures), omitted decay (5 failures), and forced `q=1` (11 failures). Aggregate `make tests` compiles all 109 registered binaries and `make run_tests` executes all 105 stable suites successfully; the four omissions exactly match `NOT_FIXED.md`.

- **AdamP (Heo et al. 2021) projected gradient optimizer** — Implemented in `include/nn/optimizers/adamp.{h,cpp}` (paper: https://arxiv.org/abs/2106.04522). Adam variant that adds a projection step on top of Adam: when the gradient direction is over-aligned with the weight vector (cosine similarity > delta), removes the component along the weight direction. This targets scale-invariant weights (Dense, Conv) where the loss is invariant to w → c·w, so any step along w is wasted + destabilizing. Algorithm: (1) standard Adam m and v updates, (2) compute cos_sim = (w · m) / (||w|| · ||m|| + eps), (3) if cos_sim > delta: subtract (w · m) / (||w||² + eps) · w from m_t, (4) bias correction + step. Defaults: lr=1e-3, β=(0.9, 0.999), ε=1e-8, δ=0.1, wd=0. Decoupled AdamW-style weight decay. State: per-parameter m, v tensors. `handles_weight_decay()` returns true. Plan: `docs/plans/2026-07-28_234101-adamp-optimizer.md`. **86/86 focused checks pass at machine precision** including: defaults round-trip (lr=1e-3, β=(0.9, 0.999), ε=1e-8, δ=0.1, wd=0, t=1, handles_weight_decay=true); non-default constructor; validated setters throw on negative lr / β1≥1 / β2≥1 / ε≤0 / δ∉[-1,1] / wd<0; constructor-time validation (6 invalid inputs throw); zero-grad step leaves params unchanged EXCEPT for weight decay (param *= 1-lr*wd); closed-form first step with delta=1.0 (no projection) — Dense(2,2) all-zero init + all-one grad + lr=1, β=0.5, ε=1 → weight -= 0.5, bias -= 0.5 (matches Adam exactly); projection IS applied for aligned gradient (cos_sim=1.0 → (w·m)/(||w||²+eps)·w subtraction removes the w-aligned component, leaving weight ~1.0 within numerical precision); projection NOT activated when delta=1.0 (cos_sim ∈ [-1,1] never exceeds 1.0); state shape correctness (m and v match param shape for both weight and bias); multi-layer independent state (Dense(3,4)→weights(4,3), Dense(4,2)→weights(2,4)); determinism (two fresh instances produce bit-exact updates over 5 random-grad steps); signature vs Adam with delta=1.0: trajectory matches Adam bit-exact (no projection); signature vs Adam with delta=0.0: trajectory differs (projection activates); bias correction across t=1,2,3 (steps produce different denoms); **end-to-end MSE reduction on y=2x linear regression from 56.18 → 0.0019 (>99% reduction)** over 200 epochs with Dense(2,1); step counter increments; handles_weight_decay=true; state accessors before step() return false; projection scale formula correctness (hand-derived `m_after = m - (w·m)/(||w||²+eps)·w` checked against Adam comparison). **NON-VACUOUSNESS VERIFIED via 3 mutation tests**: (1) stub out the projection line → 6 failures (Test 7 catches the unprojected step, Test 14 catches the Adam-equality, Test 19 catches the projection scale), (2) skip bias correction → 12 failures (Test 6 fails the closed-form, Test 13 fails the Adam-equality), (3) skip weight decay → 2 failures (Test 10 catches the unrolled weight decay). Public-API contract: validation throws `std::invalid_argument` on `n_splits < 2`, `n_splits > n_samples`, `X.rows != y.rows`, or `X.rows == 0`; non-finite eval scores produce a non-finite aggregate without crashing. Numerically stable two-pass variance formula (avoids the `sum_sq/k - mean²` catastrophic-cancellation trap). - **DiffGrad optimizer (Dubey et al. 2019)** — Implemented in `include/nn/optimizers/diffgrad.{h,cpp}` (paper: https://arxiv.org/abs/1909.11015). Adam variant modulating the first-moment step by a DiffGrad Friction Coefficient (DFC) `ξ_t = sigmoid(|g_{t−1} − g_t|) ∈ (0, 1)`. State per parameter: `m`, `v`, `g_prev`, `t` (one extra cached gradient vs Adam for the local-change rate signal). Per Algorithm 1: `m = β1·m + (1−β1)·g`, `v = β2·v + (1−β2)·g²`, `m_eff = dfc·m`, `param -= lr·√(1−β2^t)/(1−β1^t) · m_eff / (√v + ε)`, with `dfc = 1/(1+exp(−|g_prev − g|))` and `g_prev ← g` at end of step. Decoupled AdamW-style weight decay (`handles_weight_decay()=true`). Validation: `lr≥0`, `β1,β2 ∈ [0,1)`, `ε>0`, `wd≥0`; setters and constructor both throw on invalid inputs. Plan: `docs/plans/2026-07-26-diffgrad-optimizer.md`. **69/69 focused checks pass at machine precision** including: defaults round-trip (lr=1e-3, β=(0.9, 0.999), ε=1e-8, wd=0, t=1, `handles_weight_decay=true`); non-default constructor; validated setters throw on negative lr / β1≥1 / β2≥1 / ε≤0 / wd<0; constructor-time validation (6 invalid inputs throw); zero-grad step leaves params unchanged bit-exact but advances `t` (1→2→3); closed-form first-step analytic on Dense(2,2) zero-init + grad=1 + lr=1 + β=ε=0.5 → per-element `m_1=0.5, v_1=0.5, dfc=sig(1)≈0.7311 (g_prev=0 init), m_eff≈0.3655, step_size=√0.5/0.5≈1.4142, update=0.4280, param_1=−0.4280` matches formula to 1e-6; DFC varies with gradient change (constant [1,1] takes different magnitude than varying [1,2]); g_prev cache feeds step 2 correctly (after two identical constant-gradient steps, second-step weight lands at −0.025 not −0.037); real gradient produces nonzero step; multi-layer independence (each layer gets its own state, has_state/0,1 both true); decoupled weight decay shrinks param at zero grad (`param *= (1 − lr·wd)^N`); state shape correctness (m, v, g_prev all zero-init at the param's rows×cols); determinism (two fresh instances produce bit-exact step counts and handles_weight_decay after identical runs); end-to-end MSE reduction on y=2x linear regression from 1.354 → 7.4e-32 (>99.999% reduction over 200 epochs); step counter monotonic across 5 steps. **NON-VACUOUSNESS VERIFIED via 4 mutation tests**: (1) remove DFC (set dfc unused, m_eff ← m) → 6 failures (closed-form step drift on all 4 weights + 2 biases), (2) g_prev never updates (comment out `st.g_prev[i][j] = g;`) → 1 failure at exactly the predicted `−0.037 vs expected −0.025` (proving the test (f-bis) catches the cache staleness), (3) skip bias correction entirely → 6 failures at `−0.3028 vs expected −0.4282` (off by √(1−β2^t) factor), (4) signatures vs Adam (proper DFC application makes step trajectories non-trivially different). All 4 mutations caught, confirming test coverage exercises the full DFC, the g_prev update, the closed-form value, and the bias correction. Added to `include/nn/nn.h` umbrella and registered in Makefile (`build/test_diffgrad`, `make tests`/`make run_tests`).

- **DAdaptation (Defazio & Mishchenko 2023, ICML 2023)** — Implemented in `include/nn/optimizers/dadaptation.{h,cpp}` (paper: https://arxiv.org/abs/2301.07733). Learning-rate-free Adam variant: user passes `lr=1.0` (or any constant) and the algorithm automatically discovers the distance-to-solution `D` from running gradient statistics. Per Algorithm 5 of the paper: per-parameter Adam state (`m`, `v`, `s`) weighted by the running estimate `d · lr`, plus a global `D` scalar updated via the empirical inner-product bound `d_hat = r / ((1 − √β2) · ||s||_1)` where `r` is the √β2-EMA of `<g, s/denom>` and `s` is the √β2-EMA of `d · lr · g`. Two-pass algorithm matching PyTorch reference: first pass computes accumulators using OLD `v`/`s`, applies in-place EMA updates, then second pass applies the actual parameter step using NEW `v`. Bias correction toggle (off by default per paper). Decoupled (AdamW-style) or coupled (Adam-style) weight decay — `handles_weight_decay()` returns `true` when decoupled. Validation: `lr > 0`, `d0 > 0`, `eps > 0`, `betas ∈ [0,1)`, `growth_rate ≥ 1`. State accessors (`get_d()`, `get_numerator_weighted()`, `get_step()`, `get_m/v/s`, `has_state`, `num_params_with_state`). Zero-gradient early-return (sk_l1 == 0 → no update, but step counter advances). Plan: `docs/plans/2026-07-25-dadaptation-optimizer.md`. **150/150 focused checks pass at machine precision** including: defaults (lr=1.0, β=(0.9, 0.999), ε=1e-8, wd=0, d0=1e-6, decouple=false, use_bias_correction=false, growth_rate=inf, k=1); non-default constructor; validated setters throw on invalid inputs (lr≤0, β∉[0,1), ε≤0, wd<0, d0≤0, growth_rate<1); constructor-time validation (d0=0 throws, lr=0 throws); closed-form first step on Dense(2,2) with all-zero init, all-one gradient, lr=1, d0=1e-6 → per-element `m_1 = (1-β1)·1e-6 = 1e-7`, `v_1 = 1e-3`, `s_1 = (1-√β2)·1e-6 ≈ 5.001e-10`, `param_1 = -m_1/(√v_1 + eps) ≈ -3.162e-6`; D estimate monotonic non-decreasing across 5 constant-grad steps (paper Theorem); zero-grad step doesn't crash, doesn't change params, but advances `k`; decoupled WD shrinks params at zero gradient (`param *= 1 - lr*d*wd`); coupled WD applies `grad += wd*param` then uses effective gradient for `m`/`v`/`s` (PyTorch reference convention); `handles_weight_decay()` returns `false` (coupled) or `true` (decoupled); state initialization on first step (`has_state` transitions false→true, `num_params_with_state` matches W+b count, `m`/`v`/`s` shapes match param); step counter `k` increments monotonically; **determinism: two fresh instances bit-exact over 5 random-grad steps** (no RNG in algorithm); signature vs Adam (different trajectory under same gradient sequence); bias-correction toggle (BC version scales dlr by `sqrt(1-β2^k)/(1-β1^k)` ≈ 1.0005, so `d` differs from no-BC version after a few steps); growth_rate=1.02 caps `d_{k+1} ≤ 1.02 · d_k`; multi-layer independence (each layer gets its own state, step counter advances); **end-to-end training: y=2x linear regression with `DAdaptAdam(lr=1.0)` for 60 steps reduces MSE from 16.875 → 0.013 (>99% reduction, d converges to ≈3.12)**; `growth_rate=1.0` keeps `d` at d0 forever (cap binds to `min(d_hat, d)`); setters round-trip after construction. **NON-VACUOUSNESS VERIFIED via 4 mutation tests**: (1) skip `m` update → 6 failures (first-step param[0..1][0..1][0..1] checks, m_1 checks), (2) skip `d_hat = max(d, d_hat)` → 7 failures (D monotonicity, growth check, bias correction, end-to-end loss, growth_rate=1.0 keeps d at d0), (3) use β2 instead of √β2 in `s` EMA → 4 failures (first-step s_1 closed-form), (4) skip coupled WD → 6 failures (coupled WD param update checks). All 4 mutations caught, confirming test coverage exercises the Adam update, the D-update rule, the √β2-vs-β2 EMA coefficient distinction, and the coupled-vs-decoupled WD scaling. Added to `include/nn/nn.h` umbrella and registered in Makefile (`build/test_dadaptation`, `make tests`/`make run_tests`).

- **Adam-mini (Zhang et al. 2024)** — Implemented in `include/nn/optimizers/adam_mini.{h,cpp}` (paper: https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.16793). Memory-efficient Adam variant using block-wise second-moment reduction: for 2-D weight matrices, v is reduced to one value per row (mean of g² along columns), saving ~50% of optimizer state memory without performance loss. Three block modes supported: FULL (per-element v, Adam-equivalent), ROW_MEAN (one v per row — the paper's main case for 2-D weights), SCALAR (one global v). Default policy: AUTO → 2-D uses ROW_MEAN, 1-D uses FULL. State per parameter: 2 tensors (m full-shape + vmean reduced) — exactly half of Adam for 2-D params. Decoupled AdamW-style weight decay. Bias correction, per-parameter block-mode override via `set_param_block_mode(layer, idx, mode)`, parameter/gradient count & shape mismatch guards, deterministic trajectory over multiple steps, and signature tests vs Adam (different parameter trajectory under same gradient sequence). Plan: `docs/plans/2026-07-25-adam-mini-optimizer.md`. **70/70 focused checks pass at machine precision** including defaults (lr=1e-3, β=(0.9, 0.999), ε=1e-8, wd=0, mode=AUTO), non-default constructor, validated setters throw on invalid inputs (lr<0, β∉[0,1), ε≤0, wd<0), closed-form FULL mode (β1=β2=0.5, lr=1, ε=1, g=1, init=0 → -0.5), closed-form ROW_MEAN on all-equal g (all 12 entries → -0.5), ROW_MEAN distinguishes across rows (g=1 rows: denom=2; g=2 rows: denom=3), ROW_MEAN within-row reduction (g=[0,2,0] → mean(g²)=4/3 broadcast across the row), SCALAR mode closed-form (asymmetric g → single global vmean), bias correction across t=1,2,3 produces different updates, state shape correctness (weight (R,C) → m (R,C), vmean (R,1); bias (1,C) → m (1,C), vmean (1,C) for FULL), decoupled weight decay (1.0 → 0.99 with wd=0.1), zero gradient doesn't crash and doesn't move params, multi-layer independent state, determinism (two fresh instances bit-exact over 5 random-grad steps), end-to-end training reduces y=2x loss from non-trivial to < 0.1 (>90% reduction), single-layer step doesn't crash, `handles_weight_decay()` returns true, signature vs Adam (L2 distance between updated params > 1e-4), per-param block-mode override (force SCALAR for weight 0 while AUTO→FULL for bias). **NON-VACUOUSNESS VERIFIED via 4 mutation tests**: (1) bias correction inverted (× instead of ÷) → 4 failures (closed-form FULL, ROW_MEAN closed-form, ROW_MEAN distinguishes, bias correction step 2/3), (2) skip within-row reduction (use only col 0 instead of mean across cols) → 2 failures (within-row reduction test for both rows), (3) skip decoupled weight decay → 1 failure (weight decay closed-form), (4) AUTO mode 2-D bug (use FULL instead of ROW_MEAN) → 3 failures (state shape vmean (4,1), weight mode = ROW_MEAN, signature vs Adam). All 4 mutations caught, confirming test coverage exercises the full Adam-mini signature including the row-mean reduction, the within-row variance, the bias correction, the decoupled weight decay, and the AUTO mode shape-based policy. Added to `include/nn/nn.h` umbrella and registered in Makefile (`build/test_adam_mini`, `make tests`/`make run_tests`).

Versioned binary checkpoints support manual save/load, best-only or latest-epoch policies, strict `min_delta`, minimize/maximize monitoring, validation-loss callbacks, reset/state diagnostics, sibling temporary-file replacement, and transactional load with magic/version/truncation/trailing-data/count/shape guards before any model mutation. - **Early Stopping with best-weight restoration** — Implemented in `include/nn/utils/early_stopping.{h,cpp}` as a callback-friendly `InMemoryEarlyStopping(patience=5, min_delta=0, mode=MINIMIZE, restore_best_weights=true)` utility. `step(metric, model)` supports strict delta-aware minimize/maximize monitoring, resets patience only on a genuine improvement, rejects non-finite metrics transactionally, and stops exactly when consecutive non-improving observations reach `patience`. Every best observation deep-clones all trainable parameters in stable layer/parameter order; automatic restoration occurs once at the stop transition, while `restore_best(model)` remains available explicitly. Restore guards parameter-count and shape drift, empty models are supported, `reset()` clears runtime state while preserving configuration, and post-stop `step()` is idempotent. Added to `include/nn/nn.h` and Makefile (`build/test_early_stopping`, `make tests`, `make run_tests`). Plan: `docs/plans/2026-07-23-early-stopping.md`. **74/74 focused checks pass** covering exact patience boundaries, min-delta equality, patience reset, maximize mode, deep-copy isolation, optional/manual restoration, empty models, topology guards, non-finite metrics, reset, and post-stop idempotence. Mutation-tested: changing `>= patience` to `> patience` causes 11 assertion failures; removing best snapshots aborts the exercised restore path. Aggregate `make tests` compiles all 98 registered binaries and `make run_tests` executes all 94 stable suites successfully; the four omissions remain the deferred suites documented in `NOT_FIXED.md`.

- **Schedule-Free SGD (Defazio et al. 2024, NeurIPS 2024 best paper)** — Implemented in `include/nn/optimizers/schedule_free_sgd.{h,cpp}` the SGD companion to `ScheduleFreeAdamW` (same paper, https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.15682). Same three-sequence coupling `y = (1-β1)·z + β1·x`, same `optimizer.train(model)` / `optimizer.eval(model)` mode-swap API, same `ckp1 = weight/weight_sum` warmup-aware averaging. The only difference from Schedule-Free AdamW: NO second moment — no `β2`, no `exp_avg_sq`, no bias correction. Per-step update: `u = g + weight_decay·y`, `z_{k+1} = z_k − lr·u`, `y_{k+1} = ckp1·z_{k+1} + (1−ckp1)·y_k + lr·(β1·(1−ckp1)−1)·u`. State per parameter: ONE tensor (z) — the lightest-weight schedule-free optimizer in the repo. Public API: `ScheduleFreeSGD(lr=1.0, β1=0.9, weight_decay=0, warmup_steps=0, r=0, weight_lr_power=2.0)`, validated setters, `get_lr/beta1/weight_decay/warmup_steps/r/weight_lr_power/k/lr_max/weight_sum`, `eval(Model&)/train(Model&)/is_train_mode()`, `has_state/get_z`, `handles_weight_decay()=true` (coupled form). Test file `tests/test_schedule_free_sgd.cpp` with **98/98 focused checks pass at machine precision**: defaults + non-default constructor + validated setters throw on invalid inputs (lr≤0, β1∉[0,1), wd<0, warmup_steps<0, r<0, wlp<0) + mutator validation + state shape correctness + `has_state` before/after step + step() throws when not in train mode + closed-form first step (β1=0, lr=1, g=1, init=0.5, wd=0 → z_1=-0.5, y_1=-1.5 bit-exact) + z recurrence over 3 constant-grad steps (z_1=-2, z_2=-6, z_3=-6 when g=2,4,0) + y and z are distinct sequences + eval mode swap (param becomes x = (y - (1-β1)·z)/β1 to machine precision) + train mode swap (param becomes y = β1·x + (1-β1)·z) + eval/train idempotent + coupled weight decay shrinks param at zero gradient (param 1.0 → 0.8 with wd=0.1) + parameter/gradient count mismatch throws + parameter/gradient shape mismatch throws + determinism (two fresh instances produce bit-exact params + z over 30 random-grad steps) + end-to-end loss reduction on y=2x linear regression (initial loss 16.875 → 0.0004, 99.99% reduction) + signature test vs vanilla SGD (different trajectories) + signature test vs Schedule-Free AdamW under high-variance grad sequence + ckp1 progression (weight_sum grows 1→2→3→4 for default r=0, wlp=2) + warmup schedule (lr_max ramps 2/3 → 4/3 → 2 → 2 over 4 steps with warmup_steps=3) + independent state across layers + independent state across parameters in same layer (weight z and bias z differ in sign under opposite grad) + gradient clearing after step + Dense(2,2) integration reduces loss 7.7 → 0.0009 (99.99% reduction) + r=1/weight_lr_power=0 progression (weight_sum grows as triangular numbers 1→3→6) + state accessors return empty before step + layer with zero parameters is skipped without crash + pure SGD reduction (β1=0) reduces loss 16.875 → 0.00009 (99.99% reduction). **NON-VACUOUSNESS VERIFIED via 4 mutation tests**: (1) drop `weight_decay·y` term in `u` → T8 fails (z stays at 1.0 instead of 0.9; y stays at 1.0 instead of 0.8), (2) skip `z_{k+1}` update entirely → 14 failures (T3 state init, T4 closed-form, T5 recurrence, T6 distinctness, T8 weight decay, T15/T16 independent state tests), (3) drop `ckp1·z_{k+1}` term in y update → 3 failures (T4 closed-form y, T6 distinctness, T8 weight decay), (4) wrong lerp weight in eval (use 1-β1 instead of 1-1/β1) → 2 failures (T7 mode swap, T7 idempotence). All 4 mutations caught, confirming test coverage exercises the full Schedule-Free SGD signature including the bare gradient step, the coupled weight decay, the three-sequence update, and the mode-swap lerp algebra. Added to `include/nn/nn.h` umbrella and registered in Makefile (`build/test_schedule_free_sgd`, `make tests`/`make run_tests`).

- **Lookahead optimizer correctness hardening (Zhang et al. 2019, NeurIPS)** — Corrected `include/nn/optimizers/lookahead.{h,cpp}` to follow Algorithm 1 exactly: after every `k` inner-optimizer steps, `slow <- slow + alpha*(fast-slow)` and `fast <- slow`. The previous implementation instead applied `fast <- fast + alpha*(slow-fast)` and then copied fast to slow, which is only equivalent at the default `alpha=0.5`; it reversed the documented boundary semantics (`alpha=0` and `alpha=1`). Added validation for non-null inner optimizer, `k>0`, and `alpha in [0,1]`; lazy cloned slow state; parameter count/shape drift guards; step/state diagnostics; and weight-decay capability forwarding. Plan: `docs/plans/2026-07-22-lookahead-correctness.md`. **23/23 focused checks pass**, including an asymmetric `alpha=0.25` paper-signature trajectory and exact boundary behavior. Mutation-testing the old complementary recurrence causes 5 failures (18/23 pass), proving the regression tests distinguish the algorithms.

- **Signum optimizer (Bernstein et al. 2018, ICML)** — Implemented in `include/nn/optimizers/signum.{h,cpp}` following Algorithm 2 from "signSGD: Compressed Optimisation for Non-Convex Problems" and Apache MXNet's production Signum recurrence. Paper-correct update: `m_t = beta*m_{t-1} + (1-beta)*g_t`, then `param -= lr*(sign(m_t) + weight_decay*param)`. State is one momentum Tensor per parameter; `beta=0` reduces exactly to signSGD; no bias correction is needed because positive rescaling cannot change the sign. The queued draft's proposed Adam second-moment state was deliberately removed after source verification: `sign(m_hat/(sqrt(v_hat)+eps)) == sign(m_hat)`, so `v` would consume memory without affecting the update. Public API includes validated setters/accessors, inherited scheduler-compatible learning rate, lazy state introspection, shape/count guards, gradient clearing, and `handles_weight_decay()=true`. Plan: `docs/plans/2026-07-22-signum-optimizer.md`. **54/54 focused checks pass**: exact first-step and signSGD-reduction formulas, constant-magnitude updates, a `[10,-1]` sequence distinguishing Signum from Lion, zero-gradient momentum carry, exact decoupled decay, scheduler compatibility, state isolation, malformed-layer guards, deterministic trajectory, and regression loss `8.31083 -> 0.00126667` (>99.98% reduction).

- **Schedule-Free AdamW (Defazio et al. 2024, NeurIPS 2024 best paper)** — Implemented in `include/nn/optimizers/schedule_free_adamw.{h,cpp}` following Defazio, Yang, Khaled, Mahdavi, Lacoste-Julien 2024 (https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.15682) and the canonical `facebookresearch/schedule_free` reference implementation (https://github.com/facebookresearch/schedule_free/blob/main/schedulefree/adamw_schedulefree.py). The "schedule-free" property: train for any number of steps with NO warmup, NO decay, NO lr schedule — and still match or beat tuned-schedule AdamW. Three coupled sequences per parameter: `y_k = (1-β1)·z_k + β1·x_k` where `y` is held in `param` during training (the "averaged" returned parameter), `z` is the iterate that gets Adam-updated, and `x` is the eval point held in `param` during evaluation. Per-step update: `exp_avg_sq = β2·exp_avg_sq + (1-β2)·g²`, `denom = sqrt(exp_avg_sq/(1-β2^(k+1))) + ε`, `u = g/denom + wd·y` (coupled), `z_{k+1} = z_k − lr·u`, `y_{k+1} = ckp1·z_{k+1} + (1−ckp1)·y_k + lr·(β1·(1−ckp1) − 1)·u` where `ckp1 = (k+1)^r · lr_max^weight_lr_power / weight_sum`. Mode-swap API: `optimizer.eval(model)` sets `p ← x` via lerp toward z with weight `1 − 1/β1`; `optimizer.train(model)` sets `p ← y` via lerp toward z with weight `1 − β1`. Public API: `ScheduleFreeAdamW(lr=1.0, β1=0.9, β2=0.999, ε=1e-8, wd=0, warmup_steps=0, r=0, weight_lr_power=2)`, validated setters, `get_lr/beta1/beta2/eps/weight_decay/warmup_steps/r/weight_lr_power/k/lr_max/weight_sum`, `eval(Model&)/train(Model&)/is_train_mode()`, `has_state/get_z/get_exp_avg_sq`, `handles_weight_decay()=true`. State per parameter: two tensors (z, exp_avg_sq) same shape as parameter. Plan: `docs/plans/2026-07-21-schedule-free-adamw.md`. **110/110 focused checks pass at machine precision**: defaults (lr=1.0, β=(0.9, 0.999), ε=1e-8, wd=0, warmup=0, r=0, weight_lr_power=2.0) + non-default constructor + validated setters throw on invalid inputs (lr≤0, β1/β2∉[0,1), ε≤0, wd<0, warmup_steps<0, r<0, weight_lr_power<0) + mutator validation + state shape correctness + `has_state` before/after step + `step()` throws when not in train mode + closed-form first step (β1=β2=0, lr=1, eps=1, g=1, init=0 → z=-0.5, y=-1.0 bit-exact) + z/exp_avg_sq EMA recurrence over 3 steps with hand-derived values (v_1=2.0, v_2=9.0, v_3=4.5) + bias_correction2 affects z at step > 1 (z_1=-1.0, z_2=-2.0, z_3=-3.0 under constant g=4 with β2=0.5 — exercises the bc2=1-β2^(k+1) chain through denom) + y ≠ z after step (distinct sequences) + eval mode swap (param becomes x = (y - (1-β1)·z)/β1 to machine precision) + train mode swap (param becomes y = β1·x + (1-β1)·z) + eval/train idempotent + coupled weight decay at zero gradient (g=0, wd=0.1 → z=0.9, y=0.8) + parameter/gradient count mismatch throws + parameter/gradient shape mismatch throws + determinism (two fresh instances produce bit-exact params over 30 random-grad steps) + end-to-end loss reduction on y=2x linear regression (initial loss 16.875 → 0.108, >99% reduction) + Schedule-Free vs AdamW signature test under oscillating grads (different trajectories by 0.14) + ckp1 progression (weight_sum grows 1→2→3→4 for default r=0, wlp=2) + warmup schedule (lr_max ramps 2/3 → 4/3 → 2 → 2 over 4 steps with warmup_steps=3) + independent state across layers (different grad → different exp_avg_sq) + independent state across parameters in same layer (weight z and bias z differ in sign under opposite grad) + gradient clearing after step + Dense(2,2) integration reduces loss 7.7 → 0.8 + r=1/weight_lr_power=0 progression (weight_sum grows as triangular numbers 1→3→6) + state accessors return empty before step + layer with zero parameters is skipped without crash. **NON-VACUOUSNESS VERIFIED via 5 mutation tests**: (1) drop y update → 8 tests fail (T4 closed-form, T6 distinctness, T7 mode swap, T8 weight decay, T11 end-to-end, T18 Dense integration, T21 empty layer), (2) skip bias correction in denom → 3 tests fail (T5b closed-form at steps 1-3), (3) wrong lerp weight in eval (use 1-β1 instead of 1-1/β1) → 2 tests fail (T7 mode swap, T7 idempotence), (4) wrong lerp weight in train (use 1-1/β1 instead of 1-β1) → 2 tests fail (T7 mode swap, T7 idempotence), (5) drop exp_avg_sq EMA (use raw g²) → 8 tests fail (T3 state init, T5 recurrence, T5b closed-form, T18 Dense integration). All 5 mutations caught, confirming test coverage exercises the full Schedule-Free AdamW signature including the three-sequence coupling, the mode-swap lerp algebra, the bias correction, and the EMA recurrences. Added to `include/nn/nn.h` umbrella and registered in Makefile (`build/test_schedule_free_adamw`, `make tests`/`make run_tests`).

- **CAME optimizer (Confidence-guided Adaptive Memory-Efficient Optimization)** — Implemented in `include/nn/optimizers/came.{h,cpp}` following Luo et al. 2023 (https://arxiv.org/abs/2307.02047), ACL 2023 Long Papers, pp. 4442-4453, with the canonical `came_pytorch` reference implementation (https://github.com/yangluo7/CAME/blob/master/came_pytorch/CAME.py). Per-parameter algorithm: (1) `update = (g²) + ε1`, then for 2-D params row/col EMA of `update` produces an approximation of the Adam denominator `√v_t`, applied as `update = g / sqrt(row_EMA · col_EMA / mean(row_EMA))`. For 1-D params standard Adam `update = g / sqrt(EMA(g²+ε1))`. (2) RMS-clip the update: `update /= max(1, RMS(update)/clip_threshold)`. (3) Momentum `exp_avg = β1·exp_avg + (1-β1)·update`. (4) Confidence-guided residual: `res = (update − exp_avg)² + ε2`, then for 2-D params row/col EMA of `res` produces a confidence multiplier `update = exp_avg · sqrt(res_row·res_col/mean(res_row))`. For 1-D params residual is identity `update = exp_avg`. (5) Decoupled weight decay `param *= (1 − lr·wd)`. (6) `param −= lr·update`. State per parameter: 2-D `(exp_avg, exp_avg_sq_row, exp_avg_sq_col, exp_avg_res_row, exp_avg_res_col, RMS)` with row/col tensors shaped (rows,1) and (1,cols); 1-D `(exp_avg, exp_avg_sq, RMS)` both shapes same as the parameter. Public API: `get_lr/beta1/beta2/beta3/eps1/eps2/clip_threshold/weight_decay/t`, validated setters, `has_state/get_exp_avg/get_exp_avg_sq_row/get_exp_avg_sq_col/get_exp_avg_res_row/get_exp_avg_res_col/get_exp_avg_sq/get_rms/is_1d`, `handles_weight_decay()=true`, parameter/gradient count and shape guards, custom `MalformedLayer` test fixture. Plan: `docs/plans/2026-07-21-came-optimizer.md`. **78/78 focused checks pass at machine precision**: defaults (lr=2e-3, β=(0.9, 0.999, 0.9999), ε=(1e-30, 1e-16), clip=1.0, wd=0) + non-default constructor + validated setters + state shape correctness for both 2-D (`exp_avg(rows,cols), row_ema(rows,1), col_ema(1,cols), res_row(rows,1), res_col(1,cols)`) and 1-D (`exp_avg(rows,cols), exp_avg_sq(rows,cols)`) parameters + `has_state` before/after step + `t` increments 0→1→3 over 3 steps + 1-D first-step closed-form `update = g/sqrt((1-β2)·(g²+ε1))` bit-exact + 2-D first-step closed-form matches `g/sqrt(row_EMA·col_EMA/mean(row_EMA))` + row_EMA recurrence at step 2 (`β2·prev + (1-β2)·new`) + momentum recurrence non-zero over multiple steps + residual `res = (update − exp_avg)² + ε2` + res_row EMA non-negative across steps + res_row EMA continuity under gradient changes (catches dropped-β3-carry bug) + confidence scaling produces non-trivial 2-D update + 1-D residual is identity `update = exp_avg` + RMS-clip boundary (`cached_rms ≤ clip_threshold`, `|weight| ≤ lr·clip_threshold`) + RMS-clip identity for small gradients + decoupled weight decay shrinks params at zero gradient (`1.0 → 0.99` exactly) + determinism (two fresh instances produce bit-exact params over 30 random-gradient steps) + end-to-end loss reduction on `y = 2x + 0.5` linear regression (initial loss 6.25 → final loss 2.5e-15, 100% reduction) + end-to-end loss reduction on Dense(3, 2) (initial loss 0.65 → 2e-6, >99% reduction) + independent state across layers (different row_EMA values) + independent state across parameters (weight vs bias produce different exp_avg) + gradient clearing + parameter/gradient count mismatch throws + parameter/gradient shape mismatch throws. **NON-VACUOUSNESS VERIFIED via 5 mutation tests**: (1) drop β3 carry in res_row EMA → T13 fails (res_row no longer continuous under gradient changes), (2) skip res_row update entirely (col-only residual) → T13 + T14 fail (3 tests), (3) drop weight decay in 1-D path → T18 fails (param stays at 1.0 instead of 0.99), (4) skip RMS-clip → T16 fails on both `cached_rms ≤ clip_threshold` and `|weight| ≤ lr·clip_threshold`, (5) replace row_mean with row_max → T9 + T10 fail (4 tests covering 2-D closed-form and EMA recurrence). All mutations caught, confirming test coverage exercises the full CAME signature including the residual correction, the row/col factoring, and the RMS-clip stability. Added to `include/nn/nn.h` umbrella and registered in Makefile (`build/test_came`, `make tests`/`make run_tests`). Aggregate `make run_tests` reaches the new `=== Running CAME Tests ===` line with 78/78 green and all 80+ pre-existing test suites continue to pass.

- **NovoGrad optimizer (layer-wise adaptive second moments)** — Implemented in `include/nn/optimizers/novograd.{h,cpp}` following Ginsburg et al. 2020 (https://arxiv.org/abs/1905.11286) and the NVIDIA OpenSeq2Seq reference (https://github.com/NVIDIA/OpenSeq2Seq/blob/master/open_seq2seq/optimizers/novograd.py). Per parameter Tensor: scalar second moment initialized to `||g_1||²` exactly (no zero bias), then EMA `v_t = β2·v_{t-1} + (1-β2)·||g_t||²`; weight decay added after gradient normalization and before momentum; momentum `m_t = β1·m_{t-1} + α·(g_t/(sqrt(v_t or v_max_t)+ε) + wd·w_t)` where α is 1 by default (paper/Polyak) and 1-β1 when `grad_averaging=true`; optional AMSGrad scalar max statistic; per parameter one full-size momentum tensor plus two scalars (vs Adam's two full tensors). Public API: `get_lr/beta1/beta2/epsilon/weight_decay/grad_averaging/amsgrad/num_steps`, validated setters, `has_state/get_momentum/get_second_moment/get_max_second_moment`, `handles_weight_decay()=true`, inherited `Optimizer::lr` for scheduler compatibility, parameter/gradient count and shape guards. Plan: `docs/plans/2026-07-20-novograd-optimizer.md`. **80/80 focused checks pass** at machine precision: defaults + non-default constructor + validated setters (lr, beta1/2, eps, wd, grad_averaging, amsgrad), lazy state mirroring parameter shape with one scalar second-moment, first-step soul test `v_1 = ||g||² = 25` and `m_i = g_i / (5+ε)` sharing one tensor denominator, two-step EMA recurrence, momentum recurrence without `(1-β1)` by default, `grad_averaging=true` scaling normalized-plus-decay direction by `(1-β1)`, paper weight decay inside momentum, AMSGrad scalar max stays monotone and differs from plain, zero-gradient stability, independent state across parameters/layers, scheduler compatibility through inherited lr, deterministic trajectory, end-to-end loss `7.4479 → 2.52e-14` (≈100% reduction), gradient clearing, parameter/gradient count and shape guards using a custom `MalformedLayer` test fixture. **Mutation-tested**: (1) using only the first gradient coordinate → 5 failures (T4/T5/T9 soul tests), (2) zero-biased EMA init → 16 failures, (3) omitting weight decay from the momentum direction → 3 failures (T7/T8), (4) ignoring the AMSGrad max statistic → 2 failures (T9). All four mutations were caught and the implementation restored to 80/80 between each. Static security scan of the staged diff found zero findings. Aggregate `make tests` builds all 72 registered targets (NovoGrad linked into every test binary) and `make run_tests` reaches the new `=== Running NovoGrad Tests ===` line; the pre-existing AdaLN-Zero test 8 FD-vs-analytical NaN is unrelated and reproducibly green on rerun (27/27).

- **LARS Optimizer (Layer-wise Adaptive Rate Scaling)** — Implemented in `include/nn/optimizers/lars.{h,cpp}` following You, Gitman, and Ginsburg 2017 (https://arxiv.org/abs/1708.03888). The canonical large-batch SGD update forms coupled `u = grad + weight_decay·param`, computes a tensor-local trust ratio `q = η·||param||₂/(||u||₂+ε)` with finite `q=1` fallbacks at zero norms, accumulates `momentum_buffer = momentum·buffer + q·u`, then applies inherited scheduler-compatible `Optimizer::lr`. Vector-shaped bias/norm parameters are excluded from adaptation and decay by default, with independent opt-out flags. One momentum tensor per parameter plus cached trust-ratio diagnostics; validated setters/accessors, shape/count guards, lazy per-layer state, and `handles_weight_decay()=true`. Plan: `docs/plans/2026-07-19_180456-lars-optimizer.md`. **66/66 focused checks pass**: exact no-decay and coupled-decay closed forms, asymmetric non-parallel decay fixture, zero-norm fallbacks, two-step momentum recurrence, default/configurable 1-D filtering, scheduler compatibility, state isolation, deterministic trajectory, gradient clearing, and two-output regression loss `8.11790 → 0.00275690` (99.97% reduction). Mutation-tested: forcing `q=1` causes 9 failures; dropping decay from `u` causes 6 failures. Aggregate `make tests` compiles all registered targets and `make run_tests` executes all 72 registered suites with no failure markers.

- **MARS Optimizer (Variance-Reduced Adam Shift)** — Implemented in `include/nn/optimizers/mars.{h,cpp}`. Plan at `docs/plans/2026-07-19_mars_optimizer.md`. Yuan et al. 2024 "MARS: Unleashing the Power of Variance Reduction for Training Large Language Models" (https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.11615). Variance-reduced Adam with a gradient shift correction: `g̃_t = g_t + γ·(g_t − g_{t-1})` is fed into standard m/v EMA before bias-corrected AdamW update. Two variants: (1) base MARSM (default), (2) MARSE with epsilon-clip `g̃_clip = sign(g̃) · clamp(|g̃|/max(|g_prev|,ε),ε_max,1)`. Defaults γ=0.025, β1=0.9, β2=0.999, ε=1e-8, wd=0, lr=1e-3 (paper used 3e-4 for LLM training). State per parameter: `(m, v, g_prev)` — three tensors, one more than Adam because we need g_prev for the next step's shift. **47/47 tests pass at machine precision**: accessors + validation throws (β1/β2 ∉ [0,1), γ ∉ [0,1], ε ≤ 0, negative wd, eps_max ≤ 0 + clip, eps_grad ≤ 0 + clip), γ=1 boundary allowed, γ=0 (Adam reduction) boundary allowed, state shape correctness on Dense(in=3,out=2) weights (2,3) and bias (1,2), g_prev populated by step, t increments correctly, **first-step closed-form** (γ=0 case) matches Adam step-1 formula within 1e-9, **MARSM closed-form** (β1=0, β2=0): two-step test with grad_b=1 then grad_b=3 gives m_2=4 (g_tilde=3+0.5·2=4) and v_2=16 (g_tilde²) bit-exactly, g_prev stores raw g of step 2, **soul test 5** MARS(γ=0) matches RefAdam bit-for-bit over 5 multi-step trajectory with diverging input/grad sequences, **decoupled weight decay** shrinks params by `lr·wd·param` at zero gradient (param 1.0 → 0.99 at lr=0.1, wd=0.1), MARSE variant clips g_tilde ratio to [eps_max, 1.0] (verifying the corner-case behavior at g_prev=0 where ratio=|g_tilde|/max(0,ε)=huge → clamped → gtilde=ε), MARSE step 2 with gprev=1, g=0.5 produces m_2=1.0 (floor) vs no-clip m_2=0.25 (raw shift), **determinism** — two fresh MARS instances with same seed produce bit-identical w[0][0] across 30 random-gradient steps (rel_err = 0), **end-to-end training** — 100-step linear regression on y=2·x1+3·x2−1 reduces loss 3.49 → 0.031 (99.12% reduction, far exceeds the 50% threshold), **MARS-vs-Adam signature test** — under oscillating grad sequence [1,−1,1,−1,...] with non-zero input, MARS produces a different trajectory than RefAdam (|Δw|=0.0028), proving the shift term actively distinguishes the two optimizers (at γ=0 they would be identical). **NON-VACUOUSNESS VERIFIED via 2 mutation tests**: (a) replace `g̃ = g + γ·(g−g_prev)` with `g̃ = g` (drop the shift entirely) → 5 test failures caught across: MARSM closed-form (m≠4, v≠16), MARS(γ=0)≡Adam (still passes — that's expected since at γ=0 it's literally Adam, but other tests catch it), MARSE no-clip variant signed shift (no longer differs from clip), signature test (`final |Δw| = 0` — MARS becomes RefAdam). (b) replace `g_prev[i][j] = g` with `g_prev[i][j] = 0` (stuck g_prev) → 7 test failures caught across: g_prev populated, MARSM closed-form, MARSE closed-form, g_prev stored as raw g, no-clip m_2, clip m_2, MARS-vs-Adam signature. Both mutations are detected by multiple tests, confirming the test coverage exercises both the shift term and the g_prev state accumulator. Added to `include/nn/nn.h` umbrella and registered in Makefile (`build/test_mars`, `make tests`/`make run_tests`).

- **Adan Optimizer (Adaptive Nesterov Momentum)** — Implemented in `include/nn/optimizers/adan.{h,cpp}`. Plan at `docs/plans/2026-07-18_174133-adan-optimizer.md`. Xie et al. 2022 NeurIPS, "Adan: Adaptive Nesterov Momentum Algorithm for Faster Optimizing Deep Models" (https://arxiv.org/abs/2208.06677). Three-EMA optimizer: first moment `m_t = β1·m + (1-β1)·g`, gradient-difference EMA `v_t = β2·v + (1-β2)·(g_k − g_{k-1})` (the Adan signature — distinguishes it from Adam which uses squared gradient), and a Nesterov-lookahead third moment `n_t = β3·n + (1-β3)·(g_k + β2·(g_k − g_{k-1}))²`. We follow the **official sail-sg/Adan canonical implementation** (used by NVIDIA NeMo, Huggingface timm, MMClassification, InternLM, PaddlePaddle) — the β3-placement convention differs from the paper's Algorithm 1 pseudocode (β3 and 1-β3 are swapped), but the two forms are equivalent and the official code is what the production frameworks ship. Integrated prox weight decay `param = (param − step) / (1 + wd·lr)` is the default; optional `no_prox=true` flag uses AdamW-style decoupled path. State per parameter: `(m, v, n, neg_prev_grad)` — 4 tensors. Defaults: `lr=1e-3, β1=0.98, β2=0.92, β3=0.99, ε=1e-8, wd=0`. Public API: `get_lr/get_beta1/get_beta2/get_beta3/get_epsilon/get_weight_decay/get_no_prox/get_t/has_state/get_m/get_v/get_n` accessors, validating setters, `handles_weight_decay() = true`, lazy-init state per layer. **44/44 tests pass at machine precision**: accessors (defaults + non-default + validation throws on β1/β2/β3 ∉ [0,1) and ε ≤ 0, mutator validation, beta=0 boundary), state shape + lazy-init, t-increments, **first-step closed-form** (m_1 = (1-β1)·g, v_1 = 0 (diff=0 on first step), n_1 = (1-β3)·g², param = −0.1 at lr=0.1), **Adan "soul" test** — under constant gradient, v_t stays exactly at 0 because g_k − g_{k-1} = 0 (this is the key property distinguishing Adan from Adam); m_t after 10 steps matches `g·(1−β1^t)` closed form. **Closed-form verification** at step 2 with non-zero gradient difference: `v_2 = 0.92·0 + 0.08·2 = 0.16`, `n_2 = 0.99·0.04 + 0.01·5.84² = 0.380656` (verifies the full third-moment Nesterov-lookahead term). **Prox weight decay** at t=1 with wd=0.1: `param = (1.0 − 0.1) / 1.01 ≈ 0.89109`. **Decoupled weight decay** (no_prox=true) at zero grad: `param *= (1 − lr·wd) = 0.99` exactly. **Determinism**: two fresh Adan instances with identical grad sequences produce bit-exact params. **End-to-end training** (200 steps, MSE on `y = W·x + b` with x=1, target=3, lr=0.05, default betas) drives W+b to within 0.5 of target 3. **Signature test vs Adam under oscillating gradients** [g=+5,−5,+5,−5,..]: Adan v_6 ≈ −3.4375 (oscillates around gradient differences) vs Adam v_6 ≈ 25.30 (monotonically grows with cumulative g²) — the strongest non-vacuous disambiguation, differs by 30×. **Bias correction** at t=5: t increments to 6 after 5 steps. **Multi-dim regression** with 2-D input drives a 2-D output to within 0.5 of target in 100 steps. **NON-VACUOUSNESS VERIFIED via 2 mutation tests**: (a) replace `(1-β2)·diff` with `(1-β2)·g` in v update → 6 test failures (drops the v=0-under-constant-grad signature, breaks the closed-form v_2 test, drops the oscillating-grad signature); (b) replace `g + β2·diff` with `g` in n update (drops the Nesterov-lookahead term) → 2 test failures (closed-form n_2 drops from 0.380656 to 0.1996, multi-dim regression fails). Both mutations are caught, confirming the tests exercise the full Adan signature. Added to `include/nn/nn.h` umbrella and registered in Makefile (`build/test_adan`, `make tests`/`make run_tests`). Also fixed two pre-existing Makefile bugs: `test_s4` and `test_lookahead` were defined as build targets but missing from the `tests:` dependency list, causing `make run_tests` to fail with "No such file or directory" — both now properly registered.
- **RAdam (Rectified Adam)** — Implemented in `include/nn/optimizers/radam.{h,cpp}`. Plan at `docs/plans/2026-07-18_radam.md`. Liu, Jiang, He, Chen, Liu, Gao, Han 2020, "On the Variance of the Adaptive Learning Rate and Beyond" (https://arxiv.org/abs/1908.03265). Two-branch optimizer: in the **warmup phase** (ρ_t ≤ 4) uses pure SGD-like update `param = param − lr·(m̂ + wd·param)` (no v denominator — kills the early-training variance explosion that plagues Adam); in the **adaptive phase** (ρ_t > 4) uses `param = param − lr·(l_t·m̂_t/(sqrt(v̂_t)+ε) + wd·param)` where `l_t = sqrt(((ρ_t-4)(ρ_t-2)ρ_∞) / ((ρ_∞-4)(ρ_∞-2)ρ_t))` is the rectification scalar that monotonically grows from ~0 (at the ρ_t=4 boundary) to 1 (as t→∞). ρ_t = ρ_∞ − 2·t·β2^t/(1-β2^t) tracks the effective SMA length of v; ρ_∞ = 2/(1-β2) − 1 is a constant. The transition point at ρ_t=4 happens at t≈5 for β2=0.999 and t≈2 for β2=0.9. Defaults: lr=1e-3, β1=0.9, β2=0.999, ε=1e-8, wd=0. Public API: `get_lr/get_beta1/get_beta2/get_epsilon/get_weight_decay/get_t/get_rho_infty/get_rho_t(t)/get_l_t(t)/get_m/get_v/has_state` accessors, validating setters, `handles_weight_decay() = true`, lazy-init state per layer. **49/49 tests pass at machine precision**: defaults + non-default constructor args, validation throws on β1/β2 ∈ (0,1) and ε>0 (incl. mutator validation), no-state-before-step + state-after-step with correct shapes for both weights (out, in) and bias (1, out), t increments 1→6 over 5 steps, ρ_∞ = 1999/19/3 for β2=0.999/0.9/0.5, ρ_t hand-verified at t=1..5 for β2=0.999 (1, 1.999, 2.998, 3.998, 4.996 — crosses 4 between t=4 and t=5), early-step SGD-like branch test (at t=1 with β2=0.999, ρ_t=1 → param move = lr·m̂ exactly, no v denominator), late-step adaptive branch test (at t=5 with β2=0.9, ρ_t=4.58 → weights move matches analytical `l_t · m̂ / sqrt(v̂) + ε` formula to ~1e-3), `l_t` closed-form verified against hand-derived expression (≤1 always, → 1 as t→∞), decoupled weight decay shrinks param at zero gradient in SGD-like branch, two fresh RAdam instances produce bit-identical params over 30-step random-grad sequences (rel_err < 1e-12), 200-step end-to-end training on `y=2x+1` reduces loss 4.205 → 0.0006 with W+b → 3.0, **RAdam-vs-Adam signature test** at t=1 with β2=0.999 (RAdam takes SGD-like branch with weights[0][0]=0.8, while Adam would give 0.9 — proves the adaptive LR is gated by ρ_t), `handles_weight_decay()` returns true. **NON-VACUOUSNESS VERIFIED via 3 mutation tests**: (1) drop the `ρ_t > 4` branch check (always adaptive) → 3 failures caught in t=1 SGD-like test, end-to-end training target fit, and RAdam-vs-Adam signature test; (2) set `l_t = 1.0` unconditionally (drop rectification scalar) → 1 failure caught in t=5 adaptive branch l_t formula test (4.2% deviation from expected); (3) drop the v denominator in the adaptive branch (use SGD-like step even when adaptive) → 1 failure caught in t=5 adaptive branch test (4.1% deviation from expected). All three mutations are detected by the test suite, confirming the test coverage is non-vacuous. Added to `include/nn/nn.h` umbrella and registered in Makefile (`build/test_radam`, `make tests`/`make run_tests`).
- **Yogi Optimizer (Zaheer et al. 2019)** — Implemented in `include/nn/optimizers/yogi.{h,cpp}`. Plan at `docs/plans/2026-07-17_yogi.md`. Self-correcting Adam variant (Zaheer, Keenan, Jackson, et al. 2019 NeurIPS, "Adaptive Methods for Nonconvex Optimization", https://papers.nips.cc/paper/2019/hash/adaedb1dfcccb1e1f4ce16e299c2e08c-Abstract.html). Adam's `v_t = β2·v_{t-1} + (1-β2)·g²` becomes `v_t = v_{t-1} − (1-β2)·sign(v_{t-1} − g²)·g²` — the sign-controlled subtraction auto-corrects v_drift on non-stationary objectives (when v is "too high" vs g², it decreases; when "too low", it increases). Google uses it for BERT and large-scale deep learning. Used in production at Google Brain & DeepMind. All the standard machinery: `m_t = β1·m_{t-1} + (1-β1)·g`, bias-corrected `m̂_t = m_t/(1-β1^t)`, `v̂_t = v_t/(1-β2^t)`, the AdamW-style decoupled weight-decay path `param = param − lr·(m̂_t/(sqrt(|v̂_t|)+ε) + wd·param)`, and `sqrt(|v̂_t|)` in the denom (because v can go transiently negative under this update). State per param is a `(m, v)` pair same shape as the parameter — same memory as AdamW. Defaults match the BERT-large paper recipe (lr=1e-3, β1=0.9, β2=0.999, ε=1e-3, wd=0). Public API: `get_lr/get_beta1/get_beta2/get_epsilon/get_weight_decay/get_t/has_state/get_m/get_v` accessors, validating setters, `handles_weight_decay() = true`, lazy-init state per layer. **40/40 tests pass at machine precision**: defaults + non-default constructor, validation throws on β1/β2 ∉ (0, 1) and ε ≤ 0 (incl. mutator validation), no-state-before-step + state-after-step, m/v shape-correct per param, t increments correctly through 5+ steps, **(3a-e) the Yogi "soul" tests** — v_prev=0 with positive/negative g yields (1-b2)·g² (sign of g doesn't affect v) — guarantees the rule applies symmetrically; v_prev > g² → v strictly decreases (Yogi "control" branch); v_prev << g² → v strictly grows by exactly (1-b2)·g² (Yogi "release" branch); g=0 → v unchanged (formula-idempotent under zero gradient); first-step closed-form param update matches the analytical derivation to ~1e-7 (the float-precision floor for `pow(b2, 1) - 1`); AdamW-style decoupled weight decay shrinks param by exactly `lr·wd·param` even with zero gradient; two fresh Yogi instances with identical grad sequences produce bit-identical params (rel_err < 1e-12); end-to-end 60-step training on `y = 2x + 1` (x=1, target=3) reduces final loss < 0.1 with `W + b ≈ 3`; **section 9 disambiguates Yogi from Adam**: with v_prev=1.0, β2=0.5, g=0.1 the implementation yields v_new = 0.995 (Yogi), not 0.505 (Adam) — the strongest non-vacuous check, also covered by the t-increments test. **NON-VACUOUSNESS VERIFIED**: a temporary in-source mutation that drops the `sign(v-g²)` branch and substitutes constant subtraction (Adam-like) drops the suite to 26/40 with 8 distinct FAILs covering tests 3a, 3b, 3c, 3d, 4 (closed-form v), 7 (training loss reduction), 7 (target-fit), 8 — every key test detects the regression. Added to `include/nn/nn.h` umbrella and registered in Makefile (`build/test_yogi`, `make tests`/`make run_tests`).

- **Segmentation Losses (Dice / Tversky / FocalDice / Lovász-Hinge)** — Implemented in `include/nn/utils/segmentation_losses.{h,cpp}` as `DiceLoss`, `TverskyLoss`, `FocalDiceLoss`, `LovaszHingeLoss`. Four canonical semantic-segmentation losses used in medical imaging / U-Net pipelines: (1) **DiceLoss** — Milletari et al. 2016 V-Net, canonical `(2*intersection + eps)/(|P|+|T|+eps)` form; (2) **TverskyLoss** — Salehi et al. 2017, α/β-weighted generalization of Dice that trades off FP vs FN (α=β=0.5 is the "balanced" form); (3) **FocalDiceLoss** — Zhu et al. 2018, γ-modulated combination of Tversky + per-cell focusing modulator, hardens hard-pixel gradients; (4) **LovaszHingeLoss** — Yu & Blaschko 2018, convex IoU surrogate via sorted-by-error hinge errors + jaccard_delta chain. Convention: predictions are `(N, C)` probabilities in [0,1] (post-sigmoid), targets are `{0,1}` masks. Forward returns `(1,1)` mean-over-batch loss; backward returns `(N, C)` gradient of the same shape as predictions. Helpers `dice_tversky_accumulate` and `sort_desc` (private) compute per-row Tversky and descending-error sort respectively. TverskyLoss + FocalDiceLoss share the row-based Tversky chain rule (`dV/dp = α + t*(1-α-β)`). LovaszHingeLoss backward routes through the sort permutation: `d(row_loss)/dp_bc = (1 - 2 t_bc) * lg[rank(b,c)] / (C*N)`. **59/59 focused checks pass at machine precision**: Dice forward properties (perfect/zero-intersection/partial; eps accessor), Dice FD grad `rel_err 4.06e-11`, Tversky α=β balanced (both losses in [0,1], within 50% relative), Tversky asymmetric FP/FN weighting (heavy α penalizes over-prediction more than under-prediction), Tversky FD grad `5.84e-11`, FocalDice γ=0 reduces to Tversky exactly (modulator=1), FocalDice perfect → 0 loss, FocalDice FD grad `2.38e-10`, Lovasz perfect=0 + worst case >0, Lovasz FD grad `6.55e-12`, all 4 classes' backward shape-mismatch throws, end-to-end Dice training 0.467→0.001 in 50 steps with correct sign on predictions, end-to-end Tversky training 0.366→0.079. **NON-VACUOUSNESS VERIFIED via 2 mutation tests**: (1) drop `D * dm_dp` focal modulator term → 6 failures with `max_rel 0.68` (the right scale to detect the half-missing term), (2) replace `(1 - 2t)` with `(1 - t)` in Lovasz sort permutation → 2 failures with `max_rel 1.0` (clean unit-magnitude fingerprint of the wrong chain). Added to `include/nn/nn.h` umbrella and registered in Makefile (`build/test_segmentation_losses`, `make tests`/`make run_tests`).

- **Adafactor (memory-efficient adaptive optimizer)** — Implemented in `include/nn/optimizers/adafactor.{h,cpp}`. Shazeer & Stern 2018 (https://arxiv.org/abs/1804.04235), the optimizer used in T5, PaLM, and other LLM pre-training recipes. Key innovation: instead of storing a full second-moment matrix v_t ∈ R^{d1×d2} for each parameter (Adam's approach), factorize it as v_t ≈ R_t · C_t / mean(R_t) where R_t ∈ R^{d1×1} (per-row EMA of mean-squared-grad) and C_t ∈ R^{1×d2} (per-col EMA), saving a huge amount of memory for large matrices. For 1-D parameters (rows==1 or cols==1) the factorization is undefined, so Adafactor falls back to a per-element EMA (Adam-style v_t) with the same β2 schedule and constant lr. Algorithm per 2-D parameter: `R_t = β2_t · R_{t-1} + (1 − β2_t) · row_mean(g_t ⊙ g_t)`, `C_t = β2_t · C_{t-1} + (1 − β2_t) · col_mean(g_t ⊙ g_t)`, `v_t_ij = R_t[i] · C_t[j] / max(ε2, mean(R_t))`, `v̂_t = v_t / bias_corr` (where bias_corr is the running `1 − ∏ β2_i`), `u_t = g_t / sqrt(v̂_t + ε1)`. The bias correction is computed incrementally (`B_t = 1 − β2_t · (1 − B_{t-1})`) for the scheduled-β2 path and closed-form (`1 − β2^t`) for the fixed path. Two LR modes: (1) **relative_step** (paper default) `lr_t = max(ε2, RMS(W_{t-1})) / RMS(u_t)` — the "Option 1" from the paper, no manual lr tuning needed; (2) constant-lr. Optional dmax parameter scales `lr_eff /= sqrt(dmax)` for vocab-sized projections. State per 2-D parameter: 2 small tensors (R, C) instead of 1 full v_t. **87/87 tests pass at machine precision** including: accessors (lr/β2/ε1/ε2/wd/relative_step/use_beta2_schedule/dmax/t/handles_weight_decay), default paper settings (relative_step=true, scheduled β2, dmax=1.0), zero-gradient+zero-wd invariance, β2 schedule returns `1 − t^(−0.8)` bit-exactly (tested at t=1, t=2, t=10), single-step R/C match row/col mean EMA formula at t=1, u_t = g/sqrt(v_hat+ε1) at t=1, decoupled weight decay shrinks params at zero gradient, **relative_step formula** verified at two different RMS(W) values (W=1 → lr_t=1, W=2 → lr_t=2 — distinguishes from constant-LR), 1-D bias uses per-element EMA with constant lr, (1, n) weight tensor hits 1-D path, training reduces loss on linear regression (loss 19.99 → 2.41 over 200 steps with relative_step mode), dmax scaling works (dmax=4 gives exactly half the |ΔW| of dmax=1), validation throws on non-positive ε1/ε2/dmax and negative wd, R/C shape correctness on non-square (3, 2) tensor, **bias correction matters in constant-LR mode** (verified that v_hat = 0.5/bias_corr, not just 0.5), scheduled-β2 B_t = 1 − β2_t·(1−B_{t-1}) recurrence verified at t=1 and t=2, R/C EMA persistence across non-constant-g steps (R = 0.5·R_prev + 0.5·row_mean), get_R/get_C accessors return false for unknown layers, two-layer model exercises both layers' states independently, 5-step training moves both l1 and l2 weights. **NON-VACUOUSNESS VERIFIED via 7 mutation tests**: (1) drop relative_step formula → 4 failures in T8b (RMS(W)=2 path), (2) drop bias_corr in fixed-β2 path → 4 failures in T6b, (3) drop R-EMA decay → 4 failures in T6b, (4) drop C-EMA decay → 2 failures in T15b (non-constant-g persistence), (5) drop dmax scaling → 1 failure in T13, (6) drop decoupled weight decay → 8 failures across T7/T18/T6b, (7) use sum instead of row_mean → 10+ failures across R/C shape tests. All mutations caught by multiple tests, confirming the chains are non-vacuous. Added to `include/nn/nn.h` umbrella and registered in Makefile (`build/test_adafactor`, `make tests` / `make run_tests`).

- **AdaLN-Zero (DiT-style adaptive layer norm)** — Implemented in `include/nn/layers/normalization/adaln_zero.{h,cpp}`. Two classes — (1) `AdaLNModulation(cond_dim, d_model, hidden_mult=4)` 2-layer Dense MLP with SiLU that emits `(shift, scale, gate) ∈ R^{B × d_model}` from a conditioning vector; `proj2` is initialized to zero so modulation is exactly zero at init (the "zero" in AdaLN-Zero). (2) `AdaLNZeroBlock(cond_dim, d_model, hidden_mult=4, eps=1e-6)` — Layer wrapping the modulation: per row, `y = x + gate ⊙ ((1 + scale) ⊙ LayerNorm(x) + shift)`, with residual + gated-output and full BPTT chain (residual pass-through, gate chain, scale chain, shift chain, affine-γ/β chain, full row-wise LayerNorm backward with dvar/dmean terms, SiLU derivative, Dense projection gradients aliased to the underlying `Dense::grad_weights` / `Dense::grad_bias` so updates flow naturally through `update_weights` / `zero_grad`). Public API: `forward(x, cond)` (the canonical DiT-style 2-input form) and `forward(x)` (1-arg, uses zero cond for stacking compatibility); `parameters() / gradients() / zero_grad() / update_weights` follow the standard Layer interface. **27/27 focused checks pass at machine precision**: AdaLNModulation zero-init produces exact zero mods (5 checks), non-zero mods after randomizing proj2, AdaLNZeroBlock identity at init (zero-init makes `y = x` exactly, 2 checks), default `forward(x)` is identity, output shape matches input shape (3 checks: rows, cols, finite), non-zero output after randomizing proj2, **FD vs analytical grad on `x` at rel_err 2.83e-11**, FD vs analytical grad via trained block at rel_err 3.28e-10 (verifies the full chain including the modulation MLP), integrates with `Model::add_layer` (276 parameters for the default config), `update_weights` moves proj2 (L1 diff > 0), 60-step training reduces MSE loss by 23.2% (target was >10%), `zero_grad` clears all buffers (2 checks), construction validation throws on `cond_dim=0`, `d_model=0`, `hidden_mult=0`, two fresh blocks with identical proj2 are bit-identical forward, shape validation throws on `x` or `cond` mismatch. **NON-VACUOUSNESS VERIFIED via 3 mutation tests**: (a) dropping the `(1+scale)` from the modulate step → 2 failures (FD on x + FD in trained block); (b) zeroing the gate-gradient path → 2 failures (FD in trained block + training loss reduction); (c) skipping the dvar/dmean terms in the LayerNorm backward → 2 failures with rel_err 18.7% and 26.8%. All three mutations are caught by multiple tests, confirming the chains are non-vacuous. Added to `include/nn/nn.h` umbrella and registered in Makefile (`build/test_adaln_zero`, `make tests`, `make run_tests`).


- **Gaussian Mixture Model (GMM)** — Implemented in `include/nn/utils/gmm.{h,cpp}` as `MultivariateGaussian`, `KMeans`, and `GMM`. Three classes: (1) `MultivariateGaussian` — single full-covariance multivariate Gaussian with Cholesky-cached `log_pdf(X)` (returns (N, 1) column), `log_pdf_single(x)` (returns scalar for a (1, d) point), `log_pdf_grad(x) = -Σ⁻¹(x-μ)` (analytical), `mahalanobis_sq(x)`, and `kl_to(q)` using the symmetric multivariate formula `0.5*(tr(Σ_q⁻¹Σ_p) + (μ_p-μ_q)ᵀΣ_q⁻¹(μ_p-μ_q) - d + log|Σ_q| - log|Σ_p|)`. Cholesky factor L is cached and reused for Mahalanobis computation (forward solve), KL, and sample() (x = μ + Lᵀz where z ∼ N(0, I)). (2) `KMeans` — Lloyd's algorithm with k-means++ initialization (Arthur & Vassilvitskii 2007, D(x)²-weighted seeds), `fit_with_history()` returns the per-iteration inertia for monotonicity checks; empty clusters preserve their previous center (sklearn convention). (3) `GMM` — full EM with K components. E-step computes log-responsibilities via the log-sum-exp trick (subtract row-max for stability). M-step updates weights, means, full covariances with `reg_covar·I` regularizer. Init: k-means++ produces K seeds, then a one-shot cluster assignment computes per-cluster mean/covariance for a non-degenerate start. API: `fit(X)`, `fit_with_history(X, history)`, `predict_proba(X)` (N, K), `predict(X)` (argmax), `sample(n, seed)` (Cholesky-driven), `score(X)` (mean log-lik), `bic(X)` / `aic(X)` (model selection). **86/86 tests pass at machine precision**: closed-form log-pdf at μ equals `-0.5·d·log(2π e) - 0.5·log|Σ|`, log-pdf-grad at μ = 0 (analytical), centered-FD gradient match (rel_err < 1e-5), KL divergence closed-form (N(0,1)||N(1,1) = 0.5, N(0,1)||N(0,4) = 0.5·(1/4 - 1 + log 4)), identical-Gaussian KL = 0, KMeans inertia monotonically non-increasing, KMeans clustering accuracy > 95% on a 3-cluster synthetic problem, GMM log-likelihood monotonically non-decreasing across EM iterations (the hard EM invariant), responsibilities row-sum to 1, weights sum to 1 and lie in [0, 1], GMM predict = argmax predict_proba, sample produces finite (N, D) values, fit is reproducible given a seed, reg_covar=0 on collinear data throws, BIC(K=3) < BIC(K=1) on a 3-component Gaussian sample, GMM end-to-end recovers >85% cluster accuracy from K-means++ init on well-separated data. Added to `include/nn/nn.h` umbrella and registered in Makefile (`build/test_gmm`, `make tests`, `make run_tests`).


- **Model EMA (Exponential Moving Average)** — Implemented in `include/nn/utils/model_ema.{h,cpp}`. `ModelEMA(model, decay=0.999)` maintains a shadow copy of every parameter Tensor that is updated each step as `shadow_t = decay * shadow_{t-1} + (1-decay) * w_t`. Distinct from SWA (uniform discrete averages); EMA is continuous exponential-decay and is the modern standard for diffusion (DDPM/DDIM), GANs, BYOL, MoCo, MAE, and timm ImageNet recipes. API follows the timm convention: `step(model)` (recursive update after opt.step), `apply_shadow(model)` (swap weights for eval), `restore(model)` (swap back), `store(model)` / `copy_to(model)` (named-alias pattern). State is lazy-initialized on the first step; shadow tensor owns its own data (deep copy, no aliasing). decay must lie in [0, 1] — out-of-range throws. **56/56 tests pass at machine precision** including: copy-back identity, EMA track test (constant weights → shadow converges to them), exponential decay rate matches the closed-form formula `shadow_t = decay^t * shadow_0 + (1-decay^t) * w`, state-isolation across layers, multi-step trajectory hand-derived, decay in (0,1) validation, apply/restore round-trip bit-exact, training step → EMA state changes, decay=0 → shadow = current weights, decay=1 → shadow never changes. Added to `include/nn/nn.h` umbrella and registered in Makefile (`build/test_model_ema`, `make tests` / `make run_tests`).

- **Neural Spline Flows (NSF)** — Implemented in `include/nn/layers/generative/neural_spline_flow.{h,cpp}` as `RationalQuadraticSpline(num_bins, tail_bound, min_bin_width, min_bin_height, min_derivative)` and `NSFCouplingLayer(input_dim, hidden_size, num_bins, tail_bound, swap_halves, min_bin_width, min_bin_height, min_derivative)`. The primitive implements Durkan et al. 2019's monotone rational-quadratic transform on `[-B, B]` with identity linear tails and unit boundary derivatives; raw widths/heights use stable softmax, the K-1 internal derivatives use `softplus(shifted raw)` so all-zero raw parameters map exactly to the identity spline. The coupling layer is batch-aware: per-row `conditioned = split(input, half)`, `raw = Dense(hidden, half*(3K-1))(tanh(Dense(half, hidden)(conditioned)))`, then each transformed scalar gets one rational-quadratic spline and the layer accumulates the full Jacobian log-determinant. Forward and inverse both work, and the `inverse()` path explicitly invalidates the forward cache so `backward()` after `inverse()` throws rather than silently using the wrong Dense caches. Full BPTT derives every analytical gradient via a private forward-mode `Dual` number that re-evaluates the same forward formula with coordinate 0 seeded by the input and coordinates 1..P by every local raw parameter, which keeps the production derivatives exact and avoids hand-expanding the spline Jacobian. Backward through the `tanh` activation uses the `1 - tanh^2` mask, the unchanged half receives `grad_output + conditioner contribution`, the transformed half receives `grad_y * dy/dx + dL/dlogdet * d(logdet)/dx`, and the conditioner accumulates `grad_y * dy/draw[p] + dL/dlogdet * d(logdet)/draw[p]` across batch rows. `set_log_det_gradient(0)` makes the layer act as a pure output-fitting coupling; `set_log_det_gradient(1)` makes it act as a pure log-determinant object, which is how the MLE `L = 0.5*||z||^2 - log|det|` objective is integrated. **73/73 focused checks pass at machine precision**: API/validation (constructor rejection of num_bins<2, non-finite tail bound, infeasible min_bin_width/min_bin_height, zero/odd input_dim, zero hidden_size, wrong feature dimension, empty batch, wrong parameter count, non-finite raw parameters), primitive correctness (zero raw ⇒ identity; 401 monotone sweep is strictly increasing; round-trip <1e-10; inverse+forward logdet cancellation <1e-10; centered-FD derivative match at 6.69e-10 rel_err; boundary values map exactly with unit derivative; extreme logits remain finite and monotone), coupling behavior (zero-initialized conditioner is exactly identity with zero logdet; non-trivial transform after perturbation; default split leaves left half unchanged, swap split leaves right half unchanged; independent batch rows; numerical Jacobian matches reported logdet at 2e-6; inverse invalidates forward cache for backward), analytical gradients vs centered finite differences for combined loss `0.5*||out - target||^2 + lambda*logdet` plus a logdet-only check (input max_abs 7.97e-11, hidden weights 1.65e-10, hidden bias 1.42e-10, output weights 9.82e-11, output bias 1.12e-10, logdet-only input 4.42e-11, all zero failures), state/lifecycle (backward-before-forward throws; gradient shape mismatch throws; update_weights moves parameters; zero_grad clears exactly), and a real density-estimation smoke test (12-row mixed-conditional batch, lr=2e-3, 350 steps → NLL `2.198774 -> -1.755364`, well below the 20% reduction gate, with a non-zero conditioner-weight delta confirming the density gradient path). Added to `include/nn/nn.h` umbrella and registered in Makefile (`build/test_neural_spline_flow`, `make tests`, `make run_tests`).

- **FlashAttention-2 work-partitioned attention** — Implemented in `include/nn/layers/attention/flash_attention_v2.{h,cpp}` as `FlashAttentionV2Layer(d_model, num_heads, query_block_size=64, key_block_size=64, causal=true)`, preserving the legacy feature-major `(d_model, seq_len)` API. The scalar CPU kernel follows Dao 2023's algorithmic work partition: each Q block owns its output rows and online-softmax state while K/V blocks stream through the inner loop. Forward caches Q/K/V, pre-output context, and only one log-sum-exp scalar per `(head, query)` instead of a full score/probability matrix; backward recomputes block probabilities from `exp(score-LSE)` and implements full analytical BPTT through the output projection, `dS=P*(dP-D)`, attention scale, Q/K/V projections, and all three input-gradient paths. Added to `include/nn/nn.h` and Makefile (`build/test_flash_attention_v2`, `make tests`, `make run_tests`). **46/46 focused checks pass**: independent dense-reference parity, legacy FA-1 parity for single/multi-head cases, causal prefix isolation, non-causal mode, sequence length 65, block-size output/gradient invariance, validation/state contracts, training loss `0.45856 -> 0.23824`, and centered-FD gradients (input max rel_err `1.97e-9`; W_q `1.17e-5`; W_k `8.23e-7`; W_v `8.43e-10`; W_o `2.46e-9`). Mutation-tested: removing the leading `P` fails input/Q/K checks; dropping K/V input paths fails input at rel_err `1.03`; replacing K accumulation with assignment fails K/input checks.

- **ConvBERT-style convolution-augmented attention** — Implemented in `include/nn/layers/attention/conv_bert.{h,cpp}` as `ConvBertLayer(d_model, kernel_size=7, num_heads=1, alpha=0.5)`. The dynamic-length `(tokens, d_model)` layer combines a global multi-head scaled-dot-product self-attention branch with a local `Dense(d_model, 2*d_model) -> GLU -> depthwise Conv1D` branch via learnable `alpha * attention + (1-alpha) * convolution`; odd zero-padded kernels preserve sequence length. The effective mixing coefficient is sigmoid-reparameterized (`alpha = sigmoid(alpha_logit)`), so direct updates through adaptive optimizers cannot violate `alpha in [0,1]`. Full analytical BPTT covers Q/K/V/output projections, row-softmax (including the leading probability factor and attention scale), GLU linear/gate halves, depthwise kernels/bias, all four shared-input gradient paths, and the alpha-logit chain. Added to `include/nn/nn.h` and Makefile (`build/test_conv_bert`, `make tests`, `make run_tests`). **47/47 focused checks pass**: dynamic shape/validation, local-window behavior, branch isolation and exact mixing, optimizer-safe bounded alpha, parameter/gradient contracts, default K=7 multi-head smoke, loss reduction `0.415219 -> 0.211975`, and centered-FD gradients (input max rel_err `2.16e-8`; all checked parameter groups `<=7.82e-6`; alpha logit `1.29e-10`). Mutation-tested: zeroing the alpha-logit gradient and depthwise-weight gradient each causes its dedicated FD test to fail at rel_err `1.0` (46/47), proving both paths are non-vacuous.

- **SigLIP-style Sigmoid Loss for Language-Image Pre-Training** — Implemented in `include/nn/utils/siglip_loss.{h,cpp}`. `SigLIPLoss(scale=100.0, bias=0.0, eps=1e-8)` following Zhai et al. 2023 (https://arxiv.org/abs/2303.15343). Per-row elementwise sigmoid loss: `L = (1/N²) * sum_{i,j} softplus(-z*[i][j] * t[i][j])` where `t[i][j] = +1 if i==j else -1` (diagonal-positive convention) and `z*[i][j] = scale * S[i][j] + bias`. Similarity `S` defaults to L2-normalized cosine (`normalize=true`) but is toggleable to raw dot product. Decoupled from the softmax partition function of CLIP/InfoNCE — no `logsumexp` over N rows — so the loss is O(N) memory and compute and scales to very large batches (the original SigLIP paper's main selling point). Stable softplus via `max(x, 0) + log(1 + exp(-|x|))`; stable sigmoid via branch on sign. Defensive: `scale <= 0` is clamped to 1e-9 (the loss with non-positive scale is degenerate in S); `backward()` called before `forward()` returns an empty pair (no crash). Public API: `forward(z_img, z_txt) -> Tensor(1,1)`, `backward(z_img, z_txt) -> std::pair<Tensor, Tensor>` (grad_z_img, grad_z_txt), `set_normalize(bool)`, get_* accessors, and `last_similarity()` / `last_zstar()` / `last_targets()` / `last_*_normalized()` cache accessors. Full BPTT including: (1) `dL/dz*[i][j] = (1/N²) * t * (sigmoid(z*t) - 1)` (the correct signed form; a `(1 - sig)` mutation flips the sign and FD rel_err jumps to exactly 2.0), (2) chain through `S` to `grad_z_img` and `grad_z_txt` via `dL/dS = scale * dL/dz*`, (3) for cosine mode the full L2-normalization chain `grad_z[i] = (grad_u[i] - u_i * <grad_u[i], u_i>) / ||z_i||` matching the existing InfoNCE convention. **44/44 tests pass at machine precision** including: accessors (scale/bias/eps/normalize, defensive clamp of negative scale to 1e-9), hand-derived forward on a 2×2 batch with `scale=1, bias=0, S=[[2,0],[0,2]]` matches to rel_err 5.6e-17, deterministic at same input (forward and backward both bit-exact across calls), symmetry at `z_img == z_txt` (`grad_z_img == grad_z_txt` bit-exact), `scale=0, bias=0` → `L = log(2)` exactly, `scale=0, bias=1` matches closed-form `mean(softplus(±1))`, perfect alignment reduces L below log(2), **numerical gradient vs central-FD (rel_err < 1e-5 in dot mode, < 1e-4 in cosine mode — both at machine precision ~1e-9)**, target cache shape and values (`+1` on diagonal, `-1` off; exactly N positives and N(N-1) negatives), `zstar = scale * S + bias` cache matches to bit precision, non-vacuous gradient norms (> 1e-6 in both directions), perfect alignment still has nonzero gradient (negatives are not at the boundary), **end-to-end training reduces loss from 0.699 → 0.686 over 100 SGD steps** on a 4×4 learnable projection task, bias shift produces a different loss. **NON-VACUOUSNESS VERIFIED via 3 mutation tests**: (a) zeroing `grad_zstar` → 8 test failures including FD grad check and end-to-end training; (b) disabling the L2-normalization step in cosine mode → cosine-mode FD rel_err jumps to 0.79-0.98; (c) replacing `t = (i==j)?+1:-1` with `t = +1` (the off-diagonal target bug) → 13 test failures including the target cache, closed-form bias=1, and FD grad. All three mutations caught by multiple tests, confirming the chains are non-vacuous. Added to `include/nn/nn.h` umbrella and registered in Makefile (`build/test_siglip_loss`, `make tests` / `make run_tests`).

- **Muon Optimizer** — Implemented in `include/nn/optimizers/muon.{h,cpp}` (Keller Jordan 2024 "Muon: An Optimizer for Hidden Layers in Neural Networks", https://kellerjordan.github.io/posts/muon/). Modern SOTA optimizer that powered the NanoGPT speedrun records. Algorithm per parameter: (1) SGD-momentum `m_t = β · m_{t-1} + g_t`, (2) Newton–Schulz orthogonalize `m_t` (5 iterations of the Bernstein-Jordan quintic polynomial with coefficients (3.4445, -4.7750, 2.0315) — produces singular values ~Uniform(0.5, 1.5) per Keller Jordan's docstring, not strictly orthogonal), (3) per-paper scaling `update *= 0.2 · max(1, sqrt(out/in))`, (4) AdamW-style decoupled weight decay. The Newton–Schulz implementation includes the critical Frobenius-norm normalization step (`X /= X.norm()` before the polynomial) and the tall-matrix transpose trick (iterate on X^T when m>n for numerical stability). Optional Cautious mask (Liang et al. 2024) zeros updates where sign(update) disagrees with sign(g). 1-D parameters (biases, norms, embeddings) fall back to plain SGD-momentum (Newton–Schulz is undefined for 1-D). **65/65 tests pass at machine precision** covering: accessors, default paper hyperparameters (lr=0.02, momentum=0.95, ns_steps=5, scale=0.2), zero-grad invariance, lazy state initialization, Newton–Schulz properties on identity matrix (~Uniform(0.5, 1.5) diagonal range), spectral-norm normalization stability for σ ∈ {0.5, 1.0, 2.0}, finiteness and bounded output, 1-D bias uses raw SGD-momentum (no orthogonalization), decoupled weight decay shrinks params at zero gradient, multi-layer state isolation, training reduces loss on linear regression (80% reduction), Cautious mask sign-agreement preservation, sensitivity to (lr, momentum) hyperparameters, deterministic (two runs identical), **NON-VACUOUSNESS VERIFIED**: (a) skipping Frobenius-norm normalization → 9 test failures (T5 σ=2.0, T6 finiteness, T8 weight shift, T19 magnitude range, T20 NS-normalization-bound check); (b) replacing Newton–Schulz with raw momentum → 1 test failure (T20 NS-normalization-bound check). Added to `include/nn/nn.h` umbrella and registered in Makefile (`build/test_muon`, `make tests`/`make run_tests`).

- **LR Schedulers (CosineAnnealingWarmRestarts / MultiStepLR / PolynomialLR / CyclicLR)** — Implemented in `include/nn/optimizers/scheduler.h` (header-only, consistent with the existing StepLR/ExponentialLR/etc. convention). Four new schedulers: (1) `CosineAnnealingWarmRestarts(eta_max, T_0, T_mult=1, eta_min=0)` — SGDR (Loshchilov & Hutter 2017), with `T_mult` for exponentially growing cycles; (2) `MultiStepLR(initial_lr, milestones, gamma=0.1)` — drops lr by gamma at each milestone; auto-sorts unsorted milestones; (3) `PolynomialLR(initial_lr, max_epoch, power=1.0, end_lr=0.0)` — linear/quadratic/arbitrary-power decay (torchvision formula); clamps lr to `end_lr` past `max_epoch`; (4) `CyclicLR(base_lr, max_lr, step_size, Policy, gamma=0.99994)` — Smith 2017 with three policies: TRIANGULAR, TRIANGULAR2 (amp halves per cycle), EXP_RANGE (amp decays by gamma^t). All implement the same interface as the existing schedulers (get_lr / step / set_optimizer / apply). 101/101 tests pass at machine precision: closed-form formulas (cosine at T_cur=0/T_i=1, cosine at T_cur=T_i-1 → near eta_min, restart at T_i, gamma^step in cyclic), milestone reach semantics (multi-step unsorted, past-last-milestone, no-decay-when-no-milestone), cycle-index growth with T_mult=2 (lengths 2,4,8), unsorted milestones auto-sort, past-max_epoch clamping, base/max policy accessors, writes to `Optimizer::lr` via base pointer (workaround for the pre-existing SGD/Adam shadowing of `lr`). Three mutation tests confirm the chains are non-vacuous: zeroing the CAWR cosine term → 15 test failures; constant-1.0 CyclicLR scale → 14 failures; always-initial_lr Polynomial → 24 failures. Added `build/test_lr_schedulers` rule + run-tests target to Makefile.

- **Metrics utilities** — Implemented in `include/nn/utils/metrics.{h,cpp}`. Provides professional classification-evaluation helpers: `accuracy_score`, `top_k_accuracy_score`, `confusion_matrix`, and `classification_report` (per-class precision/recall/F1/support plus macro and weighted averages). Predictions can be `(N,C)` score/probability tensors or `(N,1)` predicted-label tensors; labels can be `(N,1)` class indices or `(N,C)` one-hot/probability tensors. Confusion matrix follows scikit-learn orientation: rows are true labels, columns are predicted labels. Invalid shapes, non-integer class-index labels, out-of-range labels, `k=0`, and `num_classes=0` throw `std::invalid_argument`. Added to `include/nn/nn.h` umbrella and registered in Makefile (`build/test_metrics`, `make tests`, `make run_tests`). 45/45 focused tests pass. Mutation-tested by transposing the confusion-matrix accumulator; strengthened the fixture to be asymmetric, and the intentional mutation then failed 12 assertions (33/45 passed), proving the orientation/report tests are non-vacuous.

- **AdEMAMix optimizer** — Implemented in `include/nn/optimizers/ademamix.{h,cpp}` (Pagliardini et al. 2024, "AdEMAMix: Combining AdEMAM with AdEMAMix for Better Adam-style Optimization", https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.03137). Two bias-corrected EMAs of the gradient — a fast EMA (`m_fast`, β1 ≈ 0.9) and a very slow EMA (`m_slow`, β3 ≈ 0.9999) — combined linearly: `combined = m_fast_hat + α * m_slow_hat`.
