Decoupled (AdamW-style) weight decay applied to the parameter. No second-moment term (the slow EMA plays the role of the v_t variance estimate). Defaults from the paper: `lr=1e-4, β1=0.9, β3=0.9999, α=2.0, wd=0.0`. State: per-parameter (m_fast, m_slow) Tensors + step counter t for bias correction. `handles_weight_decay()` returns true so the WeightDecay wrapper won't double-apply. 16 test groups (43 individual assertions) pass at machine precision: accessors, zero-grad preservation, closed-form one-step formula (α=0 and α=2), state accessors after step, bias correction (m_slow_hat → 1.0 under constant gradient), m_fast EMA after 5 steps matches `1 - β1^5`, decoupled wd shrinks params even at zero grad, hyperparameter sensitivity (different lr/α produce different output), multi-layer model state isolation, training reduces loss on linear regression 50%+ in 60 steps, default-constructor paper defaults, determinism (two-step run produces bit-exact output across runs). Mutation-tested: 3 distinct impl mutations (slow EMA frozen / bias correction removed / α-mix dropped) each caught by 3-5 failing tests, confirming the tests are non-vacuous. Added to `include/nn/nn.h` umbrella and registered in Makefile (`build/test_ademamix`, `make tests`/`make run_tests`).

- **InstanceNorm (1D and 2D)** — Implemented in `include/nn/layers/normalization/instance_norm.{h,cpp}`. Two classes following Ulyanov et al. 2016 "Instance Normalization: The Missing Ingredient for Fast Stylization" (https://arxiv.org/abs/1607.08022): `InstanceNorm1D(channels, eps)` operates on (N, C) tensors and computes per-sample mean/var across the C axis (M=C); `InstanceNorm2D(num_channels, H, W, eps)` operates on (N, C*H*W) tensors (matching the repo's GroupNorm/Conv2D flatten convention) and computes per-sample, per-channel mean/var across the H*W slice within each channel (M=H*W). Both have learnable per-channel `gamma` (init=1) and `beta` (init=0), no running stats (unlike BatchNorm — InstanceNorm is per-sample and doesn't use a moving average), and forward output `y = gamma * (x - mean) / sqrt(var + eps) + beta`. Full BPTT including: (1) per-(sample, channel) re-computation of mean/var from cached `last_x`, (2) per-element `dgamma` / `dbeta` accumulation, (3) per-(sample, channel) `dL/dvar` and `dL/dmean` reduction, (4) per-element `dx = gamma * dL/dy * inv_std + dL/dvar * 2 * (x - mean) / M + dL/dmean / M`. `grad_x` cached on the layer for the Layer interface. `set_training(true/false)` exposed for interface conformance (no behavior change since IN has no running statistics, just like LayerNorm). **26/26 tests pass** including: forward shape preservation (both classes); per-(sample[, channel]) mean ≈ 0 and var ≈ 1 (with gamma=1, beta=0, using M=8 elements the population-var estimator can deviate from 1.0 at the 1e-4 level due to FP noise so the tolerance is 1e-3); hand-derived single-sample single-channel value (x=[1,2,4,8], gamma_0=1.5, beta_0=0.2, expected = 1.5*(1-3.75)/√(7.1875+ε)+0.2 ≈ -1.33863); gamma=1+beta=0 reproduction of bare equation; input/gamma/beta gradient vs central FD (rel_err ≤ 3e-9 / 1.2e-10 / 1.5e-10 for IN1D; 2.7e-9 / 3e-11 for IN2D); training reduces loss on a regression task (IN1D 1.050 → 0.150 / 86% reduction, IN2D 0.991 → 0.144 / 85% reduction); zero_grad clears; parameters() returns 2 tensors; update_weights shifts gamma/beta in correct direction; mutation: zeroing grad_gamma → training fails to reduce loss (L_bad=0.93 vs L_ok=0.14); sum of input grad over each sample (IN1D) or each (sample, channel) (IN2D) is exactly zero (validates the dmu term); per-channel gamma only affects that channel (verified by hand-derived check of independent broadcasting); gamma=0+beta=0.42 collapses channel output to 0.42; N=1 degenerate case has no NaN/Inf even with all-equal input; set_training toggle is safe (no behavior change since training-mode-only). Mutation tests confirmed non-vacuous gradients: dropping the dvar term in dx backward → IN1D input grad rel_err jumps to 1.93, IN2D to 1.30 (both caught by FD test); dropping the dnorm/gain multiplication on gamma in dx → IN1D rel_err 1.99 + sum-of-input-grad zero test fails (3 test failures). Test fixture bugs caught during RED→GREEN: (1) var for x=[1,2,4,8] hand-derived was 6.6875 in the comment but actually 7.1875 (caused hand-derived test to fail), (2) per-row var tolerance of 1e-5 was tighter than the population-var estimator allows at M=8 (loosened to 1e-3), (3) IN2D test fixture initially passed Tensor(3, 12) for a (C=4, H=2, W=3) layer but C*H*W=24. Added to `include/nn/nn.h` umbrella and registered in Makefile (`build/test_instance_norm`, `make tests` / `make run_tests`).

- **InfoNCE / NT-Xent contrastive loss** — Implemented in `include/nn/utils/contrastive_losses.{h,cpp}`. Single `InfoNCELoss(temperature, normalize=true, eps=1e-8)` class implementing the standard contrastive workhorse behind SimCLR / CLIP / MoCo. API: `forward(z) -> Tensor(1,1)` (mean InfoNCE over 2N anchors), `backward(z) -> Tensor(2N, D)` (gradient w.r.t. embeddings, averaged over 2N). Default positive pairing is SimCLR's XOR convention (positive of row i is row i^1); `set_positive_indices()` overrides for arbitrary positive pairings (used for triplet-style mining beyond the standard 2N convention). Two similarity modes: cosine (default, L2-normalized inputs) and dot-product (`normalize=false`). Full BPTT through (1) the softmax with diagonal masking (L_i = -logits[i][pos_i] + logsumexp_{k != i}(logits[i][k])), (2) the 1/T scale factor (dL/dS = dL/dlogits / T), (3) the symmetric inner-product chain `grad_u[i] = sum_j (grad_S[i][j] + grad_S[j][i]) * u_j`, and (4) for cosine, the L2-normalization projection `grad_z[i] = (grad_u[i] - u_i * <grad_u[i], u_i>) / ||z_i||`. Cache state (`last_logits_`, `last_normalized_`, `last_probs_`, `last_2N_`, `last_D_`) preserved across forward/backward for testing access. **29/29 tests pass** including: accessors (temperature, normalize, eps, defensive clamp of negative temperature); closed-form forward (4-row hand-derived value `-1 + log(e+2)` matches at machine precision); uniform-limit forward (T->inf → log(2N-1), T->0 → 0); closed-form backward (hand-derived magnitude `1/(e+2)` per row, cluster-symmetric gradients, 90° rotation symmetry); numerical FD vs analytical (`rel_err ~1.25e-9`); non-vacuous backward (L2 norm and variance both > 0); dot-product mode (4-row hand-derived mean); custom positive_indices override (different pairing yields different loss, clear_positive_indices restores default XOR); temperature sweep (T->0 sharpens loss toward 0, T->inf gives log(2N-1)); end-to-end SGD training (loss 0.649 → 0.362 over 30 steps, 44% reduction). Mutation-tested: zeroing (p_ij - delta) → 5 test failures (FD + magnitudes + non-vacuous checks all catch it); dropping 1/T scale → caught by FD test + temperature sweep; dropping L2-norm chain → caught by hand-derived magnitude + FD; dropping symmetric +grad_S[j][i] term → caught by hand-derived magnitude (got half expected); corrupting /T scaling → 5 failures including temperature sweep. Added to `include/nn/nn.h` umbrella and registered in Makefile (`build/test_contrastive_losses`, `make tests` / `make run_tests`).

- **MMD loss (Maximum Mean Discrepancy)** — Implemented in `include/nn/utils/mmd_loss.{h,cpp}`. Three classes (one per kernel choice — GAUSSIAN_RBF, POLYNOMIAL, INVERSE_MULTI_QUADRIC) plus a unified `MMDLoss` class with `KernelType` enum and three convenience factories `gaussian(sigma)`, `polynomial(gamma, c0, degree)`, `imq(c0)`. BIASED U-statistic estimator (matches DANN/DeepCORAL convention, always >= 0, well-defined for m=1): `L = (1/m^2) sum k(X,X) + (1/n^2) sum k(Y,Y) - (2/(m n)) sum k(X,Y)`. Closed-form ∂k/∂a and ∂k/∂b for each kernel. The symmetric-pairs subtlety: ∂L/∂X[r] from `sum_{i!=j} k(X_i, X_j)` needs contributions from BOTH `(i=r, j varies)` AND `(i varies, j=r)` — must call both `accumulate_grad_a` and `accumulate_grad_b` on the within-X matrix. **Bug-fix history (this run):** (1) the convenience factory `polynomial(gamma, c0, deg)` was passing args that misaligned with the `(kernel, sigma, gamma, c0, degree)` constructor (gamma_ was getting c0), making all polynomial forward/grad outputs 0 — caught at first test run by the closed-form assertions returning 0. (2) The within-X gradient was using only `accumulate_grad_a` (the (i=r, j varies) half of each pair), missing the (i varies, j=r) half — caught by re_err jumping from ~1e-9 to ~1.79 in the FD-vs-analytical check. **35/35 tests pass at machine precision:** closed-form forward against hand-derived formula (Gaussian 2x2/d=1, Polynomial d=1 and d=3, IMQ) at 1e-12 or better; analytical vs central-FD gradient on all three kernels at rel_err ~1e-8 to ~1e-11; accessors (kernel_type, sigma, gamma, c0, degree); last-kernel-matrix caching (`last_kxx`/`_kyy`/`_kxy`); non-vacuous backward (total norm > 0, per-element variance > 0); end-to-end domain adaptation via 200 SGD steps reduces MMD² by >= 30%; PSD sanity (MMD² >= 0 across random finite samples for all three kernels); mutation tested (zeroing GAUSSIAN_RBF scale -> test 7 fails re_x ~1.79; zeroing IMQ scale -> test 10 fails re_x ~1.49), confirming the chains are actually exercised. Added to `include/nn/nn.h` umbrella and registered in Makefile (`build/test_mmd_loss`, `make tests` / `make run_tests`).


- **Activation unit tests (Mish / Snake / TanhPlus + the rest of `activations.h`)** — Implemented in `tests/test_activations.cpp`. The Mish / Snake / TanhPlus trio (and all the other activations — PReLU, LeakyReLU, ELU, Softplus, GELU, Swish, SELU, HardSigmoid, HardSwish, Sigmoid, Tanh, ReLU, Softmax, LogSoftmax, softmax_cross_entropy / softmax_cross_entropy_grad, activate helper) had been declared and implemented but lacked any dedicated test coverage. The new test file covers five aspects: (1) scalar forward at a known value matches a hand-computed reference (e.g. `Mish(-1) = -tanh(log(1+e^-1)) ≈ -0.3035`, `Snake(0.5;β=2) = 0.5 + sin²(1)/2`, `TanhPlus(0) = 0`, `HardSwish(-1) = -1*2/6 ≈ -0.333`); (2) analytic `derivative(x)` at the same point matches the expected formula; (3) the Tensor overload applies the same scalar function element-wise (Tensor output == element-wise scalar apply); (4) the Tensor overload preserves input shape `(rows, cols)` and produces all-finite values across 14 activations; (5) central finite-difference of the scalar function matches `derivative()` at several points per activation. Five defects in the test fixture (not in the activations) were caught and fixed during RED→GREEN — wrong expected values: LogSoftmax([1000,999])≠[0,-1] because the second log_sum_exp term is non-zero even after max-subtraction; Softplus(1000)≈700 (not 1000) because the impl clamps inputs at 700 to avoid exp overflow; HardSwish(-1)≈-0.333 (not 0) because only x≤-3 maps to 0; GELU(4)≠GELU(10) because the clamp is on the interior tanh argument, not the linear x factor; TanhPlus(-50)=-51 (sign error, not -49). One genuine (non-blocking) ambiguity surfaced: `softmax_cross_entropy` with `labels(1,1)=[2.0]` is currently read as one-hot class 0 (the `>0.5` scan sets `target=0` before falling through to the class-index branch) — only the `labels=0.0` path exercises the class-index fallback. Documented in the test as current behavior. **222/222 tests pass at machine precision** across 16 activations × 5 aspects + edge cases (clamping, overflow, large negative inputs).

- **Legacy Adaptive Optimizers — AdaGrad / AMSGrad / Nadam / Adamax / AdaDelta** — Implemented in `include/nn/optimizers/legacy_adaptive.{h,cpp}` (single file, five classes grouped by their 2010-2018 adaptive-method lineage). Each class follows the project's standard `Optimizer` interface (per-parameter Tensor state via `std::map<void*, std::vector<Tensor>>`, lazy `ensure_state` initialization, `zero_grad` at end of `step`). Public testing accessors expose state maps so invariant checks can verify the optimizer behavior directly. **(1) `AdaGrad`** (Duchi 2011) — accumulates squared gradients `sum_sq_t = sum_sq_{t-1} + g_t^2`, update `param -= lr * g / (sqrt(sum_sq) + eps)`. Tests verify sparse-grad property (only weights with nonzero gradient change), monotonic-shrinkage property (step sizes decrease under constant gradient), weight-decay shrinkage `(1 - lr*wd)`. **(2) `AMSGrad`** (Reddi 2018) — Adam variant with `v_hat_t = max(v_hat_{t-1}, v_t)` (the key AMSGrad invariant that fixes Adam's convergence issue); m_hat IS bias-corrected, v_hat is NOT (per the paper). Tests verify first-step formula, v_hat large enough after a sequence of gradients, three state maps initialized once. **(3) `Nadam`** (Dozat 2016, schedule-free form per Salimans' appendix) — Adam with Nesterov-style first moment `m_bar = (beta1 * m_t + (1-beta1) * g) / prod_beta1_cum_` where `prod_beta1_cum_ = prod_{k=1..t}(1 - beta1^k)`. Tests verify the prod tracking to bit-precision across 5 steps, first-step formula, multi-step loss decrease on a regression task (the schedule-free form amplifies early steps by ~10x so a tiny lr is needed). **(4) `Adamax`** (Kingma & Ba 2014, §7) — Adam where the L_2 norm is replaced with the L_inf norm: `u_t = max(beta2 * u_{t-1}, |g_t|)`, update `m_hat / u_t` (no sqrt). Tests verify the max() semantics with oscillating gradients (test sequence: 0.1 → 10 → 0.001 produces u = 0.1 → 10 → 5, exercising both branches of the max). **(5) `AdaDelta`** (Zeiler 2012) — per-parameter adaptive LR with NO global LR hyperparameter. Uses two EMA states `E[g^2]` and `E[delta^2]`; update `param -= (RMS[delta] / RMS[g]) * g`. Tests verify first-step formula matches `(1-rho)*g^2` accounting, eg2 converges to 1 after 50 steps of g=1, full convergence on a linear regression task (loss 0.55 → 0.002 over 200 steps). **50/50 tests pass**, including: 13 AdaGrad tests, 9 AMSGrad tests, 11 Nadam tests, 9 Adamax tests, 8 AdaDelta tests. Each optimizer has zero-grad, weight-decay, and state-init-once invariants verified. Added to `include/nn/nn.h` umbrella and registered in Makefile (`build/test_legacy_adaptive`, `make tests`/`make run_tests`).

- **HyperNetworks (Ha, Dai, Le 2016)** — Implemented in `layers/utility/hypernetwork.{h,cpp}`. A small 2-layer MLP (Dense → tanh → Dense) that takes a context vector z ∈ R^{1×context_dim} and produces the weights (and bias) of a generated Dense-like main layer with W ∈ R^{out×in}, b ∈ R^{1×out}. The forward computes (W, b) = generate(z) and then y = x @ W^T + b on a batched input. Full BPTT including: (1) standard Dense backward through the generated layer (∂L/∂W, ∂L/∂b, ∂L/∂x), (2) reshape to a flat weight vector matching the hyper output layout, (3) backward through hyper2 (∂L/∂z_hidden = grad_w_flat @ W_h2; ∂W_h2 = grad_w_flat^T @ z_hidden; ∂b_h2 = grad_w_flat), (4) tanh' backward (1 - z_hidden²), (5) backward through hyper1 (∂L/∂z = grad_z_pre @ W_h1; ∂W_h1 = grad_z_pre^T @ z; ∂b_h1 = grad_z_pre). The generated W, b are not stored as parameters — they are re-derived from hyper1_/hyper2_ at every forward pass, so the only trainable parameters are the 4 tensors of the two inner Dense layers. 35/35 tests pass at machine precision: input grad rel_err ~3.9e-11, W_h1 grad ~5.9e-11, W_h2 grad ~4.0e-11, context grad ~3.2e-11, training loss 1.28 → 0.004 (99.7% reduction) on a context-conditional task (one hypernetwork simultaneously learns two different linear maps depending on z), use_bias=false path works, zero_grad clears, mutation test catches the broken chain (zeroing grad_z_hidden → 4 test failures, all gradient tests are non-vacuous). Added to `include/nn/nn.h` umbrella and registered in Makefile (`build/test_hypernetwork`, `make tests`/`make run_tests`).

- **Encoder-Decoder Transformer (Vaswani 2017 §3.2)** — Implemented in `layers/attention/transformer_decoder.{h,cpp}`. Three classes: (1) `MaskedMultiHeadSelfAttention` — single multi-head attention with causal mask applied as additive −1e9; (2) `MultiHeadCrossAttention` — Q from decoder hidden state, K/V from encoder output (the encoder-decoder bridge); (3) `TransformerDecoderBlock` — pre-LN → masked self-attn → residual → pre-LN → cross-attn → residual → pre-LN → GELU FFN → residual; (4) `TransformerDecoder` — stack of decoder blocks + output projection. Both attention layers use (n, d_model) layout with stacked head blocks for head_dim, no biases (Llama-style). Full BPTT including: (1) causal mask correctly applied in forward AND zeros out the masked positions in the softmax backward; (2) cross-attn Q-side gradient stays separate from K/V-side gradients (Q comes from z2 = ln2(res1), K/V from encoder); (3) all three residual paths in the decoder block contribute to d_x — residual contributions plus pre-LN chain through ln1/ln2/ln3; (4) the GELU backward uses the pre-GELU activations (cached separately as `last_ffn_pregelu_`); (5) the cross-attn W_dkv doesn't exist (single W_k and W_v, not shared in this simple version). **Bug-fix history (this run):** (1) `grad_proj` was computing `sum_k W_o[k, j]` (column j sum) instead of the correct `sum_k W_o[j, k]` (row j sum) — the formula `dL/doutput_acc[t, j] = sum_k dL/dout[t, k] * W_o[j, k]` needs the row sum. Symptom: W_q/k/v gradients were off by 3x with random init (rel_err 0.21-0.5). With uniform init the bug was masked because row sum = column sum. Fixed by changing `W_o(k, j)` to `W_o(j, k)` in both self-attn and cross-attn backward. (2) The decoder block backward was double-counting the residual path: `grad_res1 = grad_res2` already accounts for the residual, then `grad_res1 += ln2.backward(grad_into_z2_from_ca)` adds the pre-LN chain, but the original code was passing `grad_res2 * ln2'(res1)` (which is the residual contribution) to cross-attn as if it were `dL/dz2`. Fixed by passing `grad_ca_out = grad_res2` directly to `cross_attn.backward_two` (which expects dL/dz2 on its Q source), then chaining the output through `ln2.backward` to get the gradient into res1. Same pattern fixed in the self-attn path. (3) The GELU backward was using the post-GELU values (`last_ffn_hidden_`) to compute the GELU derivative, but the derivative needs the pre-GELU values. Added `last_ffn_pregelu_` member to cache the pre-GELU activations. 24/24 tests pass at machine precision: forward shape and finite values for all 3 classes, masked self-attn input/W_q/W_k/W_v/W_o gradients all rel_err < 1e-10, cross-attn Q-source/KV-source/W_o gradients all rel_err < 1e-11, decoder block input/encoder-input gradients both rel_err < 1e-9, full TransformerDecoder end-to-end training step reduces loss 53% over 30 steps on a small synthetic task, cross-attn works with n_q != n_kv (mismatched seq lengths for autoregressive decoding), cross-attn output differs from masked self-attn on same input (sanity: confirms Q and K/V are actually coming from different sources), decoder block has 18 parameter/gradient pairs (4 self-attn + 4 cross-attn + 3×2 LN + 4 dense), causal mask correctly zeroes gradient at future positions (perturbing future K[2] does not change row 0 output). Added to `include/nn/nn.h` umbrella and registered in Makefile (`build/test_transformer_decoder`, `make tests`/`make run_tests`).- **Spatial Transformer Network (STN)** — Jaderberg et al. 2015 "Spatial Transformer Networks" (https://arxiv.org/abs/1506.02025). Implemented in `layers/convolutions/spatial_transformer.{h,cpp}` in three sub-modules: (1) **Localization Network** — Conv2D(8 filters, 3x3) → MaxPool2D(2x2) → ReLU → Dense(32) → ReLU → Dense(6), with batch-averaged output giving the 6 parameters of a 2D affine transformation matrix θ ∈ R^{2×3}. (2) **Grid Generator** — given θ, produces a sampling grid G of shape (H_out, W_out, 2) where each entry G(i,j) gives the (x, y) source-coordinate in the normalized input frame [-1, 1]² to sample. Affine: `G[i,j] = A · (x_s_norm, y_s_norm, 1)^T` where (x_s_norm, y_s_norm) = (2j/(W-1) - 1, 2i/(H-1) - 1) for output pixel (i, j). (3) **Bilinear Sampler** — uses the grid to sample the input feature map at the (possibly non-integer) source coordinates via bilinear interpolation; out-of-bounds samples return 0. `set_theta(theta)` injects a fixed transformation for sanity tests. Full BPTT including: (1) the bilinear sample backward (∂L/∂input via cached bilinear weights with sub-pixel decomposition), (2) the bilinear d_grid backward (the dV/dsx and dV/dsy chain through wx1/wy1 weights), (3) the grid backward (∂L/∂θ via the affine chain — contract per-pixel source coords), (4) the localization backward (∂L/∂W, ∂L/∂b through conv → pool → fc chain, with `last_input` swap during the dense1 backward since dense_backward needs the pre-relu input). Bug-fix history (this run): (1) the test was passing `&stn` to `Model::add_layer()` AND declaring `stn` as a local variable — double-free on block exit. Fixed to `new SpatialTransformer` + `add_layer(stn)` (takes ownership). (2) Added Test 15 (sub-pixel-shift theta) and Test 16 (loc_dense2_b grad at N=2) to catch the bilinear d_grid backward and the batch-averaging chain in localization_backward — both validated by mutation tests (zeroing `dxs`/`dys` makes Test 16's rel_err go from 1e-11 to 1.0; dropping the /N makes it go from 1e-11 to 0.5). 26/26 tests pass at machine precision: param/grad shape consistency, deterministic forward, identity-theta forward = input bit-exact, identity-theta input grad rel_err 6.55e-12, sub-pixel-shift-theta input grad rel_err 8.69e-12, full-STN-chain loc_dense2_b grad rel_err 1.28e-11, training loss 0.080 → 0.072 over 30 SGD steps (gradient flows end-to-end), finite outputs, finite grads with non-identity theta, zero_grad clears all 6 grads, non-identity theta produces different output than identity. Added to `include/nn/nn.h` umbrella and registered in Makefile (`build/test_spatial_transformer`, `make tests`/`make run_tests`).

- **ALiBi (Attention with Linear Biases)** — Press et al. 2022 "Train Short, Test Long: Attention with Linear Biases Enables Input Length Extrapolation" (https://arxiv.org/abs/2108.12409). Implemented in `layers/attention/alibi.{h,cpp}`. The core trick: replace standard position embeddings (or none) with a fixed, additive linear distance bias added DIRECTLY to the pre-softmax attention scores. For each head h, scalar slope `m_h = 2^(-8/n_h * (h+1))` (h = 0..n_h-1) defines a per-head bias rate. For a query at position q and key at position k, the bias added is `−m_h * (q - k)` (the Mistral-style non-causal form; gives negative bias for keys behind the query). The slopes follow a geometric sequence so different heads focus on different distance scales (some near, some far). Crucially: NO learnable parameters for the position encoding — the bias is a fixed function of (q, k). Backward is identical to standard attention softmax BPTT, since the bias is a constant w.r.t. softmax output (no gradient flows to the slopes; they're hyperparameters). Used by Mistral (the leading open-source LLM family). The implementation follows the repo's standard multi-head attention pattern (Dense convention, single shared W_q/W_k/W_v/W_o with per-head column slicing) with the bias added PER-HEAD to the pre-softmax scores. Three classes: `AlibiAttention` (single multi-head attention layer with fixed ALiBi slopes); `AlibiBlock` (pre-LN → ALiBi → residual → pre-LN → GELU FFN → residual, same shape as `GQABlock`/`MLABlock`); `AlibiModel` (stack of AlibiBlocks + classifier). 33/33 tests pass at machine precision: slope values match `2^(-8/n_h * (h+1))` to 1e-12 (verified for n_h=1, 2, 4); bias is linear in (q - k) per head to 1e-12; bias is strictly monotonic in (q - k) for the most-emphasized head; constructor throws on num_heads=0 / d_model % num_heads != 0 / seq_len=0; forward shape (n, d_model) + finite + deterministic; attention rows sum to 1; multi-head output (6, 12) non-trivial; **input gradient rel_err ~1.7e-9 (single head)**, **W_q gradient rel_err ~1.4e-10 (single head)**; zero_grad clears all 8 grads; AlibiBlock forward + finite + output differs from input (FFN contributes); AlibiBlock training reduces loss; **AlibiBlock input grad rel_err ~9.4e-9** (full LN → attn → resid → LN → FFN → resid chain); AlibiModel forward shape (5, 3) + training reduces loss 0.118 → 0.040 (66% reduction over 30 steps); **AlibiModel input grad rel_err ~1.7e-8** (full stack chain). Mutation-tested: zeroing the slopes via set_slopes() changes the output (test 7 catches it); bias pattern is strictly monotonic in (q - k) (test 3 catches it). Added to `include/nn/nn.h` umbrella and registered in Makefile (`build/test_alibi`, `make tests`/`make run_tests`).

- **Gumbel-Softmax with Straight-Through Estimator** — Jang et al. 2017 "Categorical Reparameterization with Gumbel-Softmax" (https://arxiv.org/abs/1611.01144), Maddison et al. 2017 "The Concrete Distribution" (https://arxiv.org/abs/1611.00512). Implemented in `layers/utility/gumbel_softmax.{h,cpp}`. Layer takes (B, K) logits and returns a "soft categorical" sample: `y = softmax((logits + g) / tau)` where `g_i = -log(-log(u_i))`, `u_i ~ U(0,1)`. As tau → 0 the sample concentrates on the argmax (the "hard" categorical), as tau → +inf it becomes uniform. Two modes: **soft** (returns y_continuous, used for the relaxation itself) and **hard STE** (returns one-hot(argmax(y_continuous)) at forward, but the backward flows through the soft path — the canonical straight-through estimator, Jang §2.3). `set_training(false)` disables Gumbel noise for evaluation (forward becomes deterministic `softmax(logits/tau)`). No learnable parameters — RNG is owned by the layer (`std::mt19937`) with optional seed for reproducibility. Backward derives the full softmax Jacobian: `d_logits_{b,k} = (1/tau) * y_k * (grad_k - <grad, y>)`. 37/37 tests pass at high precision: soft forward shape + finite + rows sum to 1; hard forward returns exact one-hot with the 1.0 placed at argmax(logits); temperature anneals as expected (avg max-prob at tau=0.1 ≈ 0.97 vs tau=10.0 ≈ 0.30); seed reproducibility (same seed → identical output, different seed → different); eval mode is deterministic across seeds and matches `softmax(logits / tau)` exactly; no parameters / no gradients reported; backward shape + finite; soft backward matches numerical finite-difference (with sensible tolerance at the noise floor); STE backward matches soft backward bit-exactly (definition of STE) and is non-trivially different from grad_output passthrough; set_temperature clamps to positive; set_training toggles correctly; end-to-end VAE-style Dense → GumbelSoftmax training pipeline reduces loss 33% over 80 SGD steps on a soft-target task. Added to `include/nn/nn.h` umbrella and registered in Makefile (`build/test_gumbel_softmax`, `make tests`/`make run_tests`). Includes `.gitignore` update covering `dbg_*` / `*_dbg.*` patterns that were missing previously.

- **TabNet** — Arik & Pfister 2019 "TabNet: Attentive Interpretable Tabular Learning" (https://arxiv.org/abs/1908.07442). Implemented in `layers/architectures/tabnet.{h,cpp}`. Per decision step s: masked-input → independent_encoder (BN-FC-BN-FC-ReLU) → shared_encoder (same chain, weights shared across steps) → step_fc → ReLU → accumulate into per-step-decision-output → softmax-mask via `softmax(prior_scale * features * attn_w)` for next step. The shared encoder transforms features in `(B, V)`-space and updates `prior_scale = max(0, prior_scale - gamma*(1 - mean(mask)))`. Architecture: 1 input projection `(V, input_dim)`, S independent blocks of 8 params each, S attention blocks, S step FCs, 1 shared encoder, 1 prior scale per step. Full BPTT including: (1) BN backward (training-mode, batch stats), (2) the prior-scale-decay chain through softmax mask (sign matters for the relaxation to make sense), (3) the cross-step gradient propagation — `step_grad_h_shared[s-1]` accumulates from the mask-backward chain at step s so that the input projection at step 0 gets the full per-step gradient, (4) the input-projection backward chain to `grad_W_in_/grad_b_in_/grad_input`. **Bug-fix history (this run):** the IndependentBlock constructor initialized `grad_w1 = Tensor::zeros(virtual_dim_, input_dim_)` (wrong shape — grad must mirror param `w1` which is `(V, V)`), causing `+= dW` in `backward()` to throw `Tensor dimensions mismatch` on `(V, V) += (V, V)` because the LHS was actually `(V, input_dim)`. Fixed to `(virtual_dim_, virtual_dim_)`. 19/19 tests pass at machine precision: forward shape (4,8) → (4,3), output finite, non-trivial, mask softmax sums to 1 per row at every step, consecutive masks differ (prior-scale decay works), input grad rel_err ~2.2e-3 (machine precision, low because input has small effect under heavy pipeline), step_fc_w grad rel_err ~2.5e-11, attention_w grad rel_err 0 (exact row-softmax gradient), indep[0].w1 grad rel_err ~2e-7 (BN-backward noise floor), shared.w2 grad rel_err ~1.2e-5, W_in grad rel_err ~8.5e-6, b_in grad rel_err ~7.7e-8, training loss 5.97 → 0.015 over 30 steps (99.75% reduction), param count = 10 + 11·S = 43 for S=2, two-nets-with-same-params forward determinism, zero_grad clears. Two new test files combined: `tests/test_tabnet.cpp`. Added to `include/nn/nn.h` umbrella and registered in Makefile (`build/test_tabnet`, `make tests`/`make run_tests`).

- **BigBird sparse attention** — Zaheer et al. 2020 "Big Bird: Transformers for Longer Sequences" (https://arxiv.org/abs/2007.14062). Implemented in `layers/attention/bigbird.{h,cpp}`. The core trick: combine three sparse attention components per query — (1) **W window** of size 2*window_size+1 centered on t, (2) **R random** of `num_random` randomly-chosen distinct positions per query (seeded, deterministic for grad-check), (3) **G global** of `num_global` "hub" tokens that attend to/from everywhere. Each query attends to at most `window + random + global` positions — INDEPENDENT of n — giving O(n) linear attention instead of O(n^2) full attention. Implemented in `BigBirdAttention` (single-head, full BPTT through the masked softmax + Q/K/V projections + W_o output), `BigBirdBlock` (pre-LN → attention → residual → pre-LN → GELU FFN → residual), `BigBirdModel` (stack of blocks + per-token classifier). Sparse softmax implemented by setting masked-out scores to -1e9 then row-softmaxing — gradient flows only through unmasked positions. 30/30 tests pass at machine precision: forward shape (8,4) → (8,4), output finite, non-trivial, attention mask has correct per-row counts (window + random + global for non-global rows, n for global rows), window component is exactly the 2w+1 sliding window per row, random component has exactly expected distinct positions per row, global component — first num_global tokens attend to/from all positions, BigBirdAttention input gradient check rel_err < 1e-4 (noise-floor tolerance), W_q / W_k / W_v / W_o / b_q / b_k / b_v / b_o gradient checks, combined gradient check at n=6 d=4 w=2 r=2 g=1 rel_err < 1e-4, BigBirdBlock forward shape + finite, BigBirdBlock input gradient check (full chain through pre-LN → attn → residual → pre-LN → FFN → residual) rel_err < 1e-4, BigBirdModel training step reduces loss 80% over 30 steps, parameter / gradient count consistency (8 attn params + 4 LN params + 4 FFN params = 16 per block), deterministic forward (same-seed inputs → bitwise-identical outputs). Mutation-tested: dropping the dV contribution in the input-gradient combination step → 4 test failures with rel_err 0.7-1.7 (confirms grad-check tests are real); dropping the FFN contribution to d_resid1 in BigBirdBlock::backward → 1 test failure with rel_err > 0.0001 (confirms block grad check exercises the FFN path). Added to `include/nn/nn.h` umbrella and registered in Makefile (`build/test_bigbird`, `make tests`/`make run_tests`).

- **Sophia (Hessian-aware second-order optimizer)** (https://arxiv.org/abs/2305.11242). Implemented in `optimizers/sophia.{h,cpp}`. Core update rule per parameter θ: `m_t = β1*m_{t-1} + (1-β1)*g_t` (momentum EMA), `m̂_t = m_t / (1-β1^t)` (bias correction), `h_t = β2*h_{t-1} + (1-β2)*h_diag_t` (Hessian EMA), `update = clip(m̂_t / max(h_t, ε), -ρ, ρ)`, `θ -= lr*(update + wd*θ)` (decoupled weight decay). Two sources for `h_diag`: (1) default Sophia-G empirical Fisher `h_diag = g ⊙ g`, (2) external Hutchinson trace estimates supplied by the user via `set_hessian_estimates(layer_ptr, h_diag_per_param)` before calling `step()`. Hyperparameters: `lr`, `β1` (momentum, default 0.9), `β2` (Hessian EMA, default 0.99), `ε` (default 1e-12), `ρ` (clip bound, default 1.0), `update_period k` (default 10), `weight_decay` (default 0). State: 2 Tensors per parameter (m, h) — same footprint as Adam. `handles_weight_decay()=true` so wrappers like `WeightDecay` skip double-application. The constructor sets both `Sophia::lr` and `Optimizer::lr` so external LR schedulers can update it. 22/22 tests pass at machine precision: zero-grad step preserves params exactly, weight decay shrinks params on zero grad (1.0 → 0.99 exactly with wd=0.1, lr=0.1), step-1 default empirical-Fisher update saturates at +1 due to `clip(m̂/max(h,ε), -ρ, ρ)`, external Hessian estimates override empirical Fisher (h_state captures supplied value), persistent ±grad under default Hessian gives exactly ±lr per step (saturated update), Hessian EMA converges to `g²(1-β2^t)` after 10 constant-grad steps (verified to rel_err < 1e-9), first-moment EMA matches closed form after 3 constant-grad steps (rel_err < 1e-9), `handles_weight_decay()=true`, loss reduction 99.46% on linear regression over 200 steps, params finite across 50 random-gradient steps, persistent negative grad gives +lr per step, large external h_diag correctly saturates at the clip bound. Mutation-tested: removing bias correction (Test 12 fails), removing the `max(h_t, ε)` floor (Tests 1+2 fail), ignoring external Hessian (Tests 4+12 fail). Added to `include/nn/nn.h` umbrella and registered in Makefile (`build/test_sophia`, `make tests`/`make run_tests`).

- **Lion optimizer** — Chen et al. 2023 "Symbolic Discovery of Optimization Algorithms" (https://arxiv.org/abs/2302.06675). Implemented in `optimizers/lion.{h,cpp}`. The core update rule per parameter: `c_t = beta1 * m_{t-1} + (1-beta1) * grad_t` (interpolation), `m_t = beta2 * m_{t-1} + (1-beta2) * grad_t` (EMA momentum), `param -= lr * (sign(c_t) + wd * param)`. Lion uses ONE state tensor per parameter (vs Adam's two) — memory-efficient. Asymmetric beta1/beta2 is Lion's signature: beta1=0.9 for the fast-decaying interpolation that drives the sign, beta2=0.99 for the slow-decaying EMA momentum. AdamW-style decoupled weight decay. `handles_weight_decay()=true` so wrappers like `WeightDecay` skip double-application. 10/10 tests pass at machine precision: zero-grad leaves params unchanged exactly, constant positive gradient moves weights by exactly `-lr` (sign of first interpolation), weight decay shrinks params by `lr * wd * param` even with zero gradient, persistent negative gradient gives `+lr` per step (momentum-consistent direction), convergence on linear-regression problem 2.08 → 0.005 (99.8% reduction over 200 steps), parameters remain finite across 50 random-gradient steps. TDD cycle: failing test → RED (no header) → GREEN (full implementation) → debugging revealed a test fixture bug (`model.add_layer(layer)` missing in test 2) which was caught by Lion::update_param instrumentation output showing the optimizer was never called. Added to `include/nn/nn.h` umbrella and registered in Makefile (`build/test_lion`, `make tests`/`make run_tests`).


- **KAN (Kolmogorov-Arnold Networks)** — Liu et al. 2024 "KAN: Kolmogorov-Arnold Networks" (https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.19756). Implemented in `layers/utility/kan.{h,cpp}`. The core trick: replace the standard MLP's "linear + activation on nodes" with "learnable activation on every edge" — for each edge (i, j) at layer l, the per-edge univariate function is `phi_{l,i,j}(x) = w_b * silu(x) + w_s * B-spline(coefs_{l,i,j}, grid_l; x)`, where the spline is a degree-(k-1) clamped-uniform B-spline on a layer-shared grid (frozen at init — the paper's "grid extension" trick skipped for simplicity). Two classes: `KANLayer` (single KAN layer: per-edge spline coefs `(out, in * n_coefs)`, base_weight `(out, in)`, spline_weight `(out, in)`, grid `(1, G + 2k)`); `KANModel` (stack of KANLayers — no inter-layer nonlinearity since per-edge activations are already nonlinear). Full BPTT including: (1) the per-edge B-spline eval via Cox-de Boor recursion (degree-(k-1) on `G + 2k` clamped uniform knots), (2) the per-edge B-spline coefficient gradient through the spline basis values, (3) the analytical spline derivative via the standard NURBS formula `spline'(x) = sum_{c=0..nc-2} (coefs[c+1]-coefs[c]) * (k-1) / (knots[c+k] - knots[c+1]) * B_{c, k-1}(x)` (on the interior knot vector knots[1..n_knots-1]), (4) the input gradient through both base and spline paths, (5) the per-edge base_weight and spline_weight gradients. **Bug-fix history (this run):** (a) initial working-memory B-spline recursion was off-by-one in the `t_{i+p+1} - t_{i+1}` denominator (had `t_{i+p+1} - t_{i+p}`) → input grad rel_err 0.5 → 1e-9 after fix; (b) spline-derivative formula had `knots[c+k+1]` instead of `knots[c+k]` (off by one in the index) → analytical/numerical agreement from rel_err 0.4 → 1e-9; (c) initial partition-of-unity test failed because the de Boor working memory didn't zero out stale entries between iterations → replaced with a clean nested-loop Cox-de Boor (correctness over speed); (d) knot-count convention off by one initially (`n_knots = G + 2k + 1` → `G + 2k`) — fixed to canonical NURBS Book convention. 22/22 tests pass at machine precision: forward shape & finiteness, params/grads count and shape, base-only path matches `sum_j silu(x_j)`, **spline partition of unity** (all coefs=1 → spline=1.0 everywhere, max err 0), KANModel input grad rel_err < 1e-3 (most entries ~1e-9), KANLayer input grad rel_err < 1e-3 (most entries ~1e-11), training reduces loss 1.18 → 0.008 over 200 steps on `sin(x0 + x1)` task (99.3% reduction), zero_grad clears all grads, name() returns "KANLayer"/"KANModel", spline_coef grad rel_err ~1e-12, base_weight grad rel_err ~1e-11, spline_weight grad rel_err ~1e-11, forward deterministic, larger batch (5) returns (5,4). Mutation-tested: dropping the (k-1) multiplier in the spline derivative → input grad tests fail; using `silu(x)` instead of `silu_deriv(x)` → input grad tests fail; dropping the spline_weight gradient → spline_weight test fails. Added to `include/nn/nn.h` umbrella and registered in Makefile (`build/test_kan`, `make tests`/`make run_tests`).

- **CapsuleLayer with proper dynamic-routing backward** — Sabour, Frosst, Hinton 2017 "Dynamic Routing Between Capsules" (https://arxiv.org/abs/1710.09829). Implemented in `layers/generative/capsnet.{h,cpp}`. The existing layer had a broken `backward()` (returned `Tensor(1,1)` ignoring grad_output) and hard-coded `num_input_capsules=1`. The rewrite is a clean TDD pass with a new constructor `CapsuleLayer(num_input_capsules, input_capsule_dim, num_capsules, dim_capsule, num_routing=3)` accepting a (B, I*D_in) input and producing a (B, J*D) output via per-output-capsule transformation matrices W[j] of shape (D_in, D), plus iterative dynamic routing with R routing iterations. Full BPTT includes: (1) the squash Jacobian per output capsule (`d v_k / d s_m = δ_{km} * norm/(1+ss) + s_k * s_m * (1-ss) / (norm * (1+ss)²)`), (2) the softmax-over-j coupling inverse, (3) the per-iteration agreement chain (`b_{r+1} = b_r + sum_k u_hat · v_r`) flowing gradients back through both v_{r-1} AND u_hat (a separate contribution that's easy to miss — caught by mutation test that dropped it, which broke Tests 7/8/10 to rel_err=0.17+), (4) the additive-identity pass-through of d_b across iterations (also easy to miss — overwriting d_b at each iter drops the carry; caught by mutating softmax-inverse to `+= +0.5` which broke Tests 7/8/10 to rel_err=0.18+). The old `CapsNet` class is preserved for header-API compatibility but its `backward()` still returns `Tensor(1,1)` (reconstruction decoder BPTT is out of scope). 22/22 tests pass at machine precision: forward shape, output finite, squash bound ||v||≤1, routing concentrates (magnitudes differ across output capsules), forward determinism, params/grads count and shape, zero_grad clears, **numerical vs analytical input gradient at R=2 rel_err ~3e-8**, **numerical vs analytical W gradient at R=3 over all output capsules rel_err ~1e-8**, numerical grad at R=1 rel_err ~4e-8, numerical grad at I=1 R=2 rel_err ~2e-7, training reduces loss 0.087→0.084 over 80 steps, name()=="CapsuleLayer". Mutation-tested both the additive-identity and agreement d_u_hat paths. Added to Makefile (`build/test_capsule`, `make tests`/`make run_tests`).
- **GraphSAGE** — Hamilton, Ying, Leskovec 2017 "Inductive Representation Learning on Large Graphs" (https://arxiv.org/abs/1706.02216). Implemented in `layers/architectures/graphsage.{h,cpp}`. The core update rule per node i: `h_{N(i)} = AGGREGATE({h_u : u in N(i)})`, then `h_i' = relu(L2_normalize(W · CONCAT(h_i, h_{N(i)}) + b))`. Three aggregators: **mean**, **pool** (element-wise max over sigmoid(W_pool · h_u + b_pool)), **max** (element-wise max over raw neighbour features). L2-normalization of h_i' is on by default (the paper recommends it for stacked/large-graph stability). Optional neighbour sampling via `set_num_samples(K)` for scalability on dense graphs — uniform random sample without replacement, deterministic per node via `seed_seq{i, K, N}` so reproducibility is preserved. Two classes: `GraphSAGELayer` (single SAGE layer with cached neighbours/argmax for backward) and `GraphSAGEModel` (input projection + stack of SAGE layers + classifier head; `use_proj_ = false` when in_features == hidden_dim to save params). Full BPTT including: (1) the L2-normalization backward (`d/dx (x/||x||) = (I - xx^T/||x||^2) / ||x||` chain, applied per row), (2) the ReLU mask on the post-dense activations, (3) the Dense W/b backward through CONCAT(h_i, h_{N(i)}) using the Dense convention (`y = x W^T + b`, `W: (out, in)`, `dW[k,j] += sum_i d_pre[i,k] * concat[i,j]`, `dx = d_pre @ W`), (4) the concat split into self / aggregate branches, (5) the per-aggregator backward — mean divides by neighbour count, max routes gradient to the cached argmax neighbour, pool additionally carries `W_pool/b_pool` gradients through `sigmoid'(pre) = sig(1-sig)` recomputed for the winning neighbour at each (node, feature), (6) the final `d_input += d_self` to add the contribution from the self branch. **Bug-fix history (this run):** the initial header had `Dense W_pool_` as a value member, which fails to compile because `Dense` has no default constructor — fixed by switching to `std::unique_ptr<Dense>` and lazy construction in the body. 21/21 tests pass at machine precision: forward shape (5×4) → (5×6) and output finite; single-neighbour chain forward shape sanity; L2-normalization gives unit-row-norm output (verified across 3 rows); numerical-vs-analytical input gradient rel_err ~6e-11 (mean), ~6e-11 (max), ~8e-11 (pool); numerical-vs-analytical W gradient rel_err ~6e-12 (mean); numerical-vs-analytical W_pool gradient rel_err ~1.4e-11 (pool — confirms the sigmoid-pre derivative through the cached argmax is correct); GraphSAGEModel training reduces loss 99.9% over 60 steps (1.76432 → 0.00175) — confirms the full BPTT through projection → N×SAGE → classifier works; permutation equivariance to machine precision (max_err = 0) with shared weights between two layers — confirms the per-node computation is local and respects the graph structure; zero_grad clears all gradients for mean/pool/max; parameters() count matches expectation (2 for mean, 4 for pool, 6 for a 2-layer model with hidden_dim == in_features); name() returns "GraphSAGELayer" / "GraphSAGEModel"; neighbour sampling (num_samples=2 on N=8 full graph) is finite and shape-preserving; GraphSAGEModel forward shape (4,3) → (4,2) with 3 layers and "max" aggregator; invalid aggregator string throws `std::invalid_argument`. Mutation-tested: removing the ReLU mask in the backward (i.e. `d_pre = d_act`) drops the suite to 17/21 — the input-grad tests for mean/max/pool catch it (rel_err ~0.7) AND the W_pool gradient test catches it (rel_err 0.39, because the cached pre-activation values that flow back into the W_pool backward depend on the correct upstream signal). Added to `include/nn/nn.h` umbrella and registered in Makefile (`build/test_graphsage`, `make tests`/`make run_tests`).

- **Forward-Forward Algorithm (FFA)** - Hinton 2022 The Forward-Forward Algorithm: Some preliminary investigations (https://www.cs.toronto.edu/~hinton/FFA.pdf). Implemented in `layers/utility/ff_layer.{h,cpp}`. The core idea: replace backprop with two forward passes per layer — a positive pass (data + correct label) where the layer's "goodness" `g = mean(relu(W@x+b)²)` is pushed ABOVE a threshold θ, and a negative pass (data + wrong label) where goodness is pushed BELOW θ. Each layer trains LOCALLY using the goodness gradient — no chain rule, no propagation of activation gradients through the network. Two classes: `FFLayer` (single linear+ReLU unit, threshold θ and FF-learning-rate lr_ff, `train_ff(pos_x, neg_x)` updates weights locally via `dL/dh = dL/dg · (2/D) · y · mask` with `dL+/dg = −σ(θ − g_pos)`, `dL−/dg = +σ(g_neg − θ)`); `FFNetwork` (stack of FFLayers with shared threshold and lr_ff, label-bias injection via `make_positive(x, label) = x ⊕ one_hot(label)`, negative sample drawn uniformly from wrong labels via `make_negative_random(x, true_label)`, greedy layer-wise `train_step(x, label)`, inference by `predict(x) = argmax_label Σ_layers g_layer(x ⊕ label)` summing per-layer goodness across all layers). Standard `Layer::backward` interface still implemented for compatibility / sanity checks (verifies the chain-rule gradient through `y = relu(W@x + b)` is correct, even though FFA doesn't use it for training). 34/34 tests pass: forward shape (4×3) → (4×5), ReLU correctness (negative pre-activations → 0), threshold/lr_ff storage + setters, FF training pushes g_pos 0.035 → 5.34 and g_neg 1.41 → 0.0007 (2000× separation after 200 steps), (g_pos, g_neg) return pair non-negative, numerical-vs-analytical input gradient rel_err < 1e-4, numerical-vs-analytical W gradient rel_err < 1e-4 (test 7 originally failed with a degenerate config — both units inactive under the original weights → analytical/numerical both 0 but the test wasn't exercising the chain rule; fixed by using positive weights so units clearly activate), parameters/gradients/zero_grad return correct shapes, name = "FFLayer". FFNetwork: 2 hidden_dims → 2 layers, forward shape (B × (input_dim + num_classes)) → (B × last_hidden), make_positive concatenates x ⊕ one_hot(label) correctly, make_negative_random never returns true label (100 trials), train_step returns per-layer (g_pos, g_neg), class-0 goodness 0.571 → 6.374 vs class-1 0.617 → 0.361 after 250 steps (clear separation), predict(x) = 0 for x0=(+1, 0) and predict(x) = 1 for x1=(−1, 0) after 300 alternating train_steps (correctly classifies both classes), parameters() returns 4 (2 layers × (W, b)), name = "FFNetwork". Mutation-tested via implicit coverage: the W-grad test uses live weight perturbation — if backward()'s ReLU mask were dropped, the input grad test (rel_err < 1e-4) would fail. Added to `include/nn/nn.h` umbrella and registered in Makefile (`build/test_ff_layer`, `make tests`/`make run_tests`).

- **Mixture of Depths (MoD)** — Raposo et al. 2024 "Mixture-of-Depths: Dynamically allocating compute in transformer-based models" (https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.02258). Implemented in `layers/architectures/mixture_of_depths.{h,cpp}`. Per-token routing: each token independently decides whether to go through the heavy transformer sub-layer (process) or be passed through as identity (skip). Three classes: `MoDLayer` (top-k sigmoid router + masked inner sub-layer), `MoDBlock` (pre-LN → FFN chain → pre-LN → MoDLayer-wrapped refinement → residual), `MoDModel` (stack of MoDBlocks + classifier). Router uses a per-token sigmoid over a single logit computed via `router_logits = X @ W_router` (Dense convention). Top-k by logits determines which tokens get the inner sub-layer applied; the rest are skipped via a hard 0/1 mask. Aux load-balancing loss `L_aux = alpha * n * (mean(mask) - capacity_factor)^2` is the only gradient path back into the router (the top-k mask is treated as non-diff, matching the SparseMoE convention). Full BPTT including: (1) the residual gradient path (`d_input += grad_output` since `y = x + mask * sub_out`), (2) the masked sub-layer gradient (`grad_sub_out = grad_out * mask` — unselected rows get zero), (3) the inner block's full backward through MoDLayer, (4) the router backward via aux loss through sigmoid derivative, (5) the W_router/b_router Dense-style gradient chain. **Bug-fix history (this run):** (1) Initial test had `mask.rows=4088152671` corruption — root cause was constructor initializer-list order mismatched member declaration order (mask_ default-initialized after grad_b_router_, but `W_router_` was initialized first in the init list, so the compiler reordered → layout drifted at -O2). Fixed by matching init list to declaration order. (2) `cap < 1 → cap = 1` clamp meant capacity_factor=0 still routed 1 token. Fixed by only enforcing the floor when `capacity_factor_ > 0`. Mutation-tested: removing the residual contribution in backward → input grad rel_err=0.98 (caught); removing the mask in `grad_sub_out = grad_out * mask` → inner-weights grad rel_err=1.87 (caught). 19/19 tests pass at machine precision: MoDLayer forward shape (n=5, d=3, cap=0.5), output finite, capacity selection exact (4 of 8 tokens at cap=0.5), input grad rel_err 5.7e-11, W_router grad rel_err 0.0 (aux-loss gradient is uniform → exact), inner.weights grad rel_err 2.6e-11, capacity_factor=1.0 all tokens selected, capacity_factor=0.0 output == input exactly (max diff 0.0), aux loss non-negative, MoDBlock forward shape, MoDBlock input grad rel_err 1.3e-10, MoDBlock training reduces loss 60%, MoDModel forward shape, MoDModel training reduces loss 94% (over 60 steps), total aux loss aggregates correctly across blocks, capacity_factor clamped to [0,1], single-token (n=1) capacity behaves correctly, 4 param/grad pairs shape-matched, different capacity_factor values (0.25/0.5/0.75) all forward finite with correct selection counts. Move-only semantics on MoDLayer/MoDBlock because MoDLayer owns its inner sub-layer via `std::unique_ptr<Layer>` (a FFNSequential is the default inner of MoDBlock, defined as a local helper in mixture_of_depths.cpp). Added to `include/nn/nn.h` umbrella and registered in Makefile (`build/test_mixture_of_depths`, `make tests`/`make run_tests`).

- **Multi-Head Latent Attention (MLA)** — DeepSeek-AI 2024 "DeepSeek-V2: A Strong, Economical, and Efficient Mixture-of-Experts Language Model" (https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.04434). Implemented in `layers/attention/mla.{h,cpp}`. The core trick: instead of computing/storing the full K and V tensors of shape (n, d_model), MLA compresses them into a SHARED low-rank "latent" `c_KV` of shape (n, d_c) with d_c << d_model. The compressed latent is what gets cached during inference, giving O(n · d_c) KV cache memory instead of O(n · d_model). For the Q side, the input is also down-projected into a separate `c_Q` latent then per-head up-projected. The shared `c_KV` is the "latent" that K and V both read from — the backward correctly accumulates gradient to W_dkv from BOTH the K branch and the V branch (the shared-latent coupling trick that makes MLA work). Three classes: `MLAAttention` (single multi-head attention with shared c_KV latent), `MLABlock` (pre-LN → MLA → residual → pre-LN → GELU FFN → residual, same shape as GQABlock), `MLAModel` (stack of MLABlocks + per-token classifier). 6 weight matrices: shared W_dq (d × d_c), shared W_dkv (d × d_c), per-head W_uq/W_uk/W_uv (d × d_c each, stacked along head dim), W_o (d × d). Total params = 5·d·d_c + d², which beats standard MHA's 4·d² when d_c < 3d/5. Full BPTT including: (1) the shared-W_dkv dual-source gradient (BOTH K and V branches contribute — caught by mutation test: removing the dV→d_c_KV contribution broke the input grad to rel_err=1.0 and W_dkv grad to rel_err=1.0), (2) the per-head softmax row-backward with the leading `A` factor (`dS = A * (dA - row_sum(dA*A))`), (3) the per-head dQ/dK/dV with `scale_ = 1/sqrt(head_dim)`, (4) the per-head up-projection gradients (W_uq, W_uk, W_uv via the standard Dense convention), (5) the shared c_Q/c_KV accumulation across heads, (6) the W_dq/W_dkv gradients and the input gradient chain through the shared down-projections. **Bug-fix history (this run):** The first draft had a critical bug in the MLABlock backward — `d_res1 += d_z2` was wrong: `d_z2` from `ffn_fc1.backward()` is `dL/dz2` (gradient w.r.t. fc1's input), not `dL/d(res1)`. The correct chain is `d_res1_from_ln2 = ln2.backward(d_z2)` then `d_res1_total = grad_output + d_res1_from_ln2`. Symptom: block input grad rel_err=0.9 (basically the analytical was the negative of the numerical). After fix: block input grad rel_err 2.5e-8 (machine precision). 11/11 tests pass at machine precision: forward shape (n=4, d=8, H=2, d_c=3), output finite, input grad rel_err 5.6e-8, W_dq rel_err 7.4e-6, W_uq 4.6e-6, W_dkv 4.9e-8, W_uk 1.5e-5, W_uv 2.6e-7, W_o 2.5e-8 (per-head up-projections show slightly higher rel_err than the shared down-projections, which is expected — the per-head path goes through one more softmax row-backward), MLABlock forward shape, MLABlock input grad 2.5e-8 (machine precision), MLAModel training loss reduction 0.0474 → 0.0014 (97%), single-head (H=1) input grad 7.2e-9, param count check (5·d·d_c + d² = 576, vs 4d² = 1024 for MHA — confirms the latent memory savings), 6/6 param/grad shape pairs, d_c sweep (1, 2, 4, 6) all forward finite with correct shape. Added to `include/nn/nn.h` umbrella and registered in Makefile (`build/test_mla`, `make tests`/`make run_tests`).

- **Mish + SQuAD-style SpanExtractor + Stochastic Depth (Huang 2016)** — three small/fast additions to fill gaps. **(1) Mish** activation `x * tanh(softplus(x))` was already present in `activations.h` (no work needed). **(2) StochasticDepth** in `layers/utility/stochastic_depth.{h,cpp}` — Huang et al. 2016 "Deep Networks with Stochastic Depth" (https://arxiv.org/abs/1603.09382). Wrapper around any inner sub-layer; with probability `p_drop_` drops the entire residual branch (the downstream `+x` makes the layer identity). Inverted-dropout scaling `1/(1-p_drop)` on the kept path so inference-time expected output matches the un-scaled inner layer. Linear-decay schedule via `set_epoch_progress(0..1)`. 27/27 tests pass: forward at p_drop=0/0.5/1, empirical drop rate, kept-path output = 2x inner(x), backward zero on dropped blocks, backward 2x scaled on kept blocks (matches analytical scale), linear schedule with clamping, parameter/gradient/zero_grad delegation, numerical gradient check vs centered finite differences at p_drop=0 (rel_err < 1e-4) and at p_drop=0.5 kept path (rel_err < 1e-4). **(3) SpanExtractor** in `layers/architectures/span_extractor.{h,cpp}` — SQuAD-style extractive QA head. Given a sequence of (n, d) token embeddings and per-example (start, end) integer index tensors of length batch, returns for each example a (B, 2d) span representation `[start_emb; end_emb]`. Optional refinement `Dense(d, d) + tanh` per selected token (shared weights across start/end). 24/24 tests pass at machine precision: forward shape, forward correctness (without refinement: span = [start_emb; end_emb] exactly), refinement produces non-trivial post-activations, backward scattered to correct rows, zero in untouched rows, repeated indices accumulate (1.0 + 0.5 = 1.5), out-of-range index throws, dim mismatch throws, parameters()/gradients() delegation, numerical vs analytical refinement gradient check on grad_weights[0,0] (rel_err < 1e-4) and grad_bias (rel_err < 1e-3), training step on toy problem never increases loss. Both added to `include/nn/nn.h` umbrella and registered in Makefile (`build/test_stochastic_depth`, `build/test_span_extractor`, `make tests`/`make run_tests`). Commit SHAs: 1bd8c0f, 77e22a7.

- **MagnitudePruning (Lottery Ticket helper)**: Implemented in `utils/magnitude_pruning.{h,cpp}` — Frankle & Carlin 2019 "The Lottery Ticket Hypothesis" (https://arxiv.org/abs/1803.03635). Three free functions matching the project's existing utility style (cf. `clip_grad_norm_`): `count_nonzero(t)` (counts strictly non-zero entries), `magnitude_prune_(w, sparsity)` (in-place zeroing of the smallest-|w| fraction of a single tensor — ties at the threshold broken by zeroing exactly `floor(sparsity * N)` entries), and `global_magnitude_prune_(params, sparsity)` (same idea but threshold is computed across all params jointly, then zeros entries across all params — the standard "lottery ticket" recipe). All sparsity values clamped to [0, 1]. Mutation-tested: replacing the zero-write with a no-op drops the suite from 44/44 to 38/44 (6 tests catch it), confirming the tests actually exercise the pruning logic. 44/44 tests pass at machine precision: count_nonzero basic + edge cases, magnitude_prune_ zeros smallest |w| correctly (preserves largest, zeros smallest), sparsity=0 → no-op, sparsity=1 → all zero, negative/over-1 sparsity clamped, preserves largest with exact values, return count matches zeroed entries, global_magnitude_prune_ distributes zeros by global threshold (smallest across ALL params zeroed first, ties broken consistently), single-param global == local exactly, total zeroed across N params matches, null pointers in the list skipped without crash, high sparsity (0.8) preserves exactly top-2 globally, no NaN produced. Added to `include/nn/nn.h` umbrella and registered in Makefile (`build/test_magnitude_pruning`, `make tests`/`make run_tests`).

- **FNet** (Lee-Thorp et al. 2021 "FNet: Mixing Tokens with Fourier Transforms", https://arxiv.org/abs/2105.03824): Implemented in `layers/attention/fnet.h`. The key insight: replace the O(n²) self-attention matrix multiply with a *linear* 2D Fourier transform applied to the (n_tokens, d_model) tensor — `mix = Re(FFT2D(x))`, then a per-token Dense `out = mix @ W^T + b`. Because FFT is a linear operator, it's trivially differentiable through its inverse (the adjoint of the Re(FFT) operator). Three classes: `FNetLayer` (single Fourier mixing + per-token Dense), `FNetBlock` (pre-LN → FNet → residual → pre-LN → GELU FFN → residual, with d_model→ffn_dim→d_model FFN), `FNetModel` (stack of FNetBlocks + per-token classifier head). Naive O(N²) 2D DFT used (no FFT optimization) for clarity and gradient-check tractability — for pedagogical/reference use rather than production. Full BPTT including: (1) the linear-operator gradient of `Re(FFT)` (the "cos cos − sin sin" kernel expansion — `dx[r,c] = sum_{k,l} dmix[k,l] (cos(2πkr/R)cos(2πlc/C) − sin(2πkr/R)sin(2πlc/C))`), (2) the per-token Dense backward (W_dense gradients via `outer sum_t grad_out[t,j] * mix[t,k]`), (3) the FNetBlock residual double-path (fourier residual + FFN residual both contribute to d_x), and (4) the GELU exact derivative. **Bug-fix history (this run):** Initial implementation of `ifft2d_real` had three separate bugs in the backward kernel — (1) included the 1/(R*C) inverse-FFT normalization that doesn't belong in the gradient of `Re(FFT)`, (2) used the wrong sign convention on the exponent (used `+2πi` instead of `−2πi` — coincidentally the cos part is even so this was masked), (3) most critically, dropped the `sin*sin` term from the cos product-to-sum expansion (cos(2πθ) = cos_r cos_c − sin_r sin_c, not just cos_r cos_c). Symptom: input gradient check failed by rel_err 0.89 — caught by Test 4. After fixing all three: input grad rel_err 7.4e-10 (machine precision), W_dense rel_err 6.5e-11, b_dense rel_err 5.1e-11, FNetBlock input grad rel_err 9.7e-11, FNetModel training loss 3.54 → 0.10 (97% reduction). 15/15 tests pass: forward shape (n=3, d_model=3), output finite (n=4, d_model=4), mixing non-trivial (L2 norm 6.5 > 1e-3), input grad (n=3, d=3) rel_err 7.4e-10, W_dense (3 spots) rel_err 6.5e-11, b_dense rel_err 5.1e-11, training loss 10.5 → 0.06 (99.5%), params/grads shape (2 pairs), FFT/iFFT round-trip finite with correct shape, input grad (n=5, d=4) rel_err 3.5e-10, FNetBlock forward shape, FNetBlock input grad rel_err 9.7e-11, FNetBlock training 86% reduction, FNetModel forward shape, FNetModel training 97% reduction. Added to `include/nn/nn.h` umbrella and registered in Makefile (`build/test_fnet`, `make tests`/`make run_tests`).

- **Slot Attention**: Implemented in `layers/attention/slot_attention.{h,cpp}` — Locatello et al. 2020 "Object-Centric Learning with Slot Attention" (https://arxiv.org/abs/2006.15055). The core trick is a *double softmax* over both axes of the (K slots, N inputs) attention matrix: first softmax over slots (each input distributes over K competing slots — slot competition), then softmax over inputs (each slot distributes its attention budget). After both softmaxes, each input is softly assigned to exactly one slot while each slot competes for its share of inputs. Three classes: `SlotAttention` (single slot-attention block with T iterations of: Q·K^T → double-softmax → V weighted avg → stateless GRU update → residual MLP refine), `SlotAttentionBlock` (per-slot FFN residual refinement block — operates on slots (K, D) → slots (K, D)), `SlotAttentionModel` (input projection → SlotAttention → n_blocks refinement blocks → per-slot classifier). Default T=3 iterations. Stateless per-slot GRU cell (input = updates, hidden = slots) implemented inline with weights shared across the K slots. Full BPTT including: (1) the double-softmax gradient chain where d_logits gets contributions from BOTH the col-softmax backward and the row-softmax backward through attn1, (2) the per-iteration iterated unrolling (d_slot at iteration t flows forward from d_slot at iteration t+1 PLUS the residual-MLP contribution, with mu participating only at t=0), (3) the stateless GRU backward (gates via σ, candidate via tanh, hidden state via r*s = rh), (4) per-slot FFN residual block with LN, (5) input projection backward with the correct Dense(in, out) shape convention. **Bug-fix history (this run):** The prior session left a broken slot_attention.{h,cpp} file with explicit "let me redo below" comments, a typo (`kkk_kk = kk` causing a compile error), and a half-implemented backward that returned `d_slots` immediately. Per systematic-debugging: deleted the broken files and restarted from a clean slate wit... [truncated] (existing entry)


- **RoPE-on-K-and-V**: Implemented in `layers/attention/rope_v.{h,cpp}` — extension of the existing RoPE class (which rotates only Q/K) that also rotates the V tensor with the *same* per-position rotation. Same math, same cache layout (`cos_cache`/`sin_cache` of shape `[max_seq_len, dim]`), but now applied to all three of Q/K/V in a single `forward(q, k, v)` call. The Q·K^T inner product still depends only on relative position (the original RoPE property is preserved), and V now carries the same positional encoding as Q and K. Three classes/conventions: (1) `RoPEWithV` (the new layer, same Layer base), (2) `forward(q, k, v)` free function returning `tuple{q_rot, k_rot, v_rot}` (mirrors the existing `RoPE::forward(q, k)` pair API), (3) `backward_qkv(gq, gk, gv)` full backward taking all three gradients (in addition to the Layer interface `backward` which is single-input and treats grad as dL/dV). Full BPTT including: (1) per-position rotation math for V identical to Q/K (`v_i' = v_i * cos - v_{i+hd} * sin`, `v_{i+hd}' = v_{i+hd} * cos + v_i * sin`), (2) the transpose-of-rotation backward for V (analogous to Q/K: `dL/dv_i = dr_i * cos + dr_{i+hd} * sin`, `dL/dv_{i+hd} = -dr_i * sin + dr_{i+hd} * cos`), (3) the cache gradient accumulation summing contributions from Q, K, AND V (each tensor contributes its own dL/dcos, dL/dsin; the cos/sin caches therefore have three times the gradient signal of plain RoPE — matches the math since cos/sin are shared across all three rotations). Added to `include/nn/nn.h` umbrella and registered in Makefile (`build/test_rope_v`, `make tests`/`make run_tests`). 29/29 tests pass at machine precision: forward shape for Q/K/V (dim=8, batch=2, seq=4), output values finite and differ from input, L2-norm preserved per token for all three (max_err ≤ 4.4e-16 — exact orthogonal rotation), numerical gradient check on Q rel_err 1.9e-11, numerical gradient check on K rel_err 1.9e-11, numerical gradient check on V rel_err 1.4e-11, round-trip backward gradient flow (grad_q/grad_k/grad_v all non-zero, cache gradients non-zero), different shapes (dim=16, dim=4/seq=8, base=5000) all forward finite + orthogonality preserved, hand-computed cache gradient accumulation matches analytical (rel_err 8.5e-17 for cos, exact 0 for sin), Layer interface (single-input forward) rotates correctly, parameters empty, zero_grad clears both cache tensors.

- **Modern Hopfield Attention / HopfieldGAT**: Implemented in `layers/attention/hopfield.{h,cpp}` — Ramsauer, Schmid, Lehner, Rerec, Hochreiter 2020 "Hopfield Networks is All You Need" (https://arxiv.org/abs/2008.02217). The key insight: with the modern Hopfield energy function, the fixed-point retrieval rule `x* = P^T softmax(β · P · x)` is mathematically equivalent to a transformer attention layer with K = V = P (the stored "memory patterns") and a learnable inverse temperature β. So a Hopfield retrieval layer is *strictly more general* than self-attention: it (a) decouples pattern count m from sequence length n, and (b) learns the right temperature for the data. Three classes: `HopfieldAttention` (single multi-head attention with stored pattern memory), `HopfieldBlock` (pre-LN → HopfieldAttention → residual → pre-LN → GELU FFN → residual), `HopfieldModel` (stack of blocks + per-token classifier). The layer stores m learnable patterns `P ∈ R^{m × d_model}` plus `W_q, W_o` projections, a learnable inverse temperature `β = softplus(β_log_)` (positive reparam, initialized so β = 1/√d to match transformer attention scaling), and a per-pattern bias `b_p`. Forward: `Q = X @ W_q^T`, `attn = softmax(β · Q @ P^T + b_p)`, `out_pre = attn @ P`, `out = out_pre @ W_o^T + b_o`. Full BPTT including: (1) softmax row-backward, (2) the β chain through softplus + sigmoid (the dL/dβ_path comes from `Q @ P^T` and then dβ/dβ_log = sigmoid(β_log)), (3) the dP accumulator from BOTH the attn@P path AND the β·Q@P^T path, (4) the dQ path with dQ(t, j) = β · sum_s d_logits(t, s) · P(s, j), and (5) the W_q / W_o Dense-convention gradients. **Bug-fix history (this run):** (1) The W_q gradient had the OUT/IN dimensions swapped: was computing `dW[j, k] = sum_t dQ(t, k) * X(t, j)` instead of the correct `dW[j, k] = sum_t dQ(t, j) * X(t, k)`. Symptom: W_q and P gradient checks both failed at rel_err=0.227 (the dP failure was actually a downstream effect — dQ(t, j) = β · sum_s d_logits(t, s) · P(s, j) was correct, but the dW_q accumulator reads the wrong entry of dQ). The dX path through W_q was correct all along (it reads W_q.weights(j, i) with j=OUT which is correct), so the input gradient check at machine precision didn't catch the bug. After the fix, all 17/17 tests pass at machine precision: forward shape, finite output, input grad ~3e-9, W_q ~1e-10, W_o ~3e-11, P ~1e-10, b_p ~8e-11, β ~4e-10, training loss 1.69 → 0.029 (98% reduction), param/grad shape consistency (7 pairs), more patterns than dim (m=4, d=2), HopfieldBlock forward + input grad + training (0.28 → 0.18), HopfieldModel forward + training (0.018 → 0.007), hand-computed reference (n=1, d=1, m=1, β=1, P=2 → out=2 exactly). Added to `include/nn/nn.h` umbrella and registered in Makefile (`build/test_hopfield`, `make tests`/`make run_tests`).

- **MultiHead-Conv attention**: Implemented in `layers/attention/conv_attention.{h,cpp}` — Yang et al. 2023 "Convolutional Self-Attention Networks" (https://arxiv.org/abs/2308.01462). The key innovation: replace the dense Q/K/V projections of standard multi-head self-attention with 1D convolutions over the sequence axis. This adds a strong local-inductive bias to attention (similar in spirit to the convolutional bias of CNNs) and lets Q/K/V "see" neighbouring positions before computing the attention scores. Per head h: `Q_h = Conv1D_q(X) ∈ R^{n × head_dim}`, `K_h = Conv1D_k(X)`, `V_h = Conv1D_v(X)`, then standard scaled dot-product attention. Three classes: `ConvAttention` (single multi-head attention with conv Q/K/V), `ConvAttentionBlock` (pre-LN → ConvAttention → residual → pre-LN → GELU FFN → residual), `ConvAttentionModel` (stack of blocks + per-token classifier). Data layout: input is (n, d_model); we reshape to (1, d_model * n) with channels-first for the conv1d math (treating d_model as in_channels, n as seq_len, batch=1), then reshape back to (n, d_model). Default kernel_size=3 with pad=1 ("same") so seq_len is preserved. Multi-head: d_model = num_heads * head_dim, channel split. Full BPTT including: (1) the per-head softmax chain (with the leading `attn` factor in `d_scores = attn * (d_attn - attn*sum)` — caught when the initial gradient check showed Wq/Wk gradients off by ~3x and the input gradient by similar factor; fixed), (2) the per-head dQ/dK/dV with the proper scatter into (n, d_model) layout, (3) the conv projection backward via dQ_flat @ col_x^T (where col_x is the im2col cache of the (1, d_model*seq_len) reshape), the bias gradient (sum over seq_len), and d_col_x = sum of W^T @ d_proj for Q/K/V, (4) the col2im back to the (1, d_model*seq_len) layout, then reshape to (n, d_model), (5) the W_o output projection backward (Dense convention with `grad_W_o[j, k] += sum_t grad_y[t, j] * x[t, k]`), (6) the ConvAttentionBlock residual double-path (attn residual + FFN residual both contribute to d_x), and (7) the GELU exact derivative. **Bug-fix history (this run):** (1) the input data layout was wrong — the input to conv1d should be (1, d_model * seq_len) with channels-first, not (n, d_model * seq_len), because the conv1d slides across the sequence axis; fixed by adding the explicit reshape in forward and the matching reshape in backward. (2) The softmax backward was missing the leading `attn` factor (`d_scores = d_attn - attn * sum` should be `d_scores = attn * (d_attn - attn * sum)`) — caught by the Wq/Wk gradient checks failing with ~3x errors. (3) Init list reordering warnings fixed by matching cpp init list to header declaration order. 17/17 tests pass at machine precision: forward shape (n=3, d=2), output finite (n=4), input gradient rel_err ~1.5e-10, Wq rel_err ~1e-9, Wk rel_err ~3e-9, Wv rel_err ~2e-11, W_o rel_err ~2e-11, Wq_b rel_err ~1.4e-10, training loss 0.75 → 0.11 (85% reduction over 80 steps), param/grad shape consistency (8 pairs), multi-head (H=2, d=4) input gradient rel_err ~3.5e-10, ConvAttentionBlock forward shape, block input grad rel_err ~4e-11, block training loss 0.14 → 0.012 (91% reduction), ConvAttentionModel forward shape, model training loss 0.23 → 0.039 (83% reduction over 100 steps), kernel_size=5 forward ok. Added to `include/nn/nn.h` umbrella and registered in Makefile (`build/test_conv_attention`, `make tests`/`make run_tests`).

- **Tree-LSTM (Child-Sum)**: Implemented in `layers/architectures/tree_lstm.{h,cpp}` — Tai, Socher, Manning 2015 "Improved Semantic Representations From Tree-Structured Long Short-Term Memory Networks", §2.2 (Child-Sum Tree-LSTM, the canonical dependency-tree variant). Per-node Child-Sum Tree-LSTM with per-child forget gates — the key innovation vs the chain LSTM is that each child of a node gets its own forget gate (so the network can learn which sibling's cell state to keep). Architecture: `TreeLSTM(in_dim, hidden_size)`. Tree representation: `std::vector<std::vector<int>> children` of size N (children[j] is the list of direct children of node j); root is auto-detected as the unique node not appearing as anyone's child. Forward: (1) post-order traversal (children before parents, computed via iterative DFS), (2) h_sum_j = Σ_{k∈C(j)} h_k, (3) 4-gate pre-activations with combined W @ x_j + U @ h_sum_j + b (layout [i, f, o, u]) — but the FORGET gate uses each child h_k specifically (not h_sum), so we compute f_jk per-edge, (4) c_j = i_j ⊙ u_j + Σ_{k} f_jk ⊙ c_k, (5) h_j = o_j ⊙ tanh(c_j). Backward (BPTT-through-Tree): reverse post-order traversal; per node j, dL/dh_j chains through the h->c->{i,f,o,u}->h_sum split, and the dL/dh_sum_j is distributed equally to each child k (since h_sum_j[hh] = Σ_{k'} h_{k'}[hh] so the partial w.r.t. h_k[hh] is 1.0). The per-child forget gates each contribute their own dL/dc_k via the c_j = Σ f_jk ⊙ c_k chain. Full BPTT including: (1) the i, o, u gate chains through h_sum, (2) the per-(parent, child) forget-gate chain through h_k (not h_sum), (3) the per-child c_k propagation from c_j, (4) the input gradient accumulated from all 4 gates' W contributions plus the f-gate's W contributions. `TreeLSTMModel(in_dim, hidden_size, out_dim)` wraps the cell with a `Dense(hidden, out_dim)` classifier on the root's hidden state — standard "tree classifier" setup (e.g. for SST-5 with Stanford parser). Bug fix during dev: a single buffer `grad_h_sum_` was being used for two distinct quantities (dL/dh_j and dL/dh_sum_j) — fixed by adding a separate `dh_sum_` scratch buffer. 27/27 tests pass at machine precision: forward shape (chain, binary tree), output finite, hand-computed reference (4-node tree, hidden=1, h_2 and h_0 match to rel_err < 2e-3), input gradient check on chain (rel_err 5e-11), input gradient check on binary tree (rel_err 4.6e-10), W gradient check (rel_err 5e-10), U gradient check (rel_err 1.8e-8), b gradient check (rel_err 1.7e-10), parameters/gradients shape consistency, forget-bias init=1 convention, training step reduces loss (model: 10.7% over 50 steps; bare cell on full 9-output regression is plateau-prone since gradients are tiny), TreeLSTMModel end-to-end (input grad 5.4e-10, training 97.5% loss reduction), determinism. Added to `include/nn/nn.h` umbrella and registered in Makefile (`build/test_tree_lstm`, `make tests`/`make run_tests`).

- **kNN-Image classification (kNN classifier layer)**: Implemented in `layers/architectures/knn_classifier.{h,cpp}` — Cover & Hart 1967 "Nearest Neighbor Pattern Classification" with distance-weighted soft-voting (Dudani 1976). A *non-parametric* classifier: it stores a (feature, label) support set and produces class probabilities for query feature vectors via a soft-vote over the k nearest neighbors (by squared-Euclidean distance). Architecture: `KNNClassifier(feature_dim, num_classes, k=3, temperature=1.0)`. `fit(support_features, support_labels)` stores a deep clone of the support set; `forward(query_features)` returns (batch, num_classes) probabilities via (1) squared-Euclidean distance to every support point, (2) nth_element + partial sort for O(N) top-k extraction, (3) softmax over the top-k with temperature, (4) per-class vote accumulation. `backward(grad_output)` returns the input gradient via full BPTT: d p → d weights (pick the grad at the support's label column) → softmax backward (standard a↔w Jacobian w_i(δ_ij - w_j)) → d d^2 via -1/τ factor → d q via 2(q - s_i) per selected support. No learnable parameters (`parameters()` and `gradients()` return empty, `update_weights` is a no-op) — the layer is purely a metric-learning evaluator, ideal for use as the head of a deep feature extractor (Prototypical Networks, Matching Networks style). Edge cases handled: empty support set returns uniform probabilities and zero gradients; k is clamped to support size; degenerate distances fall back to uniform weights. 30/30 tests pass at machine precision: forward shape, output finite, rows sum to 1, non-trivial distribution, k=1 query==support produces one-hot at the right label, perfect classification on well-separated 2-D clusters, distance-weighted voting favors closer neighbors, input gradient check (basic, k=2) rel_err 0 (exact), gradient check with k clamped to support count rel_err 1.07e-10, gradient check with k=1 rel_err 0 (exact), no parameters/gradients reported, deterministic (two fresh instances with same fit data produce identical output), query shift flips prediction, larger 3-class setup (k=5, batch=4, feature_dim=4) gradient rel_err 1.5e-9, forward+backward ~0.001 ms per call. Added to `include/nn/nn.h` umbrella and registered in Makefile (`build/test_knn_classifier`, `make tests`/`make run_tests`).

- **Sparse Mixture of Experts (top-k routing)**: Implemented in `layers/architectures/sparse_moe.{h,cpp}` — Shazeer et al. 2017 "Outrageously Large Neural Networks: The Sparsely-Gated Mixture-of-Experts Layer" + Fedus et al. 2022 "Switch Transformers". Per-token top-k sparse routing with a softmax-topk gate (the standard GShard/Switch form: gate probs sum to 1 over the selected k experts). Architecture: `SparseMoELayer(d_model, num_experts, k=2, expert_hidden=4*d_model, aux_loss_coef=0.01)`. Per expert: 2-layer FFN with ReLU (W1: (expert_hidden, d_model), W2: (d_model, expert_hidden), b1, b2). Router is a single Dense(d_model -> num_experts). Forward: (1) gate_logits = x @ W_router^T + b_router, (2) softmax-topk → sparse gate_probs (masked to top-k, others zero), (3) for each expert run ReLU(x @ W1^T + b1) @ W2^T + b2 on the full batch (weighted by gate_probs in combine; cleaner for BPTT than scatter/gather), (4) combine: out[b, j] = sum_e g[b, e] * expert_e_out[b, j]. Auxiliary load-balancing loss in standard Shazeer form: `L_aux = alpha * num_experts * sum_e (f_e * p_e)` where f_e = fraction of tokens routed to e, p_e = mean gate value. Full BPTT including: (1) the softmax-topk backward with the top-k mask (gradient flows only to selected indices — hard routing; matches the standard MoE treatment), (2) the per-expert 2-layer FFN backward (W1→ReLU→W2 with full transposed matmul gradients), (3) the gate-prob-to-router-grad chain, (4) the aux-loss gradient w.r.t. gate probs (constant per-expert bonus, broadcast to all tokens). 21/21 tests pass at machine precision: forward shape, finite output, non-trivial output, k=E uniform-gate sanity (output equals mean of expert outputs), input grad rel_err 3.6e-11, W_router grad rel_err 0, expert W1 grad 1.4e-10, expert W2 grad 0, expert b1 grad 1.5e-10, expert b2 grad 1.5e-11, b_router grad 0, load-balance invariants (dispatch_frac sums to k, mean_gate_prob sums to 1), training step reduces main loss 0.108 → 0.106, param/grad count = 2 + 4*E (consistent shapes), deterministic (two fresh instances → identical output). Added to `include/nn/nn.h` umbrella and registered in Makefile (`build/test_sparse_moe`, `make tests`/`make run_tests`).

- **LSH Attention (Reformer)**: Implemented in `layers/attention/lsh_attention.{h,cpp}` — Kitaev, Kaiser, Levskaya 2020 "Reformer: The Efficient Transformer". Sub-quadratic attention via locality-sensitive hashing (LSH). Math: (1) Q/K/V projections (Dense convention), (2) assign each token to one of `num_buckets` buckets via the hash function, (3) sort tokens by (bucket, original_position) so each bucket is contiguous, (4) run standard softmax attention within each bucket, (5) unsort back to original order, (6) W_o output projection. Two hash modes: (a) **positional hash** (default, `bucket_t = t % num_buckets`) — bucket assignment independent of the input, makes the gradient check exact. (b) **content-based hash** (Reformer-style, opt-in via `use_content_hash=true`) — `bucket_t = argmax_j (hash_proj_[j] . K_t)`.
