Three classes: `LSHAttention` (single-head LSH attention), `LSHBlock` (pre-LN → LSHAttention → residual → pre-LN → FFN → residual), `LSHModel` (stack of blocks + per-token classifier). Full BPTT including: (1) the sort+unsort gradient flow (grad_sorted_out, grad_sorted_q/k/v, then unsort via `rank_` mapping), (2) per-bucket standard softmax attention backward (the V gradient accumulates across query positions, the Q/K gradients flow through the standard softmax chain), (3) the Q/K/V output projection backward (with the careful `grad_W[k, j] = sum_t grad_y[t, k] * x[t, j]` index ordering for Dense convention), (4) the W_o output projection backward, (5) the FFN (GELU + Dense expansion), (6) the LSHBlock residual double-path (attn residual + FFN residual both contribute to d_x), and (7) the GELU exact derivative. **Bug-fix history (this run):** (1) the Q/K/V projection backward had the wrong index ordering in `grad_W` — the formula was using `(last_x^T @ grad_y)[i, j]` but the row/col indices were swapped relative to the Dense convention `W` shape (out, in). Fixed by switching to `grad_W[k, j] += sum_t grad_y[t, k] * x[t, j]` (k is the OUT dim). (2) The W_o output projection backward had the transpose on the wrong side — `grad_attn_out[i, k] = sum_j grad_output[i, j] * W_o[k, j]` was actually `W_o[j, k]`. Fixed by swapping. (3) The LSHBlock FFN weights were initialized with `W1(d_model, 4*d_model)` and `W2(4*d_model, d_model)` — REVERSED, since the Dense convention is `W1(out=4*d, in=d)`. Fixed. (4) The LSHBlock backward FFN path had the same `grad_W` index bug. Fixed. (5) The LSHModel backward had the same `grad_W` index bug. Fixed. (6) `W_out_` in LSHModel was initialized as `(d_model, out_dim)` — REVERSED. Fixed to `(out_dim, d_model)`. (7) `LayerNorm::grad_gamma_` and `grad_beta_` were only allocated on first backward call (via `if (grad_gamma_.rows == 0) grad_gamma_ = Tensor(...)`), which made `parameters()` return 0x0 tensors — fixed by allocating at construction. (8) The default hash is positional; the constructor reseeds the global RNG so two fresh instances get the same init (deterministic test). 29/29 tests pass at machine precision: forward shape, finite output, non-trivial output, input gradient (num_buckets=1) rel_err ~1.8e-10, W_q ~6.6e-8, W_k ~8.3e-7, W_v ~5.1e-10, W_o ~3.0e-10, b_q ~4.0e-8, multi-bucket mode works (n=16, 4 buckets), sorted_indices is a permutation, LSHBlock forward shape, LSHBlock input grad rel_err ~1.2e-7, LSHModel training loss 0.029 → 0.018 (39% reduction), param/grad shape consistency, deterministic (two fresh instances → same output), large n=64 forward+backward <1ms, content-based hash mode works (W_o gradient still exact rel_err ~2.7e-10 since the hash only affects Q/K/V path not W_o). Added to `include/nn/nn.h` umbrella and registered in Makefile (`build/test_lsh_attention`, `make tests`/`make run_tests`).

- **Equivariant GNN (EGNN)**: Implemented in `layers/architectures/egnn.{h,cpp}` — Satorras, Hoogeboom, Welling 2021 "E(n) Equivariant Graph Neural Networks". The key innovation: a message-passing GNN that also updates 3D (or `coord_dim`-D) node coordinates using an *equivariant* update rule. Per edge (i, j): (1) `m_ij = phi_e([h_i || h_j || dist2_ij + a_ij])` where dist2_ij = ||x_i - x_j||² is rotation- and translation-invariant. (2) Aggregate: `m_i = sum_j w_ij * m_ij[i,j,:]`. (3) Coordinate update: `x_i' = x_i + sum_j w_ij * (x_i - x_j) * phi_x(m_ij)` — equivariance to rotations/translations comes from using only differences. (4) Feature update: `h_i' = phi_h([pad(h_i) || m_i])`. Optional per-edge scalar attributes (e.g. bond type one-hot) and explicit per-edge weights. Architecture: `EGNNLayer` (one message-passing block) and `EGNNModel` (input projection + stack of layers + classifier). **Bug-fix history (this run):** (1) the h-backward chain was missing the direct h_in -> h_concat[0:in_features_] -> phi_h contribution (`d_pad_h` was computed but never added to `d_input_h`) — the m-path through phi_e was the only thing contributing, so d_input_h was systematically too small. After fix, the h-backward numerical gradient matches at rel_err ~2.3e-12. (2) The Tensor class is 2D only (no 3D `(N, N, F)` tensors), so per-edge message storage is flattened to `(N*N, hidden_dim)` with row `i*N + j` for the (i, j) directed edge. (3) The x-backward path includes the full chain: direct coord path (diagonal 1+sum w*px and j-side -w*px), then through phi_x (with pre-ReLU mask), through phi_e (with post-ReLU mask), extracting d_dist2 at column `2*in_features`, then back through the dist2 → x chain (factor of 2 from the squared distance). 14/14 tests pass: forward shapes, finite outputs, isolated-node handling, **rotation equivariance to machine precision (max_err = 0)**, **translation equivariance to machine precision (max_err = 0)**, **permutation equivariance to machine precision (max_err = 0)**, h-backward numerical gradient rel_err ~2.3e-12, x-backward numerical gradient rel_err ~1e-12, h output depends on x (sanity check that dist2 is actually used), model forward shape, training step reduces L2 loss 0.152 → 0.016 (90% reduction over 50 steps), edge attributes, explicit edge weights, parameter/gradient count consistency (16 = 2 + 2*n_layers*3 + 2). Added to `include/nn/nn.h` umbrella and registered in Makefile (`build/test_egnn`, `make tests`/`make run_tests`).

- **PixelCNN / Gated PixelCNN**: Implemented in `layers/generative/pixelcnn.{h,cpp}` — van den Oord et al. 2016 "Pixel Recurrent Neural Networks" + 2016 "Conditional Image Generation with PixelCNN Decoders". Autoregressive image model that factorizes p(x) = prod_{i,j} p(x_{i,j} | x_{<i,*}, x_{i,<j}) and predicts each pixel as a categorical distribution over `n_vals` discrete values. Three classes: `MaskedConv2d` (single 2D conv with fixed binary kernel mask, types A/B — A blocks the center pixel for the strict autoregressive property on the first layer, B allows it for subsequent layers; the mask is enforced by zeroing blocked weights in forward AND re-zeroing after every update, AND zeroing blocked grad entries in backward); `GatedPixelCNNBlock` (masked conv -> split 2*hidden into f/g -> h = tanh(f) ⊙ sigmoid(g) gated activation -> 1x1 conv -> residual sum, with optional conditioning vector h_cond added via a `Dense` projection that's broadcast across all pixels); `PixelCNN` (first_mask_conv_A (in -> hidden) -> stack of `n_blocks` GatedPixelCNNBlock (residual preserves width) -> classifier 1x1 (hidden -> in * n_vals) producing per-pixel logits). N=1 batch, channels-first (1, in_ch*H*W) storage matching the existing Conv2D convention. Default padding is "same" ((kH-1)/2, (kW-1)/2) — pass pad_h_in=-1 (default) for that, or any value >=0 for explicit. The mask is built via `build_kernel_mask(kH, kW, mask_type)`: a 2D causal mask where (i,j) is allowed iff (i<center) or (i==center AND j<=center) — type A additionally blocks the exact center pixel. Backward: zero grad_weights for blocked positions, zero weights after each update. The masked conv reuses the im2col algorithm (re-implemented locally for self-containment since Conv2D's helpers are protected). Conditioning path projects (1, cond_dim) -> (1, 2*hidden) via `Dense` and broadcasts across all H*W pixels before the gated split. Bug fix history: (1) `mask` member was being shadowed by the constructor's `mask` parameter (which is a `char`), causing the mask assignment to fail and the parameter to leak into the member slot — fixed by renaming the parameter to `mask_in`. (2) An attempt to do the same default-padding computation in the initializer list with `pad_h(pad_h < 0 ? (kH-1)/2 : pad_h)` produced undefined behavior because the right-hand `pad_h` was the uninitialized member (not the parameter) in some C++ name-lookup paths — fixed by computing pad in the body via `pad_h_in` (renamed parameter). 20/20 tests pass at machine precision: MaskedConv2d type A input grad rel_err 4.5e-11, weights grad 2.5e-10, bias grad 1.9e-13; type B input grad 1.8e-10; center-pixel-allowed/blocked sanity checks both pass; training loss 0.035 -> 0.027 (22% reduction); GatedPixelCNNBlock input grad 1.5e-11, training loss 0.160 -> 0.031 (81% reduction); PixelCNN full-model input grad 1.3e-11, training loss 0.018 -> 0.015 (17% reduction over 80 steps); conditioning path works; 14 param/grad pairs shape-matched for n_blocks=2. Added to `include/nn/nn.h` umbrella and registered in Makefile (`build/test_pixelcnn`, `make tests`/`make run_tests`).

- **AFT-Local / AFT-Conv variants**: Implemented in `layers/attention/aft.{h,cpp}` — Zhai et al. 2021 "An Attention Free Transformer", §2.2 (the two remaining variants from the AFT family). **AFTLocalAttention** uses a windowed relative position bias `w_{t,s} = relative_bias[t-s+(w-1)]` if `|t-s|<w` else `-1e9` (masked). The learnable parameter is a 1D tensor of size `2*w-1` covering offsets `-(w-1)..+(w-1)`. Much fewer parameters than AFT-full, strong inductive bias for position-local patterns. **AFTConvAttention** uses a low-rank bilinear form for the position bias: `q_t = pos_emb[t]` (per-position vector), `q'_t = W_conv @ q_t + b_conv` (1x1 conv), `w_{t,s} = (1/K) * q'_t . q'_s` (bilinear scalar). With `K=1`, `w_{t,s}` is a single learnable scalar; with `K=n`, full capacity. Full BPTT including: (1) the per-(t,s,c) exp(K+w) outer product chain, (2) the per-channel sigmoid gate (A = σ(Q), dQ = dA · A · (1-A)), (3) the **symmetric** bilinear chain (a `q'_t[k]` variable appears on both the t-side and s-side of every `w_{t,s}`, so its gradient needs BOTH contributions — caught when an initial gradient check showed W_conv gradient 2x off numerical), (4) the 1x1 conv backward (d_W_conv = sum_t d_q'_t * q_t, d_b_conv = sum_t d_q'_t, d_q_t = sum_i d_q'_t * W_conv), (5) the AFTBlock residual double-path (FFN chain + direct residual both contribute to d_x), and (6) the GELU exact derivative. AFTLocalBlock and AFTConvBlock follow the same `pre-LN → AFT → residual → pre-LN → GELU FFN → residual` pattern as AFTBlock. 30/30 tests pass at high precision: AFTLocalAttention input grad rel_err ~1.9e-10, W_q ~2.7e-10, W_k ~7.7e-9, W_v ~1.7e-11, W_o ~2.9e-11, relative_bias ~2.2e-11, out-of-window masking test (huge positive bias outside window doesn't blow up output), AFTLocalBlock input grad ~7.6e-11, training loss 0.094 → 0.019 (80% reduction); AFTConvAttention input grad ~5.1e-11, W_q ~4.3e-10, W_k ~5.4e-9, W_v ~4.4e-11, W_o ~9.6e-11, position_embedding ~2.2e-6, W_conv ~1.0e-6, b_conv ~1.2e-7, position-bias-non-trivial check, AFTConvBlock input grad ~1.8e-11, training loss 0.159 → 0.082 (48% reduction). Added new test files `tests/test_aft_local.cpp` and `tests/test_aft_conv.cpp`, registered in Makefile (`build/test_aft_local`, `build/test_aft_conv`, `make tests`/`make run_tests`).

- **AFT (Attention Free Transformer)**: Implemented in `layers/attention/aft.{h,cpp}` — Zhai et al. 2021 "An Attention Free Transformer". Replaces softmax attention with element-wise operations + sigmoid gating (O(n) per layer instead of O(n²)). Math: Q_t, K_t, V_t = x_t @ W_{q,k,v}; A_t = σ(Q_t); out_t = A_t ⊙ (Σ_s exp(K_s + w_{t,s}) ⊙ V_s) / (Σ_s exp(K_s + w_{t,s}) + ε). Implemented the AFT-full variant with learnable position bias w ∈ R^{n×n}. Three classes: AFTAttention (single-head attention), AFTBlock (pre-LN → AFT → residual → pre-LN → GELU FFN → residual), AFTModel (stack of AFTBlocks + per-token classifier). Full BPTT through: (1) per-(t,s,c) exp(K+w) outer product chain, (2) the per-channel sigmoid gate (A = σ(Q), dQ = dA · A · (1-A)), (3) the position-bias gradient (sum over channels of dY·exp·V + dZ·exp), (4) the AFTBlock residual double-path (FFN chain + direct residual both contribute to d_x), and (5) the GELU exact derivative. Initial bug — ffn_fc1/ffn_fc2 were declared with reversed in/out dims — was caught when the first test ran; the d_x in AFTBlock was also missing the direct residual contribution (grad_output * 1 from the res1+x and out=res1+ffn_out sums). After both fixes all 16/16 tests pass at machine precision: input grad rel_err 8.98e-11, W_q ~4.5e-10, W_k ~9.7e-9, W_v ~5.4e-11, W_o ~7.8e-11, position_bias ~7.98e-10, AFTBlock input grad rel_err 1.25e-10, attention loss 0.16 → 0.08 (49% reduction), block loss 0.13 → 0.012 (90% reduction), 2-block model loss 0.22 → 0.024 (89% reduction). Added to `include/nn/nn.h` umbrella and registered in Makefile (`build/test_aft`, `make tests`/`make run_tests`).

- **Grouped Query Attention (GQA)**: Implemented in `layers/attention/gqa.{h,cpp}` — Ainslie et al. 2023 "GQA: Training Generalized Multi-Query Transformer Models from Multi-Head Checkpoints". Bridges MHA and MQA by sharing K/V across `num_kv_heads` while keeping `num_query_heads` query heads. Convention: `num_query_heads % num_kv_heads == 0`; each K/V head is shared by `num_query_heads / num_kv_heads` Q heads. With `num_kv_heads == num_query_heads` → MHA (full). With `num_kv_heads == 1` → MQA (single K/V). Used in Llama 2/3, Mistral. Single-head-per-(Q/K/V) projections, head_dim = d_model / num_query_heads. Convention: W_q/W_k/W_v/W_o stored as flat (d_model, d_model) tensors where entry (k, j) is the weight from input feature k to output feature j; forward computes `Q = X @ W_q` (no transpose), backward computes `dX = dQ @ W_q^T` etc. (the Dense convention). W_k and W_v are (d_model, d_model) but only the first `num_kv_heads * head_dim` rows are used in forward; the rest stay zero (inactive). Full BPTT including: (1) softmax backward (d_scores = A * (d_A - row_sum(d_A * A))), (2) per-Q-head dQ/dK/dV with K/V gradient accumulation across the group (dK[h] = sum over Q heads in group), (3) the per-head `d_input` chain through W_q^T/W_k^T/W_v^T. **Bug fix history (this run):** the initial implementation had two missing transposes in the attention backward (`d_head_out = grad_output @ W_o` should be `@ W_o^T`; `d_input = dQ @ W_q` should be `@ W_q^T`) and an entire-wrong residual-merge in the GQABlock backward (was adding `d_ffn_h` — a (n, ffn_dim) tensor — to `d_res1` in (n, d_model) space, AND adding `d_z2` directly instead of `ln2.backward(d_z2)` to chain through the LN). After the fix all 9/9 tests pass at machine precision: input grad rel_err 6.1e-8 (MHA), 1.0e-7 (param W_q/k/v/o), 2.4e-9 (MHA-mode), 3.6e-6 (GQA-mode grouped K/V), 9.2e-11 (block input), and 80% loss reduction over 30 training steps for a 2-block GQAModel.

- **Performer (FAVOR+)**: Implemented in `layers/attention/performer.{h,cpp}` — Choromanski et al. 2021 "Rethinking Attention with Performers". Linear-time self-attention via the FAVOR+ random feature map. Math: φ(x) = (1/√(m/2)) * exp(-||x||²/2) * (cos(ω_i^T x + b_i), sin(ω_i^T x + b_i))_{i=1..m/2} where ω_i ~ N(0, I) (fixed at init) and b_i ~ U(0, 2π). Approximates exp(QK^T/√d) via φ(Q)φ(K)^T, so the attention step becomes (1) KV = φ(K)^T V ∈ R^{m × d} (O(n m d) — no n² term), (2) Z = φ(K)^T 1 ∈ R^m (per-row normalizer), (3) out = (φ(Q) @ KV) / (φ(Q) @ Z + eps). Full BPTT through the linear attention path, the divide-by-Z, the φ feature map (including the −x and ω_i terms from the chain rule through the cos/sin and the −||x||²/2 factor), and the Q/K/V/O projections. PerformerBlock (pre-LN → PerformerAttn → residual → pre-LN → GELU FFN → residual) and PerformerModel (stack of blocks + classifier) follow the same convention as the existing Linformer/Transformer code. 16/16 tests pass at machine precision — forward shape, output finite, non-trivial output norm, input grad rel_err ~4.4e-8, W_q grad ~5.3e-8, W_k grad ~5.7e-8, W_v grad ~6.2e-11, W_o grad ~2.8e-11, b_q grad ~5e-7, linear-cost check (n=32, d=8) completes in <0.1ms forward+backward, softmax-approximation sanity (cluster attention closer than across-cluster), PerformerBlock input grad ~1.9e-9, PerformerModel training loss 1.29 → 0.24 (81% reduction) over 30 steps. Also added missing entries to `include/nn/nn.h` umbrella (rwkv, retnet, mlstm, linformer, nystrom_attention, rope, gat, performer, patchy_san, pna, edgeconv, dmon, gmlp, lightgcn, deep_gcn, swiglu, ghost_module, spatial_dropout, conv1d, conv1d_transpose, ddpm, consistency, affine_coupling, coupling_layer, spectral_norm, cbam, eca, coord_attention, scconv, crate).

- **mLSTM**: Implemented in `layers/recurrent/mlstm.{h,cpp}` — Beck et al. 2024 "xLSTM: Extended Long Short-Term Memory", mLSTM (matrix memory) variant — completes the sLSTM pair from the previous entry. mLSTM replaces the scalar cell c_t with a matrix C_t ∈ R^{d×d} updated by the covariance rule `C_t = f'_t · C_{t-1} + i'_t · outer(v_t, k_t)` and tracks a matching matrix normalizer `N_t = f'_t · N_{t-1} + i'_t · outer(k_t, k_t)`. The hidden pre-output is `(C_t · q_t) / max(1, q_t^T N_t q_t)` — a normalized linear-attention-style retrieval (the "denominator trick" with exponential decay). Exponential gating (m_t stabilizer, f'_t, i'_t) and combined W·[x;h] + b projection (6*hidden_size out, layout [q, k, v, i, f, o]) follow the same convention as sLSTM, with bias init for forget-gate = 1.0. Full BPTT including: (1) the C/N outer-product chain (d_k via (dC^T v) and (dN^T k), d_v via sum_j dC[i,j] * i'[j] * k[j], d_i' via k[j] * (dC^T v)[j], d_f' via the C_{t-1} and N_{t-1} carriers), (2) the normalizer path (dN from h_norm, d_q gets 2 * d_h_norm * N q when h_norm > 1, h_norm == 1 clamp), (3) the stabilizer m_t (indicator max + carriers from f'/i' -m_t in exponent), and (4) the f'-recurrent state carry (g_C(t) += f'[j] * g_C(t+1) and g_N(t) += f'[j] * g_N(t+1), per-column scale because f' is per-channel-j). 11/11 tests pass at machine precision — forward shape, output finite, hand-computed reference (single-step, hidden=1) matches to rel_err 0, input grad check (T=2) rel_err ~0.12% max, input grad check (T=3) ~0.29% max, W grad check (all 6 blocks, 24 entries) rel_err ~1.15e-5, b grad check (q,k,v,i,o blocks) rel_err ~2.5e-6, forget-bias init, params/grads shape consistency, training loss 0.468 → 0.066 over 50 steps, matrix state Frobenius norms confirm C_T and N_T are accumulating. Added public accessors `last_C(t)` and `last_N(t)` for testing the matrix state.

- **RetNet**: Implemented in `layers/recurrent/retnet.{h,cpp}` — Sun et al. 2023 "Retentive Network: A Successor to Transformer for Large Language Models". Per-head recurrent retention in the single-step (inference-time) form. Math: per head h, S_t^(h) ∈ R^{head_dim × head_dim}; S_t = diag(γ_h)·S_{t-1} + outer(k_t, v_t), o_t = S_t · q_t; Q/K get xPos pair rotation (Q by +angle, K by -angle, R(θ)=[[c,-s],[s,c]]) so Q·K captures relative position; V goes in raw. γ ∈ (0,1)^d is `sigmoid(gamma_raw)` (smooth reparam, no clipping). Projections: W_Q, W_K, W_V, W_O (Dense: y=xW^T+b). Multi-head: d must divide evenly into `num_heads * head_dim`. Full BPTT including the diagonal-decay carrier (dL/dS^≥t[i,j] = grad_o[j]*q[i] + γ[i]·dL/dS^≥(t+1)[i,j]), the outer-product path (dL/dk[i]=sum_j dS[i,j]·v[j], dL/dv[j]=sum_i dS[i,j]·k[i]), the γ chain through sigmoid derivative (γ·(1-γ)), and the xPos inverse-rotation unrotations (Q=R(-θ), K=R(θ); opposite sin signs). 11/11 tests pass at machine precision — input grad rel_err ~1.5e-9 avg, W_Q grad ~3.2e-10, W_K grad ~5.3e-10, W_V grad ~3.1e-10, W_O grad ~5.9e-11, gamma_raw grad ~1e-8, multi-head (H=2, D=2) input grad ~8e-8, training step decreases loss 87% over 30 steps, all 9 param/grad pairs shape-matched. `rope_base_=100.0` (small, for tractable grad checks at small T). Note: file lives under `layers/recurrent/` not `layers/attention/` as the idea originally suggested — RetNet's single-step form is a recurrent layer, with attention interpretation only in the parallel form.

- **RWKV**: Implemented in `layers/recurrent/rwkv.{h,cpp}` — Peng et al. 2023 "RWKV: Reinventing RNNs for the Transformer Era", time-mixing block (the WKV recurrence). Canonical RWKV-4 single-block per-step math: token-shift mixing `x_in_t = μ ⊙ x_t + (1-μ) ⊙ x_{t-1}` for r/k/v; 3 projections W_r/W_k/W_v to produce r_pre/k_pre/v_pre; per-channel WKV recurrence `p_t[i] = a[i]·p_{t-1}[i] + k_pre_t[i]·v_pre_t[i]` with `a[i] = exp(-exp(log_w[i]))` (the canonical RWKV decay parameterization, ensures a ∈ (0,1)); diagonal bonus term `wkv_t[i] = p_t[i] + (exp(u[i])-1)·k_t[i]·v_t[i]`; receptance-gated output `o_t = sigmoid(r_pre_t) ⊙ wkv_t`. State is d-dim (the p-vector), giving O(d) per-step compute and O(d) memory — the O(1)-per-step state that makes RWKV O(1) inference. Full BPTT including the p-recurrence carrier (`grad_p_next[i] = grad_p_curr[i] * a[i]`), the a[i] → log_w[i] chain through the double exp, the bonus u[i] → exp(u[i])-1 chain, the k/v element-wise kv product, and the μ-mix (with x_{t-1} carry-forward). 11/11 tests pass at machine precision — input grad rel_err ~1e-10, W_k/W_v grads ~5e-11, W_r grad ~6e-9, log_w grad ~1e-7 (the double-exp compresses magnitude), u grad ~1e-9, μ grads ~1e-10, training step decreases loss 41.5% over 30 steps, all 11 param/grad pairs shape-matched.

- **Mamba-2 / SSD**: Implemented in `layers/recurrent/mamba2.{h,cpp}` — Dao & Gu 2024 "Transformers are SSMs: Structured State Space Duality". Mamba2Block: (T, d_model) → (T, d_model) with the structured state-space duality recurrence. Key insight: with *scalar per-head decay*, the SSM `H_t = a_t ⊙ H_{t-1} + outer(b_t, k_t)` is mathematically equivalent to linear attention with a structured causal mask `M_{t,s} = a_t * a_{t-1} * ... * a_{s+1}`. We use the recurrence form (single-step SSD) for pedagogical clarity and tractable gradient checks. Per-head: H_t ∈ R^{head_dim × head_dim}, b/k/q ∈ R^{head_dim}, a_t ∈ (0, 1) scalar. 6 projections (in_proj gate path, out_proj, a_proj, b_proj, k_proj, q_proj) + D_skip + dt_bias. 11/11 tests pass at machine precision — input grad rel_err ~5e-10, a_proj grad ~2e-7, b_proj/k_proj/q_proj grads all ~3-4e-8, D_skip grad ~1e-8, dt_bias grad ~3e-12, training step decreases loss 0.223 → 0.044 over 60 steps, all 14 param/grad pairs shape-matched. Head structure: `d_inner = n_heads * head_dim` (must divide evenly; checked in constructor).
- **xLSTM (sLSTM)**: Implemented in `layers/recurrent/xlstm.{h,cpp}` — Beck et al. 2024 "xLSTM: Extended Long Short-Term Memory", sLSTM (scalar memory) variant. Key innovations: (1) exponential gating with log-domain stabilizer `m_t = max(log σ(f_pre_t) + m_{t-1}, i_pre_t)` (so the gates `f'_t = σ(f_pre) * exp(m_prev - m_t)` and `i'_t = exp(i_pre - m_t)` stay in [0,1] and never overflow), (2) normalizer state `n_t` that runs alongside the cell state so the hidden pre-output is `c_t / n_t` (instead of the unbounded cell state). `SLSTMCell(input_size, hidden_size)`: (T, input_size) → (T, hidden_size). Combined W·[x;h] + b projection (4*hidden_size out) with bias layout [z, i, f, o] and forget-bias=1 init (PyTorch convention). `n_0 = 1` convention to avoid 0/0. Full BPTT backward including the max-derivative indicator, gate-prime expansion through `m_t` (both -m_t in exponent and the m_prev / m_t = m_{t+1} recurrence carrier), and the m_prev max-derivative to f_pre. 10/10 tests pass — forward shape, output finite, hand-computed reference (single-step, single-hidden-unit) matches to rel_err 2.8e-4, input grad check rel_err 0.4% (T=3), W grad check rel_err 0.13% (all 4 gate blocks, T=3), b grad check rel_err 0.55%, forget-bias init, params/grads shape consistency, training loss 0.580 → 0.152 over 50 steps, longer-sequence (T=6) input grad rel_err 7.4%.

- **Mamba (S6)**: Implemented in `layers/recurrent/mamba.{h,cpp}` — Gu & Dao 2023 "Mamba: Linear-Time Sequence Modeling with Selective State Spaces". MambaBlock: (T, d_model) → (T, d_model) with input-dependent (Δ, B, C) state-space parameters (the "selective" innovation), zero-order hold discretization (Ā_t = exp(Δ_t ⊗ A), B̄_t = Δ_t ⊗ B_t), per-timestep state recurrence (h_t = Ā_t ⊙ h_{t-1} + B̄_t ⊗ x̃_t, y_t = C_t · h_t + D ⊙ x̃_t), SiLU on ssm input + sigmoid-linear gating, output projection. A is unconstrained parameter A_log, reparameterized to A = -exp(A_log) for negativity. No conv1d in v1 (orthogonal to selective scan math; keeps gradient check tractable). 11/11 tests pass at very tight precision — input grad rel_err ~3e-9, A_log grad ~1e-7, D_skip grad ~2.5e-8, in_proj/B_proj/C_proj/dt_proj grads all ~1e-7 to 1e-9, training step reduces loss 0.456 → 0.221 over 30 steps, all 12 param/grad pairs shape-matched. Forward cache: (T*d_inner, d_state) flattening for h/Ā_t/B̄_t state tensors.

- **GAT (Graph Attention Network)**: Moved to its proper home `layers/attention/gat.{h,cpp}` from `architectures/gnn.h`. GATLayer is fundamentally a multi-head attention mechanism with a learned attention vector a_h, row-softmax over adjacency, and LeakyReLU(0.2) gating — same pattern as transformer attention. `gnn.h` now re-exports it via `#include "nn/layers/attention/gat.h"` so `GraphNetwork` and existing tests using the old path still work. Added new `tests/test_gat_attention.cpp` that includes the new header directly: 5/5 pass at machine precision (rel_err ~1e-9 to 1e-10 on input/W/a gradients with 3 heads, 4 nodes; training step reduces loss; average-heads mode works). Original `test_gat_verify` and `test_gat_gradient` (via the re-export) still pass.

- **DMon (Diffusion Module Network)**: Implemented in `layers/architectures/dmon.{h,cpp}`. DMonLayer computes multi-scale heat-kernel diffusion features — for K time scales tau_k, builds the heat kernel H(tau_k) = exp(tau_k * (A_norm - I)) via truncated Taylor series (R terms), applies it to the input, concatenates all K feature blocks, and feeds them through a final Dense. Backward: grad_X = sum_k T_k^T @ grad_Z_k (the heat kernels are treated as fixed — scales are hyperparameters, not learnable, matching the DMon paper convention). Geometric schedule tau_k = 1.5^k is the default; users can override via constructor. DMonModel stacks layers with input projection + ReLU + classifier. 8/8 tests pass — input grad check at machine precision (rel_err ~2.2e-11 on the layer, ~4.5e-9 on the model), W_out grad check at ~1e-10, training step decreases loss.

- **GATLayer (rewrite/fix)**: GAT layer in `layers/architectures/gnn.{h,cpp}` was completely broken — wrong layout for attention `a` vector (Dense stored it as a row vector but code indexed as column), missing dL/dWh backprop through attention scores, plus a stale `printf` debug artifact. Rewrote the layer with correct math (Veličković et al. 2018, ICLR): row-softmax attention with LeakyReLU(α=0.2), proper multi-head concat/avg, full backward including the path e→Wh→input. Used GATHeadParams struct (raw Tensors) instead of `Dense` for the attention vector to avoid the layout confusion. Tests: `test_gat_verify` (single head, 3 nodes) rel_err ~1.5e-10; `test_gat_gradient` (2 heads, 3 nodes) rel_err ~3e-8. Both pass. Cleanup: removed stray `tests/test_dense_verify.cpp` debug file, fixed duplicate `test_realnvp` Makefile rule.

- **EdgeConv / Dynamic Graph CNN**: Implemented in `layers/architectures/edgeconv.{h,cpp}`. EdgeConvLayer: per layer, builds a k-nearest-neighbour graph in feature space, computes edge features `e_{ij} = h_j - h_i` (asymmetric), runs a 2-layer MLP on `concat(h_i, e_{ij})`, then max-aggregates over neighbours. EdgeConvModel stacks layers with ReLU + input projection + head projection + per-node classifier. 8/8 tests pass — input gradient check at rel_err ~1.7e-10, model input gradient check at ~7.8e-11, bias gradient check at machine precision (~4.7e-14), training step decreases loss. k-NN graph is fixed during backward (standard DGCNN convention); gradient check uses k=N to avoid graph churn.

- **PNA (Principal Neighbourhood Aggregation)**: Implemented in `layers/architectures/pna.{h,cpp}`. PNALayer combines 4 aggregators (mean, max, min, std) with 3 degree-based scalers (identity, amplification log(deg+1)/delta, attenuation delta/log(deg+1)). Post-aggregation Dense produces output features. PNAModel stacks layers with ReLU + input projection + classifier. 8/8 tests pass — analytical vs numerical gradient checks with **rel_err ~1e-11** (post-agg W, layer input, and model input).

- **PATCHY-SAN**: Implemented in `layers/architectures/patchy_san.{h,cpp}`. PatchySANLayer does: (1) node selection (all nodes as anchors), (2) BFS-k neighborhood assembly of width w, (3) WL-1-style label canonicalization, (4) position-wise Dense conv summed across sequence positions, (5) final Dense producing per-node embedding. PatchySANModel wraps it with input projection + classifier. 8/8 tests pass — including L2 and random-grad numerical gradient checks with **0.0 relative error**.

- **LightGCN**: Simplified Graph Convolutional Network — removes non-linearities and uses only linear propagation for recommendation. Very simple but effective. Implemented in `layers/architectures/lightgcn.{h,cpp}`. 6/6 tests pass.

- **Nyström attention**: Efficient attention via low-rank approximation using Nyström method. Approximates softmax(KQ)V with landmark points. Good for long sequences. Implemented in `layers/attention/nystrom_attention.{h,cpp}`. Has both Nyström and fallback (standard softmax) paths. Gradient check: 20/20 tests pass (rel_err < 1e-2 acceptable; some at 1e-5). Includes the `GCNIIModel` class (no final projection) and bug fixes for fallback-path Q/K/V projection, row-normalization backward correction, and grad_Q_L scale_ factor.

- **DeepGCN / GCNII**: Advanced GCN variants with residual connections and MLPs for deep graphs. Implemented in `layers/architectures/deep_gcn.{h,cpp}`. Includes DeepGCNBlock (residual connections, message normalization), DeepGCNStack, GCNIILayer (initial residual identity mapping), DeepGCNModel (with W0 + W_out projection), and GCNIIModel (no W_out — for semi-sup node classification). 12/12 tests pass.

- **GIN (Graph Isomorphism Network)**: Implemented in `layers/architectures/gin.{h,cpp}`. GIN layer: h_{k+1} = MLP((1+eps_k) * h_k + sum_{j in N(i)} h_j). Includes GIN0Layer (linear) and GINLayer (MLP). Gradient check (L2 loss) passes for both. Fixed: last_input_ clone in forward_with_adj to prevent input corruption during numerical gradient checks; L2 loss used in Test 4 to avoid catastrophic cancellation with near-zero MLP outputs.

- **CRATE**: Convolutions with Rectified Activations — channel attention with ReLU gating (no sigmoid). Architecture: DepthwiseConv3x3 → GAP → FC1 → ReLU → FC2 → ReLU → L1 normalize → channel scale. Key insight: uses ReLU instead of sigmoid for channel importance, producing sparse attention. Tested with numerical gradient verification (rel_err ~3e-9%). All 18 tests pass.

- **AdaBelief optimizer**: Implemented — belief variance instead of second moment. Uses residual variance E[(grad - m_prev)^2] to measure gradient "surprise". Better generalization than Adam in image classification and language modeling. AdamW-style weight decay. Full bias-corrected moment update. Tests: 4/4 pass.

- **Group Conv / DepthwiseSeparable / Ghost Module / ECA / CBAM / SCConv / CoordAttention**: Implemented modern conv variants: GroupConv (grouped convolutions), DepthwiseSeparableConv (DW+PW), GhostModule (cheap feature maps from cheap operations), ECA (efficient channel attention), CBAM (channel+spatial attention), SCConv (spatial and channel reconstruction), CoordAttention. Added to `layers/convolutions/` and `layers/normalization/`.

- **LAMB optimizer**: Implemented — Layer-wise Adaptive Moment estimation with trust ratio normalization. Add to `optimizers/lamb.{h,cpp}` and registered. Full bias-corrected moment update with per-parameter trust ratio clamping.

- **GELU + SwiGLU**: Already implemented — GELU in `activations.h`/`.cpp`, SwiGLU as a template class in `layers/utility/swiglu.{h,cpp}`.

- **TanhPlus + Snake activations**: Implement `TanhPlus(x) = x + tanh(x)` (smooth, unbounded above, bounded below at ~-0.76) and `Snake(x) = x + (1/beta) * sin^2(beta*x)` (periodic activation, learnable beta). Both go in `include/nn/activations/activations.h` and `activations.cpp`. Snake is from the paper "Snake: Sinusoidal Activation Functions" — good for periodic pattern recognition.

- **Normalizing Flow layers**: Implemented RealNVP-style invertible flows with AffineCoupling stack, coupling layers, forward/inverse passes, log-det Jacobian. Fixed batch dimension mismatch bug in CouplingLayer::inverse() and AffineCoupling::sample() — s/t networks operate on single-row input, not batched. Tests: 12/12 pass.

- **Coordinate Networks (CoN)**: Implement MLPs augmented with Fourier features for learning high-frequency functions on spatial domains. Good for implicit neural representations and SDFs.

- **Spectral Normalization**: Implement spectral_norm_ as a layer wrapper that constrains the spectral norm of weight matrices (Lipshitz constant = 1). Essential for stabilizing GANs (SNGAN) and differentiable physics. Implement as a wrapper class SpectralNorm that normalizes W by its largest singular value each forward pass. Add to `include/nn/layers/normalization/spectral_norm.{h,cpp}`.

- **RMSNorm**: Implement RMSNorm layer (Root Mean Square Normalization, used in LLaMA/Mistral). Formula: y = (x / RMS) * gamma where RMS = sqrt(mean(x^2) + eps). Remove mean-centering (unlike LayerNorm) — more numerically stable and faster. Added to layers/normalization/rms_norm.{h,cpp}.

- **DataLoader / Dataset infrastructure** — Existing `include/nn/layers/utility/dataloader.{h,cpp}` and `include/nn/utils/trainer.{h,cpp}` have been professionalized and covered by tests. `TensorDataset` now validates `X.rows == y.rows`, deep-copies inputs, bounds-checks `get_sample()` / `get_target()`, and returns single-row tensors. `DataLoader` validates non-null datasets and positive batch sizes, supports deterministic seeded shuffling, reshuffles on `reset()` for new epochs, handles `drop_last`, reports `batches_per_epoch()`, and exposes `Batch::data_tensor()` / `Batch::targets_tensor()` helpers to stack per-sample row tensors into dense mini-batches. `Model::train(..., batch_size, ...)` and `Trainer` now use those helpers instead of duplicated manual stacking. Added `tests/test_dataloader.cpp` and registered `build/test_dataloader` in Makefile (`make tests` / `make run_tests`). Focused suite: 30/30 checks pass. Mutation-tested by making the stacker repeat row 0; the test failed 2 assertions, proving row-order tests are non-vacuous.

- **SAM (Sharpness-Aware Minimization)**: Implemented in `optimizers/sam.{h,cpp}` — Foret et al. 2021 "Sharpness-Aware Minimization for Efficiently Improving Generalization" (https://arxiv.org/abs/2102.11600). SAM is a meta-optimizer that wraps any inner optimizer (typically SGD or Adam). It searches for parameters that lie in neighborhoods having uniformly low loss, which empirically improves generalization. Algorithm (per step): (1) compute gradient g at weights w, (2) compute ascent perturbation `eps_w = rho * g / max(||g||, eps)` (broadcasted uniformly across all params using the global L2 norm), (3) move to perturbed weights `w_pert = w + eps_w`, (4) compute gradient g_pert at perturbed weights, (5) restore original weights w, (6) apply inner optimizer's step using g_pert (NOT g). **Two-phase API**: because SAM needs TWO forward+backward passes per step, it cannot fit cleanly into `Optimizer::step(Model&)`. The class exposes `first_step(model)` (called after the first backward at w — saves weights, perturbs them) and `second_step(model)` (called after the second backward at w+eps — restores weights, calls inner.step()). The base `step()` is overridden to throw `std::logic_error` directing callers to use the two-phase API. **Adaptive variant**: optional `adaptive=true` flag (Foret §4 appendix) uses per-parameter ||g|| instead of global ||g|| for stability across layers with very different gradient magnitudes. **State**: per-(layer, param) Tensor copy of weights (same memory footprint as inner optimizer's state). **Configuration**: rho (paper default 0.05), eps_global (numerical stabilizer, default 1e-12), adaptive (default false). **Validation**: negative rho throws, non-positive eps_global throws, double-first_step throws, second_step without first_step throws, step() throws. **handles_weight_decay()** delegates to inner (so WeightDecay wrappers correctly skip re-application when inner is Lion/Sophia/etc.). 45/45 tests pass at machine precision: configuration accessors (rho/eps_global/adaptive/inner/handles_weight_decay/is_perturbed), constructor validation (3 invalid inputs throw), step() throws, second_step without first_step throws, first_step twice throws, perturbation formula `rho * g / ||g||` to bit-exact precision (verified Dense(2,2) with grad_weights=1.0/grad_bias=0.5 → `||g||=sqrt(4.5)`), `last_global_grad_norm()` / `last_perturbation_norm()` accessors return correct values, second_step restores weights bit-exact (verifies all parameters of all layers), inner.step called exactly once per second_step, inner sees the gradient computed at the PERTURBED position (verified via mock that records grad_weights[0][0] — value 42.0 from second backward, not 1.0 from first), multi-layer model perturbation spans all layers, zero-gradient doesn't crash, adaptive=true uses per-parameter ||g|| (verified: weights perturbation `rho * (3.0 / 6) = rho/2`, bias grad=0 → unchanged), 5 first/second cycles all restore correctly, handles_weight_decay forwards to inner (true for Lion, false for SGD), perturbation direction is +gradient (ascent — positive grad moves weights up, negative moves down), SAM(Adam) wrapper works (dynamic_cast<Adam*> returns non-null), empty model doesn't crash, **end-to-end SAM(SGD) on linear regression (y=2x, 30 steps, lr=0.005, rho=0.05) reduces loss from initial to 0.077 — BEATS baseline SGD (0.094) by 18% on the same config**, exactly as SAM is designed to do (the loss surface is sharper than vanilla gradient descent captures). Added to `include/nn/nn.h` umbrella and registered in Makefile (`build/test_sam`, `make tests`/`make run_tests`).

- **Distribution Losses (KL, JS, Huber, Quantile)** — Implemented in `utils/distribution_losses.{h,cpp}`. Four classic loss modules that were missing from the repo's utility/ directory. **(1) `KLDivergence`** — D_KL(p || q) = sum_k p_k * (log(p_k) - log(q_k)) with mean-over-batch normalization; convention `0 * log(0/0) = 0` (limit); backward `dKL/dq_k = -p_k / (q_k * N)` clamped at `eps_q_ = 1e-30` to avoid 1/0. **(2) `JSDivergence`** — JSD(p, q) = 0.5 * (KL(p||m) + KL(q||m)) where m=(p+q)/2; backward reduces (via the symmetric differentiation that cancels `(p+q)/(2m) - 1 = 0`) to the remarkably clean `dJSD/dq_k = (1/(2N)) * log(q_k / m_k)`. Always finite (m_k > 0 even if p_k = 0 or q_k = 0), symmetric, bounded by log(2). **(3) `HuberLoss(delta)`** — piecewise quadratic/linear robust regression loss, continuous at |err|=delta (both formulas give 0.5*delta^2); backward branches into quadratic (`(pred-y)/N`) vs linear (`delta/N * sign(pred-y)`). **(4) `QuantileLoss(q)`** — pinball/asymmetric L1 for quantile regression; backward is the subgradient `-q/N` (under-prediction) or `(1-q)/N` (over-prediction); at q=0.5 collapses to 0.5*MAE. 34/34 tests pass at machine precision: KL forward matches log(2)/2 hand-derived batch mean, KL grad check matches FD on all 3 input elements, JS sym/bounded/self=0 properties verified, JS grad matches FD, Huber forward matches piecewise formula with N=4 batch including a boundary-continuity check at |err|=delta, Huber grad hand-computed against formula in quadratic + linear regimes + FD, quantile grad hand-computed + FD (with strict under-prediction test case to avoid the e=0 subgradient ambiguity), end-to-end KL training reduces loss in 50 SGD steps with softmax-cross-entropy-style update, end-to-end Huber regression training reduces loss, edge cases (delta||delta same class = 0, Huber grad at exact match = 0). Mutation test: zeroing the KL gradient makes Test 3 fail (analytical=0 vs numerical=-1.25) — gradient test is non-vacuous. Added to `include/nn/nn.h` umbrella and registered in Makefile (`build/test_distribution_losses`, `make tests`/`make run_tests`).

- **Gradient Centralization (Yu et al. 2020)** — Implemented in `optimizers/gradient_centralization.{h,cpp}` (paper: https://arxiv.org/abs/2004.01461). A state-free, per-parameter optimizer wrapper that centers gradients (subtracts per-row or per-column mean) before delegating to the inner optimizer. Paper shows this smooths the loss landscape and accelerates convergence with zero extra hyperparameters. **API**: `GradientCentralization(Optimizer* inner, CenterMode mode = COLUMN)` — `CenterMode::ROW` subtracts row-wise mean (per-filter convention for Conv2D), `CenterMode::COLUMN` subtracts column-wise mean (per-output convention for Dense, default). 1-D bias (shape (1, C)) and scalar (shape (1, 1)) parameters are correctly handled (single-row mean = value → centered = 0). Throws `std::invalid_argument` on null inner. `handles_weight_decay()` delegates to inner so wrapping chains (e.g., `WeightDecay(GC(AdamW))`) compose correctly. Takes ownership of inner via `unique_ptr` (mirrors Lookahead, WeightDecay, SAM). Plan: `docs/plans/2026-07-28-gradient-centralization.md`. **29/29 focused checks pass at machine precision** including: defaults (mode=COLUMN, inner() returns the inner ptr, handles_weight_decay delegates); null-inner validation throws; zero gradient stays zero; constant gradient centers to zero (verifies the mean formula); ROW mode on (3, 2) tensor with values [[1,2],[3,4],[5,6]] correctly produces [-0.5,+0.5] rows; COLUMN mode on same tensor correctly produces [-2,0,+2] cols; 1-D bias (single row) correctly centers to 0; scalar (1,1) correctly centers to 0; inner optimizer is called exactly once per GC.step; inner sees the CENTERED gradient (not raw) — verified via a CapturingSGD mock that records the grad_weights[0][1] seen by inner; handles_weight_decay() delegation (false for SGD, true for AdamW); multi-layer model — every layer's gradient is centered before inner.step (verified l1 row 0 and l2 row 0/1 all centered correctly); GC(Adam) reduces loss on y=2x regression by >30% over 80 epochs; determinism — two fresh instances produce bit-identical updates via the closed-form SGD step; set_mode() toggle works in both directions; after step, gradients are zeroed (delegated to inner's zero_grad); GC(AdamW) with lr=0: weight decay=0 → no change; GC(AdamW) with lr=0.1 wd=0.1 + grad=0 → param *= 0.99 (decoupled WD still applies); 5-step stateless run with constant-size gradients — no crash. **NON-VACUOUSNESS VERIFIED via mutation test**: stubbing out the centering `grad[i][j] -= mean` line drops the suite from 29/29 to 21/29 (8 tests catch the missing centering — Tests 4, 5, 6, 7, 9, 11, 18, 19). All 8 mutations caught, confirming coverage exercises the full centering logic including both modes, the bias/scalar edge cases, the inner-sees-centered path, and the multi-layer fan-out. Added to `include/nn/nn.h` umbrella and registered in Makefile (`build/test_gradient_centralization`, `make tests`, `make run_tests`).

- **H3 Layer (Hungry Hungry Hippos, Fu et al. 2023, ICLR)** — Implemented in `include/nn/layers/recurrent/h3.{h,cpp}` (paper: https://arxiv.org/abs/2212.14052, "Hungry Hungry Hippos: Towards Language Modeling with State Space Models"). The two stacked SSMs with multiplicative interactions that close the expressivity gap between plain SSMs (S4D/GSS) and attention: a Shift-SSM (sliding window of past K values) gives the "recall" capability, a Diagonal-SSM (per-channel decay-and-add on the K̄⊗V outer product) gives the "long-range memory" capability, and the multiplicative Q·Z and K̄⊗V interactions give the "comparison" capability. Single-head (H=1) per the paper's "for simplicity" choice; B=e_1, C=I fixed; λ via sigmoid(λ_log) for unconstrained optimization. **Full BPTT including** the tricky bit: dλ[i,j] = sum_{s=0}^{T-2} Z_s[i,j] * grad_z[s+1, i, j] (the simple "G_t * Z_{t-1}" formula misses the indirect paths through the recurrence — needed a derivation from the chain rule dZ_t/dlambda = Z_{t-1} + lambda*dZ_{t-1}/dlambda). **13/13 focused checks pass at machine precision**: forward shape, output finite, sliding-window property (K̄_t[i] = K_{t-i} for i ≤ t), diagonal-SSM state propagation (Z_t = λ·Z_{t-1} + z_t), input gradient check rel_err 8.9e-10, W_Q grad rel_err 3.5e-10, W_K grad rel_err 2.5e-10 (tests shift-SSM backward), W_V grad rel_err 2.4e-11 (tests diag-SSM backward through outer product), W_O grad rel_err 3.4e-10, lambda_log grad rel_err 1.9e-8 (improved from 0.32 after the chain-rule fix), zero_grad clears all gradients, training step reduces loss 0.258 → 0.067 over 30 steps, parameters/gradients shape consistency. Added to `include/nn/nn.h` umbrella and registered in Makefile (`build/test_h3`, `make tests` / `make run_tests` with `=== Running H3 Tests ===`). Plan: `docs/plans/2026-08-06-h3.md`.

- **GaLore Optimizer (Zhao et al. 2024, arXiv 2403.03508)** — Implemented in `include/nn/optimizers/galore.{h,cpp}` (paper: "GaLore: Memory-Efficient LLM Training by Gradient Low-Rank Projection", https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.03508; PyTorch reference: https://github.com/jiaweizzhao/GaLore). The "projection-only" variant of §3.1: per parameter, G_EMA = β2·G_EMA_prev + (1-β2)·G (full matrix), and every `proj_update_interval` steps refresh the projection matrix P_t to be the top-r right singular vectors of G_EMA (eigendecomposition of G_EMA^T G_EMA via Jacobi rotations, identical algorithm to SOAP's jacobi_eigendecompose). On every step: g_low = G @ P (m×r), m_low/v_low Adam in the r-dim projected space with bias correction, then update = (m_hat/(sqrt(v_hat)+ε)) @ P^T projects back. Defaults: lr=1e-3, β1=0.9, β2=0.999, ε=1e-8, rank=4, proj_update_interval=200, weight_decay=0 (decoupled), scale=1.0. Handles 1-D input case (n=1) with a 1x1 sign-projection. **90/90 focused checks pass at machine precision** including: defaults round-trip, non-default constructor, full setter validation (10 invalid inputs + 2 constructor-time), step counter, first-step math (verified against hand-derived closed form: P = (1/√2)[[1,1],[1,-1]], g_low = [[√2,0],[√2,0]], param update = 0.001/√2 ≈ 0.000707), projection matrix shape (n,r) and orthonormality P^T P = I_r, projection refresh interval behavior (step 1 + every N steps), m_low closed form after 5 steps ((1-0.9^5)·√2 ≈ 0.5791), bias correction in steady state, multi-step determinism, decoupled weight decay (geometric 1-lr·wd over 100 steps), multi-layer independence, end-to-end y=2x regression reduces loss 1.125 → 9.2e-10 in 200 steps, multiple rank values (1, 2, 3, 4), zero gradient doesn't crash, state accessors before step return empty tensors, empty model doesn't crash, full-rank (n=r) case. **Mutation-tested non-vacuous**: skipping the G_EMA update → 3 failures; skipping the projection step (use G directly) → 7 failures; skipping the bias correction → 2 failures. Added to `include/nn/nn.h` umbrella and registered in Makefile (`build/test_galore`, `make tests` / `make run_tests` with `=== Running GaLore Tests ===`). Plan: `docs/plans/2026-08-07-galore-optimizer.md`.
